Files changed: `Sources/MacAgentCore/AutomationStores.swift`, `TavilySearchProvider.swift` (new), `AgentActionExecutor.swift`, `InstantCommandResolver.swift`, `WebResearchMarkdownCapabilityAdapter.swift`, `WebResearchService.swift`; `Sources/MacAgent/AgentViewModel.swift`, `AppDelegate.swift`, `RoutineDetailView.swift`, `CommandCenterView.swift`, `FloatingWidgetView.swift`; tests `AutomationStoresTests.swift`, `TavilySearchProviderTests.swift` (new), `AgentActionExecutorTests.swift`, `QuickDispatchTests.swift`, `ScheduledRoutineRunTests.swift`, `ProductShellTests.swift`; docs this file, `sonny-manual-test-checklist.md`.
Tests: `env CLANG_MODULE_CACHE_PATH="$PWD/.build/clang-module-cache" swift test --disable-sandbox -Xswiftc -F ...` (full flags per CLAUDE.md) -> pass, 414 tests in 43 suites (from 385 at branch start — the 373 recorded in branch 10's entry above was stale, corrected 2026-07-30).

The manual pass produced seven fixes, folded into this branch per the fix-in-branch rule: confirmation-dialog bodies use pronouns instead of repeating the quoted name the title already carries; the search summary's hardcoded "sources" now pluralizes (newly visible because partial synthesis can reduce the surviving count to one — pinned by `webResearchSearchSummaryUsesSingularSourceWhenOnlyOneSourceSurvives`); the JavaScript-rendered-page finding below; the missing-main-menu finding below, which broke ⌘A/⌘C/⌘V/⌘X/⌘Z in every text field app-wide; the missing-target clarification now cross-references the *other* store on an exact (store-normalized) name match — "run hehe" with a workspace named hehe saved used to list routine names while ignoring it, and now asks "did you mean to open that?" instead, in both directions, with no fuzzy matching, the same-kind list kept as the fallback, and an unreadable other store degrading to that list rather than failing (four tests pin all of this in `AgentActionExecutorTests`); the widget composer pill's trailing inset now matches the Start button's 8pt vertical inset — only while the button is rendered, so the in-flight state keeps its shipped 14pt look — a view-layer change the suite cannot cover, verified visually by the user like the rest of the widget; and seventh, `InstantCommandResolver.quickDispatchResolution` now cross-references the other store when a kind-locked verb's name misses its own — the root cause being kind-locked verb prefixes ("run" only ever looked its name up as a routine, "open" only as a workspace, while "start"/"launch" sit in both lists and already resolved cross-kind), so "run hehe" with only a workspace named hehe fell through to the planner, which structurally cannot name saved items and asked a generic question that never mentioned the item that exists; the fix lives in the resolver because it is the only layer that knows saved names on this path (the executor's cross-kind clarification from fix five sits behind the planner, which the branch-10 tool-description nudge steers away from emitting plans naming missing targets, so it never fires for this input shape — it stays, still reachable via the scheduler delete race), and it clarifies with the executor's exact cross-kind wording rather than silently cross-resolving, consistent with the collision handling's defer-rather-than-auto-run decision — exact normalized-name match only, explicit kind-prefixed forms covered by the same check, three-way ambiguity with an allowlisted app stepping aside to the planner, and names saved nowhere still reaching the planner; seven `QuickDispatchTests` pin all of it, with the four clarify cases mutation-checked (cross-kind block reverted, all four fail).

Behavior added:
- A saved routine can be deleted from its detail view; a saved workspace from a labeled danger-tone Delete button on its card. Both get a confirmation dialog and both are refused while a task is in flight.
- `RoutineStore.delete(routineNamed:)` / `WorkspaceStore.delete(workspaceNamed:)` — single synchronous load-mutate-write, throwing the existing missing-item errors rather than no-oping.
- Web search works for real: `TavilySearchProvider` behind the branch-3 `WebSearchProviding` seam, key from `TAVILY_API_KEY` (environment-only, never persisted). Absent key degrades to the existing "Web search provider not configured." error — no new UI, no new error path.

Behavior preserved (required, no blanket claims):
- Scheduled unattended runs: the full `ScheduledRoutineRunTests` suite passes, including trust gating, catch-up, tier-3+ backstop, and history tagging — plus the new delete-race case.
- Manual routine runs from the detail view, schedule authoring/removal, and the unattended-trust toggle — untouched by the new delete control in the same panel.
- Workspace Open and the one-time "Mark as team" control — untouched; `WorkspaceCard` only gained the delete affordance.
- The web-research adapter and its whole safety posture — zero changes to `WebResearchMarkdownCapabilityAdapter`, `WebResearchService`, `SafeURL`, or the synthesizer; `StaticWebSearchProvider`-backed adapter tests unchanged and passing.
- `deleteLocalData` still guards only `isRunning` — observed, deliberately not touched (see below).

Architectural decisions / pitfalls discovered:

**Delete affordances are a stated wireframe exception; the asymmetry between the two surfaces is deliberate.** Neither `11-MainAppRoutines.svg` nor `13-MainAppWorkspaces.svg` contains any delete/trash/menu affordance (verified against the raw SVGs — every grep hit is sidebar nav), while spec §6.8 mandates the feature, so the affordances are designed, not matched — same class of exception as branch 10's schedule authoring. A routine deletes from its detail view; a workspace deletes from its card, because *the affordance lives where the content lives*: a routine's steps and schedule sit behind a detail view, a workspace's whole content is already visible on its card, and building a `WorkspaceDetailView` solely to host a delete button would invent a surface to solve a placement problem. Both deletes get the "Delete Local Data" confirmation treatment — unlike "Remove schedule", which deliberately has none, because a removed schedule is recoverable by making another and deleted steps/run history are not. The routine sheet must `dismiss()` explicitly after deleting: its `live` lookup silently falls back to the stale presentation snapshot when the routine disappears, and nothing auto-closes the sheet.

**Deletion is UI-only — no planner-invokable capability, and that is a decision, not an omission.** A new `AgentOperation` case would be exposed to the planner schema unless deliberately excluded, would need adding to `validateRoutineSteps`' reject list, its own tier-3 gating, and name-collision handling in the instant resolver. "Delete my morning routine" by voice is a defensible follow-up feature with that full blast-radius analysis, not a rider on this branch.

**The in-flight guard is `isRunning || isAwaitingApproval`, wider than `deleteLocalData`'s.** A run parked at an approval still holds a prepared plan that re-reads the store when approved, so deleting under it is the same hazard as deleting mid-run — and the broader condition is what `checkScheduledRoutines` and every running-indicator gate already treat as "in flight". `deleteLocalData`'s `isRunning`-only guard predates that convention; it was observed and left alone rather than silently widened, since changing it was outside this branch's checkpoints. Error copy reads "Finish or stop the current task…" so it stays accurate for the parked-at-approval case.

**The scheduler's "unreachable" claims were load-bearing analysis and adding delete invalidated them.** `checkScheduledRoutines` reads the routine, then spawns `performScheduledRun` as a `Task` — a real suspension point before the run re-resolves the routine by name — so a delete inside that window now genuinely reaches the missing-routine clarification path, which fails closed (notice, nothing recorded, nothing left in flight). Both comments were rewritten before shipping the button, and the race is pinned deterministically: the test deletes synchronously after `checkScheduledRoutines` returns, which on the main actor is guaranteed to land before the spawned task body's first line. A delete landing later, between prepare and execute, fails closed separately through the generic catch.

**Tavily decisions.** Constructed per `makeExecutor()` with `try?` — the only throw is a missing key, and nil falls back to `UnavailableWebSearchProvider`, so absence degrades into the already-tested error instead of a new failure surface, and a key exported after launch works on the next run. `max_results` clamps to 1...20 (Tavily's documented range, verified against their live API docs 2026-07-30, floor 1 because 0 is a paid request for nothing). The provider drops response garbage (URLs that don't parse as http(s)) but does **no** `SafeURL` policy validation — that stays single-authority in the adapter, where a private-address result URL must keep failing loudly rather than being quietly filtered. Search results contribute only their URLs downstream; titles/snippets never reach the synthesizer or the output Markdown (pinned by the existing no-`sources`-field schema tests).

**The app shipped with no main menu at all — never deliberate, just unnoticed, and it silently broke every menu-routed key equivalent.** Found during this branch's manual pass: ⌘A and ⌘V did nothing in the floating widget's command field. `main.swift` is a bare AppKit lifecycle (`NSApplication.shared` + delegate + `run()`) with no SwiftUI `App`/`Scene` to synthesize a menu, and the only `NSMenu` anywhere was `makeStatusMenu()`'s status-item dropdown — macOS dispatches the standard editing shortcuts by matching key equivalents against main-menu items, so with no `NSApp.mainMenu` they never reached the first responder's `selectAll:`/`paste:`/etc. at all, in *any* text field (the Command Center clarification field had the identical failure structurally — same app, same absent menu, no field-specific key handling anywhere). Typing worked because that is direct key input. App-wide ⌘Q was equally broken: the status menu's own "q" equivalent only dispatches while that dropdown is open. Fixed by installing a real `NSApp.mainMenu` in `AppDelegate` — an Edit menu (Undo/Redo/Cut/Copy/Paste/Select All, nil targets so they route through the responder chain) plus an app menu carrying only Quit, which both fixes ⌘Q and keeps the bold app-menu slot correctly filled whenever the bar is visible under `.regular` policy. Installed under `.accessory` too: menu visibility and key-equivalent routing are separate. Deliberately not added: File/View/Window/Help. **Verification is honest about its limits: key-equivalent dispatch is AppKit-level and this project has no UI test harness, so no automated test covers it** — a menu-exists assertion would be theater, not coverage; the manual checklist rows added under "Menu bar & launch" are the real verification.

**Pitfall: a shared `URLProtocol` handler static races under Swift Testing's parallel execution.** `TavilySearchProviderTests` installs a different handler per test on one `nonisolated(unsafe) static var`; run in parallel, one test's teardown nils the handler out from under another — three tests failed exactly that way on the first run. Fixed with `@Suite(.serialized)` and a comment. `WebResearchSynthesizerTests` never hit this only because its tests never reset the handler.

Known limitations / deferred scope:
- No natural-language delete path (planner-invokable capability) — see the decision above.
- Tavily is absent from the permission-readiness panel (`PermissionReadinessCapabilityAdapter` reports the OpenAI key only) — deliberately deferred, not overlooked.
- Per-search cost is not metered; the adapter's existing "Searching web for ..." log line is the only per-call visibility. Metering is branch 13's territory per its credit-weighting note above (web research = 3 credits partly because of Tavily's ~$0.008/search).
- `StoredWorkspace` remains non-`Identifiable` and there is still no workspace detail view.
- `deleteLocalData`'s narrower `isRunning`-only guard is a known inconsistency left in place — whoever next touches that method should widen it to match, with this entry as context.
- **JavaScript-rendered pages cannot be summarized, and that is extraction working correctly, not a bug** (investigated 2026-07-30 during this branch's manual pass, via `summarize ycombinator.com/companies`): `SwiftSoupReadableWebExtractor` reads the static HTML the server sends, and a client-rendered directory page has no article-shaped content in it — not a fetch, URL-validation, or search-provider failure. Resolved by hinting in the `.noReadableContent` error copy ("may render its content with JavaScript") rather than leaving the bare not-found message, since that is the dominant real-world cause for a page that looks fine in a browser; a headless-rendering fallback would be a real feature with its own cost/security surface, deliberately not scoped here.
- **Two manual-pass findings deferred by explicit user decision (2026-08-01), a stated exception to the fix-in-branch rule with a named landing spot — the immediately-next branch, not a backlog.** (1) The menu bar's "New Task" item is a no-op: the real fix needs an actual widget-summon/focus trigger the app currently has no mechanism for, its own small design rather than a patch. (2) Workspace URLs open in the system default browser, not the workspace's own browser app (`WorkspaceBrowserOpener` calls `NSWorkspace.shared.open(url)` with no target app, so a Safari workspace opens its URLs in Chrome) — found by co-founder review during this branch's pass. Both land in the next branch alongside the cheap half of the approval-friction work.

Open questions for the next chat (required, write "none" if true): none

Next branch: the small-bugs branch agreed 2026-08-01 ("New Task" widget-focus trigger, per-workspace browser targeting, first-run approval legibility) — superseding branch 10's `feature/instant-utility-quick-results` pointer. The full upcoming-branch resequencing agreed the same day (small bugs → workspace-as-restriction-scope → structural approval relaxation → task-history controls → task-detail rehydration, all ahead of branch 12) will be written into the roadmap table together with the workflow-v2 transition note, not piecemeal here.

### Branch: feature/sonny-8-widget-composer-focus
Status: complete
Date: 2026-08-03
Tickets: SONNY-8 — the status menu's "New Task" item (and the push-to-talk hotkey) now bring the floating widget forward *and* put the cursor in its composer, by routing through the shared `widgetPresentationRequest` counter instead of calling `FloatingWidgetWindowController.show()` directly.
Reviewed by: fresh session (per WORKFLOW.md step 7) on PR #20 — two cycles, their record living wholly on SONNY-8's comments rather than the PR thread (backfilled here 2026-08-07 under SONNY-61). Cycle 1 at `c5aa693`: verdict "the fix is correct and the branch is mergeable," five findings — F1, this entry's false claim that the title-order assertion pinned "Open Sonny" and "Quit Sonny"'s targets and selectors, disproven by mutation, the one in-scope fix; F2, the untested Combine sink now load-bearing for four hand-driven entry points instead of two, accepted by the user as a triage note and routed to SONNY-25; F3, zero automated coverage on the focus hop itself, matching the ticket's own scoping; F4, a host-dependent test side effect nothing asserts, no action; F5, a pre-existing false dated confirmation in the manual-test checklist, filed as SONNY-26. Cycle 2 at `f08c825` (per-item target/action/keyEquivalent assertions; suite green, 416 tests in 43 suites): "Approved from the reviewer's side," the user's manual pass the only remaining gate; the implementing session's own pre-PR adversarial pass (five independent lenses over the diff — runtime behavior, Swift 6/AppKit semantics, test quality, ticket-scope compliance, surrounding-regression — each finding then put to three refutation votes) produced two raw findings, both refuted 3-0 and both about the pre-existing untested Combine sink / test-assertion breadth rather than this diff. Findings recorded on SONNY-25 rather than discarded.

Spec sections covered: none — this is a manual-pass follow-up (roadmap row A / Plane module "A — manual-pass follow-ups"), not spec-driven work. §17.3's cockpit surfaces are unchanged in requirements.
Files changed:
- `Sources/MacAgent/AppDelegate.swift`
- `Sources/MacAgent/AgentViewModel.swift` (doc comments only — two comment blocks that named the old call path; zero behavioral lines)
- `Tests/MacAgentTests/ProductShellTests.swift`
- `docs/sonny-v1-implementation-changelog.md`

Tests: `env CLANG_MODULE_CACHE_PATH="$PWD/.build/clang-module-cache" swift test --disable-sandbox -Xswiftc -F -Xswiftc /Library/Developer/CommandLineTools/Library/Developer/Frameworks -Xlinker -rpath -Xlinker /Library/Developer/CommandLineTools/Library/Developer/Frameworks -Xlinker -rpath -Xlinker /Library/Developer/CommandLineTools/Library/Developer/usr/lib` → exit 0, 416 tests in 43 suites passing (2 new). `swift build` → exit 0.

Behavior added:
- "New Task" in the status menu asks for the widget by bumping `AgentViewModel.widgetPresentationRequest` through the new `AppDelegate.requestWidgetPresentation()`, so the existing subscription fronts the panel *and* `FloatingWidgetView`'s existing `onChange` focuses the composer — expanding the compact capsule first when it had auto-collapsed.
- The push-to-talk hotkey's press/release closures became `handlePushToTalkPress()`/`handlePushToTalkRelease()`, and the press half calls the same `requestWidgetPresentation()` rather than its own `show()`. One path, not two.

Behavior preserved (required, no blanket claims):
- **Command Center row actions** (`runRoutineWidget`, `openWorkspaceWidget`) still bump `widgetPresentationRequest` directly at `CommandCenterView.swift:1870`/`:2049` — untouched, and their existing `ProductShellTests` cases (origin tagging, instant-resolve, the running-task block) still pass.
- **Push-to-talk recording** — the closure bodies moved verbatim into named methods with the same ordering (present first, then `beginPushToTalkVoice()`), so a hotkey run is still attributed `TaskOrigin.widget` and release still routes to `endPushToTalkVoice()`.
- **Launch presentation** — `applicationDidFinishLaunching` still calls `widgetController.show()` directly and `FloatingWidgetView.onAppear` still focuses the composer on first render, so launch focus is byte-for-byte the behavior it was.
- **The system notification's default action** still calls `show()` directly; deliberately unchanged (see below).
- **The rest of the status menu** — "Open Sonny" and "Quit Sonny" keep their targets and selectors, now pinned per item by target/selector/key-equivalent assertions (plus an exact title-order assertion). The original wording claimed the title-order assertion was the pin; a reviewer's decoy-selector mutation on PR #20 stayed green and proved it was not, since a rewired item keeps its title. The assertions were added rather than the claim softened — see the pitfall below.
- **`NSApp.mainMenu`/Edit-menu key-equivalent routing** and `main.swift`'s `AppDelegate()` construction are untouched; the delegate's new view-model parameter is defaulted, so the executable builds the same real `AgentViewModel` it always did.

Architectural decisions / pitfalls discovered:

**`show()` and "focus the composer" are two different halves, and only one of them lives in AppKit.** This is the whole bug. `FloatingWidgetWindowController.show()` does `orderFrontRegardless()` + `makeKey()` — it has no way to reach `pillFocused`, which is a `@FocusState` private to `FloatingWidgetView`. So calling `show()` on an already-visible panel is a no-op the user can see nothing of, which is exactly why "New Task" read as broken. The counter is the only thing both halves observe. Any future entry point that wants the widget must bump the counter; reaching for `widgetController.show()` re-creates this bug by construction.

**The Combine sink is synchronous, the SwiftUI `onChange` is not — and that ordering is load-bearing for push-to-talk.** `@Published` publishes in `willSet`, so `viewModel.widgetPresentationRequest += 1` runs `observeWidgetPresentationRequests()`'s sink (and therefore `show()`) inline, before the next statement. That preserves the hotkey's original "front the panel, then start recording" order exactly. `FloatingWidgetView`'s `onChange` lands later, on SwiftUI's next render pass — which is the right order anyway: focus set after the panel is key is what makes focus stick.

**Bumping the counter restarts the widget's auto-collapse timer; `show()` did not.** `onChange` calls `scheduleAutoDismissIfNeeded()`, so clicking "New Task" now gives the user a fresh 6s instead of inheriting whatever remained of the previous timer — under the old path the widget could collapse a second or two after the user summoned it. Deliberate and desirable, but it *is* a behavioral difference beyond focus, and it means a visible `.result`/`.failure` also gets its clearing deferred by up to 6s when the widget is summoned. Same treatment Command Center's row actions already got.

**Two direct `show()` callers were audited and left alone on purpose, not missed.** Launch (`applicationDidFinishLaunching`) is correct without the counter because `onAppear` focuses the composer on first render, and routing it through the counter would add an ordering dependency on the subscription being installed first for no user-visible gain. The system notification's default action (`SonnyNotificationService`'s `onOpen`) is a product question rather than a wiring bug: the approval case self-expands (`.permission` is not collapsible, so `scheduleAutoDismissIfNeeded` forces `isCompact = false`) and the user came to press a button, not type. Full analysis is on **SONNY-25** so nobody re-derives it.

**Testing AppKit glue: inject the view model, do not drive `applicationDidFinishLaunching`.** `AppDelegate` gained a defaulted `init(viewModel:)` and an internal `makeStatusMenu()` so a test can build the real menu, assert its target/selector, and dispatch through `perform(action)` exactly as AppKit would. This works only because every AppKit-owning collaborator on the delegate is `lazy` — an unlaunched delegate registers no status item, no Carbon hotkey, no routine-scheduling timer, and no subscriptions. Launching it in a test would do all four for real. The consequence is a deliberate boundary: tests stop at the view model, and the `@FocusState` hop stays a manual-test item.

**Making a hotkey test safe without a microphone: set `isRunning`, not an env var.** `beginPushToTalkVoice()` gates on `canUseVoice`, which reads `OPENAI_API_KEY` out of the process environment — uncontrollable from a test, so a developer with the key exported would have had the test open the mic. `canUseVoice` also requires `!isRunning`, which a test *can* set, making the guard deterministic on every host. It also pins the more interesting assertion: the widget is still asked to come forward when voice refuses to start, because that request is what puts the resulting error on screen.

**Both new tests were mutation-checked, not just observed passing.** Emptying `requestWidgetPresentation()`'s body made all five counter assertions fail; the original body restored them. A test that passes against a deliberately broken implementation is the failure mode this project cares about, and asserting it is cheap.

**Pitfall the first mutation pass missed: a shape assertion is not a wiring assertion, and the changelog claimed otherwise.** The original menu test asserted `menu.items.map(\.title)` against an exact array and this entry described that as pinning the other two items' "targets and selectors." It did not — rewiring "Quit Sonny" to a different `@objc` selector leaves every title identical, so the assertion stays green, which a reviewer's decoy-selector mutation on PR #20 demonstrated directly. The lesson generalizes past this file: mutation-checking only the behavior a ticket set out to change leaves every *incidentally* asserted thing unverified, and a confident sentence in a changelog is exactly how that gap survives review. Fixed by asserting target, selector, and key equivalent per item (⌘Q included — app-wide Quit was menu-routed and silently broken once already), and re-running the same decoy mutation to confirm it now fails. `openCommandCenter()`/`quit()` became internal for the same reason `requestWidgetPresentation()` is: a `private` `@objc` method cannot be named in `#selector` from the test target even under `@testable`.

Known limitations / deferred scope:
- The sink that turns a bump into the actual `show()` call is still exercised by no test, before or after this branch — an accepted structural limit (it needs `applicationDidFinishLaunching`), recorded on SONNY-25 rather than left to be rediscovered.
- SONNY-25 (Backlog, untriaged, unattached) holds the remaining-direct-callers question. Nothing was deferred out of this ticket's own acceptance criteria.
- Roadmap row A is only partly delivered: SONNY-9 (per-workspace browser targeting) and SONNY-10 (approval-friction audit) are still outstanding on their own branches.

Open questions (required, write "none" if true): none

Next branch: the rest of Plane module "A — manual-pass follow-ups" — `feature/sonny-9-workspace-browser` (running in parallel with this one; expected-touched-areas verified disjoint at ticket-creation time, `Sources/MacAgent*` here vs `Sources/MacAgentCore` there) and `feature/sonny-10-approval-friction-audit`. Merge one branch at a time and rebase the other worktree, per WORKFLOW.md step 3.

Files changed: `Sources/MacAgentCore/WorkspaceBrowserOpener.swift` (new), `HackerNewsService.swift` (removals only), `OpenWorkspaceCapabilityAdapter.swift`; tests `Tests/MacAgentCoreTests/WorkspaceBrowserOpenerTests.swift` (new), `AgentActionExecutorTests.swift`, `AgentRunnerTests.swift`, `QuickDispatchTests.swift`, `ProcessSeamTests.swift`; docs this file.
Tests: `env CLANG_MODULE_CACHE_PATH="$PWD/.build/clang-module-cache" swift test --disable-sandbox -Xswiftc -F ...` (full flags per CLAUDE.md) -> pass, **430 tests in 44 suites**, exit 0 — measured after the rebase onto `main` with SONNY-8 merged, which is the number that matters for this branch. The baseline is the 416 tests in 43 suites SONNY-8's entry records for `main`; the delta is exactly this branch's 14 new tests and its one new suite, which corroborates that figure without this session having re-measured `main` itself. (Pre-rebase, off the older base, the same suite ran 428 in 44 from 414 in 43 — superseded by the numbers above, and noted only because the commit message still quotes them.)

Behavior added:
- A workspace that lists a browser among its apps opens its URLs in *that* browser. The original repro — Chrome as the system default, a workspace holding Safari plus a URL, the URL landing in Chrome — no longer happens.
- "The workspace's browser" is the first browser-capable app in the workspace's own saved apps list, so save order decides. No schema change: nothing new is persisted, the browser is derived at run time from `StoredWorkspace.apps`.
- A named browser that is not installed, or that refuses to launch, falls back to the system default browser and logs why. This is never surfaced as a user-facing error — opening in the wrong browser beats failing an otherwise-good workspace open.

Behavior preserved (required, no blanket claims):
- A workspace with no browser among its apps opens its URLs exactly as before, through the untargeted default-browser call — pinned by `workspaceWithoutABrowserAppStillOpensURLsInTheSystemDefaultBrowser`, which asserts the browser argument is literally `nil`, not merely that the URL opened.
- The three non-workspace browser callers are untouched and still use the system default: `OpenSafeURLCapabilityAdapter` (a plain "open github.com"), `OpenAppSearchURLCapabilityAdapter`, and `WebResearchMarkdownCapabilityAdapter`'s Hacker News link. They call the `open(_:)` shorthand, which forwards to `open(_:using: nil)`. **Pinned, not merely asserted** — six `#expect(browserOpener.openedBrowsers == [nil])` assertions at the existing call sites for those three adapters, added 2026-08-03 in response to a PR #21 reviewer finding: this bullet originally made the claim in prose while every one of those tests checked only which URL opened, so a regression targeting a browser there would have kept them all green. Each of the six is mutation-proven live (see below). One of them also covers a routine's `openURL` step, which is the same shorthand reached through nested dispatch.
- Apps still open before URLs, `SafeURL.validateWebURL` still gates every URL, `MacAppCatalog` still gates every app, the workspace-open summary string is unchanged, and the capability stays tier 1 — `workspaceCanBeSavedAndOpened` and the whole `AgentActionExecutorTests`/`AgentRunnerTests`/`QuickDispatchTests` set pass unmodified except for the fake-opener signature update.
- A default-browser open that macOS declines still throws `BrowserOpeningError.failedToOpen` — the fallback is a fallback, not a swallow. Pinned by `aFallbackTheDefaultBrowserAlsoDeclinesStillThrows`, where both the named browser and the default refuse.

Architectural decisions / pitfalls discovered:

**The new signature is the protocol *requirement*, and the old one is the extension — deliberately that way round.** `BrowserOpening` now requires `open(_ url: URL, using browser: MacApp?)`, with `open(_ url: URL)` as a protocol-extension shorthand forwarding `nil`. The tempting inverse — keep `open(_:)` as the requirement and add `open(_:using:)` with a default implementation that ignores the browser — compiles for every existing conformance without edits, which is exactly its problem: a conformance that implements only `open(_:)` would silently drop browser targeting and no one would find out until a workspace opened in the wrong browser again. With the requirement on the two-argument form, that same conformance fails to compile. Six test fakes across three files needed updating; that is the feature working, not friction.

**Browser recognition keys on bundle identifier, not display name.** `MacAppCatalog.resolve` already collapses aliases down to one canonical `MacApp` before recognition ever runs — "Google Chrome" and "Chrome" both land on `com.google.Chrome` — so matching the resolved app's bundle identifier means one source of truth instead of two that can disagree. `WorkspaceBrowserCatalog.browserBundleIdentifiers` also lists Arc, Firefox and Edge, which `MacAppCatalog.default` does not carry yet: adding one of those to the allowlist should make it browser-aware in that same edit, with no second place to remember to update. Both halves are pinned (`recognitionCoversBrowsersTheDefaultCatalogDoesNotCarryYet` asserts the default catalog yields exactly Safari and Chrome *and* that the three absent identifiers are recognized), and a browser-shaped display name with an unknown bundle identifier is asserted *not* to be a browser — the set is the whole rule, nothing infers browser-ness from a name.

**The fallback lives inside `WorkspaceBrowserOpener`, not in the adapter.** The contract that makes the seam readable is "a named browser is a preference, not a requirement" — so the opener owns resolve-or-fall-back, and the adapter just says which browser it prefers. Putting it in the adapter would have meant catching an error there and retrying the other call, which reads as error handling and would have made it easy to swallow a genuine open failure along with an unavailable-browser one.

**Pitfall: a default argument on a `public init` cannot name a `private` static.** The live seams started out `private static func launchServicesOpen` and `private static let logger`, referenced from the initializer's default argument values — which fails with *"is private and cannot be referenced from a default argument value"*, because default arguments on public API are inlined at the call site and so must be at least as visible as the API itself. Both are now `public` with a comment saying that visibility is the only reason. Worth knowing before designing any other injectable-seam type with live defaults.

**The relocation took the whole seam, not just the struct.** `BrowserOpening`, `BrowserOpeningError` and `WorkspaceBrowserOpener` were all sitting in `HackerNewsService.swift`; none of them was ever about Hacker News, and moving only the struct would have left the protocol behind in the same wrong file. All three moved to `WorkspaceBrowserOpener.swift`, `HackerNewsService.swift`'s now-unused `import AppKit` came out with them, and the one existing opener test moved from `ProcessSeamTests` into the new `WorkspaceBrowserOpenerTests` with its own doc comment intact — the one explaining that a discarded `false` from `NSWorkspace.open` made every failed open report success, and that the test injects the refusal instead of using a real unregistered scheme. (An earlier draft of this bullet quoted that comment as *"a unit test must not drive Launch Services"*; that phrase is the heading of the `feature/full-repo-correctness-review` pitfall bullet further up this file, not text that appears in the test. Corrected 2026-08-03 after a PR #21 reviewer finding — the rule it names is the reason the test is written that way, but citing a changelog heading as source-code provenance sends the next reader looking for a comment that does not exist.)

**Verified by mutation, not by a green suite.** Seven reverts in total were applied and the suite rerun on each. Three on the feature itself: the adapter calling `open(url)` again (3 executor tests fail, and the no-browser test correctly still passes); the opener rethrowing instead of falling back (4 fallback tests fail); and `firstBrowser(in:)` returning `apps.first` instead of the first browser (4 ordering tests fail, including the executor-level one). Three more on the *unchanged* paths, added with the reviewer-requested assertions, each forcing one adapter to target a browser it should never target — `OpenSafeURLCapabilityAdapter` (3 red: `openAppAndURLExecutionUseInjectedOpeners`, `routineRunUsesNestedDispatchForMixedChains`, `tierOneVoiceCommandAutoRunsWithoutApprovalDecision`), `OpenAppSearchURLCapabilityAdapter` (2 red), and the Hacker News open (1 red) — six reds across six new assertions, so none is dead recording. And one on the fallback log's punctuation: dropping the trailing-period strip turns `aBrowserThatIsNotInstalledFallsBackToTheDefaultBrowserAndLogsWhy` red while the `.failedToOpen` case correctly stays green, since only one of the two descriptions ends in a period. All seven restored, full suite green afterwards.

**Pitfall the reviewer caught, worth generalizing: prose in this file is not evidence, and the two drift apart silently.** Three PR #21 findings were all the same shape — a confident sentence with nothing behind it. The "three non-workspace callers still use the system default" bullet was pure assertion (fixed above). This entry cited *"a unit test must not drive Launch Services"* as a code comment on the moved test when it is the heading of a pitfall bullet further up this very file, sending a reader looking for text that does not exist. And the fallback log line shipped with a doubled period — `"…com.apple.Safari.. Falling back…"` — because `AppOpeningError.appNotInstalled`'s description already ends in one and the template appended another; four `contains`-style assertions all passed straight through it, which is exactly what substring checks are bad at. The log line is now asserted whole, in both the period-carrying and period-free cases.

Known limitations / deferred scope:
- **A routine with the same ingredients still does not do this** — "open Safari" then "open github.com" as routine steps opens the URL in the system default browser, because an `openURL` step dispatches to `OpenSafeURLCapabilityAdapter` and its `open(url)` call. Filed as **SONNY-24** (backlog, untriaged) rather than absorbed here: a routine is an *ordered list of steps* where a workspace is a set of apps, so it needs a product decision first (does a browser step bind every URL step, or only later ones?), and fixing it would have meant editing two adapters SONNY-9's expected-touched-areas did not name plus changing the dispatch of a plain standalone "open github.com".
- **Only Safari and Chrome are reachable today**, because `MacAppCatalog.default` carries no other browser and `workspaceRunSpec` rejects any app outside the allowlist. Arc/Firefox/Edge users get the default-browser behavior until the catalog gains their app — a catalog question, not a browser-targeting one.
- **Neither the preview nor the activity log mentions the chosen browser.** Kept deliberately: the ticket's contract is that an unavailable browser is not a user-facing error, and the manual test observes the real outcome directly (the URL opens in Safari). The fallback's only trace is the `com.sonny.macagent`/`browser-opening` `Logger` line. If the silence proves confusing in real use, the act log is the cheap place to surface it.
- No per-workspace browser picker UI, and no `StoredWorkspace` schema change — both explicit non-goals of SONNY-9. Reordering a workspace's apps is currently the only way to change which browser it prefers, and there is no UI for that either.

Open questions for the next chat (required, write "none" if true): none

Next branch: none opened by this session. `feature/sonny-8-widget-composer-focus` (SONNY-8) merged 2026-08-03 ahead of this branch, which then rebased onto it. Roadmap row A's last item is `feature/sonny-10-approval-friction-audit` (SONNY-10, the cheap half of approval-friction work), not yet started — after which row A closes and row B (`feature/workspace-restriction-scope`) is next, still needing its planning phase and the founder decision with the co-founder before any implementation.

### Branch: feature/sonny-10-approval-friction-audit
Status: complete
Date: 2026-08-03
Tickets: SONNY-10 — audit every risk-escalation site in `MacAgentCore` for over-eager tier-3 escalations, fix only clear misfires, and close the first-run approval-legibility gap. Spawned discovery tickets SONNY-28, SONNY-29, SONNY-30, SONNY-31 (all backlog, untriaged; SONNY-29 and SONNY-31 attached to module C).
Reviewed by: fresh session (per WORKFLOW.md step 7) on PR #23 at `1462e64`, against main at `915fec9`, the review's record living wholly on SONNY-10's comments rather than the PR thread (backfilled here 2026-08-07 under SONNY-61). Verdict "nothing found here blocks the merge"; the reviewer reran the full suite itself (438 tests in 45 suites at `1462e64`, against 430 in 44 re-measured on main) and raised seven findings, all dispositioned before merge, the arc running the cap's full three cycles. F1 — the branch's new test could trap the whole suite on a real regression (`#expect` on a count, then direct indexing); fixed at `2a9f4dc`, re-verified by the reviewer's re-check ("F1 is closed. No new findings"). F2 — the egress-disclosure audit was one-directional: a `.writeMarkdown` step could disclose "Data leaves device: no" while a Hacker News preset still opened the browser and fetched the network; filed by the reviewer as SONNY-32. F3 — SONNY-28's data-loss characterization holds only for the mock converter, not the production Word path; posted as a correction on SONNY-28, which the user then repriced at triage. F4–F7 — wording and comment claims broader than the facts, marked no-action nits by the reviewer and corrected anyway at `633b0ae`, after the re-check had closed the arc. The implementing session ran its own six-cluster read-only audit (data-egress classification, nested-plan inheritance, file-overwrite escalations, named-store replacement escalations, policy defaults + tier aggregation, first-run legibility) covering 43 distinct sites, then put every proposed fix to two independent adversarial lenses (reachability, and scope-against-the-ticket's-bar) with unanimity required to survive. One of four candidates survived; the three rejections are recorded under deferred scope below rather than discarded.

Spec sections covered: §10/§11 (risk and approval) audited, not revised — no tier definition, no requirement mapping, and no policy default changed. §3.3.3's permission panel gains first-run-only copy; this branch changes nothing about its steady state (which is not itself wireframe-identical — see the wireframe-fidelity note below).
Files changed:
- `Sources/MacAgentCore/AgentActionExecutor.swift`
- `Sources/MacAgent/AgentActivityPresentation.swift`
- `Sources/MacAgent/FloatingWidgetView.swift`
- `Tests/MacAgentCoreTests/AgentActionExecutorTests.swift`
- `Tests/MacAgentTests/WidgetApprovalExplainerTests.swift` (new)
- `docs/sonny-v1-implementation-changelog.md`

Tests: `env CLANG_MODULE_CACHE_PATH="$PWD/.build/clang-module-cache" swift test --disable-sandbox -Xswiftc -F -Xswiftc /Library/Developer/CommandLineTools/Library/Developer/Frameworks -Xlinker -rpath -Xlinker /Library/Developer/CommandLineTools/Library/Developer/Frameworks -Xlinker -rpath -Xlinker /Library/Developer/CommandLineTools/Library/Developer/usr/lib` -> exit 0, **438 tests in 45 suites**, up from the 430 in 44 measured on `main` before this branch. `swift build` -> exit 0.

Behavior added:
- An apps-only workspace open no longer claims "Data leaves device: yes" on the approval panel. `.openWorkspace` moved out of the flat `dataEgressOperations` set into a `stepLeavesDevice` branch that loads the named workspace and answers on whether it actually carries URLs — the same load-the-saved-thing shape `.runRoutine` already used.
- The first-run approval panel now says why *this* action needs approval, not only that Sonny always asks. `RiskApprovalCopy.riskReason` renders as a second `isFirstApproval`-gated line, sourced from the copy the risk engine already builds and Command Center already shows.

Behavior preserved (required, no blanket claims):
- **A workspace that really carries URLs still reports egress** — pinned by `openingAWorkspaceCarryingURLsStillReportsDataLeavingDevice`, which exists specifically so the fix cannot silently degrade into "always no". Mutation-checked: reverting the fix turns the two apps-only tests red while this one correctly stays green.
- **Every other member of `dataEgressOperations` is unchanged and still unconditional** — `.openHackerNews`, `.fetchHNHeadlines`, `.webToMarkdown`, `.openAppSearchURL`, `.openURL`, `.playMedia`, `.invokeShortcut`. Each was audited against its owning adapter's execute path and every one leaves the device on every execution, so none belongs in the conditional branch.
- **The `.runRoutine` recursion is behaviorally identical.** It still loads the routine and scans one level, and one level is still sufficient — stored routines cannot contain `.runRoutine` *or* `.openWorkspace`, both rejected by `SaveRoutineCapabilityAdapter.validateRoutineSteps`. `runRoutineWrappingOpenURLReportsDataLeavingDevice` passes unmodified.
- **No tier moved anywhere.** `dataLeavesDevice` only ever fed `RiskApprovalCopy`; `AgentActionExecutor.assessRisk` derives tiers purely from the adapters' own assessments. `openingAnAppsOnlyWorkspaceDoesNotClaimDataLeavesDevice` asserts the effective tier is still 1, and `RiskApprovalTests.defaultExecutableCapabilityTiersMatchSpec` passes unmodified.
- **This branch adds nothing to the steady-state permission panel.** `firstRunApprovalExplainerLines` returns an empty array once `hasCompletedFirstApproval` has flipped. Pinned only at the level it is actually tested: `noExplainerLinesOnceTheFirstApprovalHasBeenResolved` pins the pure function's `isFirstApproval: false` input, **not** the real first-to-second-approval transition — nothing anywhere touches `AgentViewModel.hasCompletedFirstApproval` in tests, a pre-existing gap this branch did not close and should not be read as having closed (recorded by PR #23's fresh-session review, finding F7).
- **`hasCompletedFirstApproval` itself is untouched** — still `private(set)`, still read with `object(forKey:) as? Bool`, and still flipped in exactly two places (`performApproval`'s allow branch, `cancelCurrentRun`'s deny branch). "Two flip sites" is not "two sites that touch a pending approval": `performStart` and `clearInMemoryLocalDataState` also clear `approvalRequest` without flipping the flag, correctly, because neither is the user *resolving* anything. A reader who takes the two flip sites as the complete list of places pending-approval state moves will be surprised.
- **`CommandCenterAttentionPanel` is untouched** — it already rendered all five `RiskApprovalCopy.lines` including `riskReason`, so the two surfaces now agree on the first run instead of diverging. `CommandCenterAttentionSurfaceTests` passes unmodified.

Architectural decisions / pitfalls discovered (required, write "none" if true):

**The ticket's own framing — "over-eager tier-3 escalations" — needed recalibrating before the audit could be useful, and this is the single most reusable finding here.** Under `RiskApprovalPolicy.default`, `requiresUserApproval` is true for *both* `.lightweightConfirmation` (tier 2) and `.explicitApproval` (tier 3), and both render the same widget panel. **A tier-2 → tier-3 escalation therefore adds no prompt a user would not already have seen.** Every prompt anyone has ever complained about is something crossing the tier-1 → tier-2 line, not the tier-2 → tier-3 line. Anyone hunting approval friction by grepping for tier-3 escalations is looking in the wrong place.

**Where tier 3 does have teeth is unattended runs, and there it is absolute.** `AgentViewModel` executes a scheduled routine with `approvalDecision: .approved(.tier2)` — the only tier an unattended run can carry — and `AgentRunner` requires `approvedTier >= effectiveTier`. So any tier-3 escalation does not prompt; it *skips the run*, and because most escalation conditions are state-based and sticky, it usually skips every future run too. That is the real cost of a spurious tier 3, and it is invisible in the foreground.

**`dataLeavesDevice` is copy, not gating — worth knowing before touching it.** It feeds one line of `RiskApprovalCopy` and nothing else; no tier anywhere reads it. That is what made the `.openWorkspace` fix safe to make inside an audit ticket: the blast radius is a sentence, not a gate. It also means a wrong answer there is purely a truthfulness defect, which is exactly why it still had to be fixed — a permission prompt that misstates what is about to happen trains users to stop reading it.

**The membership-set-versus-lookup distinction is the reusable rule.** An operation belongs in `dataEgressOperations` only if it leaves the device on *every* execution regardless of its parameters. An operation that merely *names* saved content — a workspace, a routine — cannot be answered by the operation alone and needs a lookup branch. `.openWorkspace` had been filed under the first rule while being the second kind, and `.runRoutine` sitting right beside it with the correct treatment is what made the inconsistency findable. Any future operation that references stored content should be checked against this rule before being added to the set.

**Adversarial verification earned its keep by killing more than it confirmed.** Four fix candidates went in, one came out. Two were rejected on a factual ground the original auditor had missed — `WidgetPermissionPanel` renders `WidgetExistingStepRows` above everything else, so the plan-level `actionDescription` a proposed fix wanted to add is strictly coarser than the per-step titles already on screen. The lesson generalizes: "field X is not rendered" is a grep result, not a finding, until you have read what *is* rendered.

**Wireframe-fidelity exception, stated rather than silent (per CLAUDE.md's rule).** `docs/sonny-design-system-reference.md:145` specifies the permission panel as one "Allow access to [resource]" line plus two icon-only buttons — no explanatory lines at all. This branch adds a second `isFirstApproval`-gated line. That is a deliberate, bounded exception, not drift: the first-run moment is the one place `docs/sonny-founder-design-decisions.md` explicitly asks for "first-time-specific framing on the panel itself," the existing reassurance sentence is already that same class of accepted deviation, and both disappear permanently once the user resolves one approval, so the panel the wireframe governs is **unchanged in steady state by this branch**.

That narrower claim is the accurate one, and the difference matters. The panel is *not* byte-identical to the wireframe in steady state, and this branch is not what makes it so: `WidgetPermissionPanel` also renders an ungated `escalationReasons` line, so any escalated approval — the hundredth as much as the first — still carries a line the wireframe does not specify. That line predates this branch (it shipped with branch 10's escalation-reason work) and is left exactly as found here, but it is a second, pre-existing deviation from the same wireframe row and is recorded as one now rather than staying implicit — flagged by PR #23's fresh-session review, finding F5.

**Presentation logic that needs a test has to leave the view.** This repo has no SwiftUI view-inspection harness, so a copy change made inline in `WidgetPermissionPanel.body` would have been untestable and would have violated the ticket's "every behavior change has a test that fails with the change reverted". Putting it in `AgentActivityPresentation` as a pure function follows the pattern `TaskGreetingFormatter` and `WorkspaceAvatarInitial` already set. The test that pins *which* `RiskApprovalCopy` field is read gives every field a distinct fixture value, so rendering `undoDescription` by mistake fails loudly instead of shipping a plausible-looking wrong sentence.

Known limitations / deferred scope:

- **Recorded and deliberately not changed — `SaveRoutineCapabilityAdapter` inherits the nested plan's execution-time escalations onto the *save*.** `assessRisk` folds `nested.escalations` and `nested.effectiveTier` into the save's assessment, but `execute` only writes the routine file — it never runs a step. Saving a routine whose `writeMarkdown` step names an explicit `~/Desktop/hn.md` that already exists therefore shows "Markdown output already exists at ~/Desktop/hn.md." on the approval panel for an action that will not touch that file. Reachability was verified end to end. It is left unchanged because it fails the ticket's clear-misfire bar in both directions at once: the sentence is literally true of the filesystem at assessment time, `.claude/rules/macagentcore-conventions.md` explicitly requires all three nested closures precisely so nested risk is not silently dropped, and removing the fold would delete the only save-time signal that the routine being saved will later assess tier 3. **This is the primary input to module C's structural work** — the question it needs answered is whether save-time risk means "what this action does" or "what this thing will do when it runs", which is a product decision, not a bug fix.
- **Recorded — the baseline tier-2 assignments for purely-local, explicitly-requested saves** (`CreateWorkspace`, `SnippetSave`, `SaveRoutine`, `RunRoutine`, `DocxConversion`). These are where real prompt friction lives, since tier 2 is the line that adds a prompt at all. Every one is a blanket capability-class assignment rather than a false claim about any specific run, so none clears the misfire bar; relaxing them is exactly the structural relaxation module C is gated on, and module B (workspace restriction scoping) is gated ahead of that.
- **Recorded — `InvokeShortcutCapabilityAdapter` records no `CapabilityRiskEscalation` for its tier-2 case**, so a Shortcut without clean observed history asks for approval showing no Shortcut-specific reason. Shared with every baseline-tier-2 capability rather than specific to Shortcuts; debatable copy work, not a misfire.
- **Recorded — `involvedResource` can join up to three resources into a line the widget renders with `lineLimit(1)`.** The single-line treatment is literal wireframe fidelity; the data model has outgrown the wireframe's single-resource assumption. A design question, not a defect.
- **Recorded — `SnippetStore.findExactTrigger` matches on trim only** while `WorkspaceStore`/`RoutineStore` fold case and diacritics. Internally consistent (the lookup key and the storage key use the same function), so it produces neither false nor missed escalations on its own; the cross-capability inconsistency is real but is matching-semantics work.
- **The curated-example half of branch 9's checkpoint 8 remains explicitly deferred**, unchanged by this branch, for the reason already recorded in `docs/sonny-founder-design-decisions.md`: the open question is *which surface* it lives on now that Command Center has no composer, and that needs founder input rather than an implementation guess.
- **Four discovery tickets filed, untriaged, none implemented here** (WORKFLOW.md step 5 — a session never implements a ticket it created for itself): **SONNY-28** DOCX conversion silently overwrites its own output when two same-named `.docx` files share one output folder, with no escalation possible because that adapter has no `assessRisk` override at all; **SONNY-29** `AgentActionExecutor.assessRisk` is the only plan walk that is not chain-segmented, so in a plan with two steps of the same operation only the first is assessed (module C); **SONNY-30** `CreateWorkspace`/`SaveRoutine` decide existence with `try?`, so a store load failure suppresses a correct replacement escalation — the anti-pattern the store conventions name explicitly, and `SnippetSave` is the sibling that gets it right; **SONNY-31** a scheduled routine containing a `save_snippet` step is permanently skipped from its second run onward, because run one creates the trigger that makes run two tier 3 (module C).

Open questions (required, write "none" if true): none — every open item above is either a filed ticket or an explicitly recorded module-B/C decision.

Next branch: none opened by this session. Roadmap row A ("A — manual-pass follow-ups") closes with this branch: SONNY-8, SONNY-9 and SONNY-10 are all complete. Row B (`feature/workspace-restriction-scope`) is next and still needs its planning phase plus the founder decision with the co-founder before any implementation; row C (`feature/approval-relaxation-structural`) inherits this audit's recorded-but-unchanged findings and SONNY-29/SONNY-31 as its concrete starting material.

### Branch: feature/sonny-29-chain-segmented-risk
Status: complete
Date: 2026-08-04
Tickets: SONNY-29 — make `AgentActionExecutor.assessRisk` chain-segmented so every segment the executor actually runs is risk-assessed, and stop `WebResearchMarkdownCapabilityAdapter` resolving/assessing only the first matching output. Spawned discovery tickets SONNY-34 and SONNY-35 (both backlog, untriaged, attached to module C).
Reviewed by: fresh session (per WORKFLOW.md step 7) on PR #25. **Finding F1, closed before merge:** the max-across-segments aggregation silently fixed two *further* under-assessments beyond the collision escalations the ticket named — a per-step baseline tier (Shortcut trust) and a whole invisible nested plan (a second `.runRoutine` step) — and neither had any regression protection. Both are now pinned by tests and mutation-checked against three separate reverts (segmentation off, no max across segments, no escalation union); the two behaviors are written up under "Behavior added" below rather than left as undocumented side effects. The reviewer also corrected a mislabeled row in the implementing session's mutation table (the recorded result belonged to a narrower "gate the preset check on a write step" mutation, not to literal deletion of the branch, which catches harder); the corrected table is on SONNY-29's fix comment. Every mutation result, before and after, is recorded on SONNY-29's comments.

Spec sections covered: §10/§11 (risk and approval) — the *walk* changed, nothing else. No tier definition, no escalation kind, no policy mapping, and no `shouldChainWhenRepeated` classification was touched.
Files changed:
- `Sources/MacAgentCore/AgentActionExecutor.swift`
- `Sources/MacAgentCore/WebResearchMarkdownCapabilityAdapter.swift`
- `Tests/MacAgentCoreTests/AgentActionExecutorTests.swift`
- `docs/sonny-v1-implementation-changelog.md`

Tests: `env CLANG_MODULE_CACHE_PATH="$PWD/.build/clang-module-cache" swift test --disable-sandbox -Xswiftc -F -Xswiftc /Library/Developer/CommandLineTools/Library/Developer/Frameworks -Xlinker -rpath -Xlinker /Library/Developer/CommandLineTools/Library/Developer/Frameworks -Xlinker -rpath -Xlinker /Library/Developer/CommandLineTools/Library/Developer/usr/lib` -> exit 0, **446 tests in 45 suites**, up from the 438 in 45 measured on `main` before this branch. `swift build` -> exit 0.

Behavior added:
- Risk assessment is chain-segmented. `assessRisk` walks `assessmentSegments(in:)`, which splits a `.chain` with the same `segmentPlans(in:)` `previewChain`/`executeChain` already use and hands every other plan to its adapter whole exactly as `previewCapability`/`executeCapability` do. A chain of two `.createLocalDraft` steps now assesses both: the second draft's collision with an existing file raises tier 3 and gates on explicit approval instead of overwriting silently at tier 2. Same for two zip segments and for the Hacker-News-preset-plus-web-research chain.
- Escalations aggregate as a union with exact duplicates collapsed, so two steps colliding with the same file state it once on the approval panel rather than twice in one joined sentence.
- `WebResearchMarkdownCapabilityAdapter.resolveDefaultOutputs` no longer returns after resolving the first `.writeMarkdown` step, so a `.webToMarkdown` step later in the same plan gets a concrete default path in the prepared plan, its preview, and the approval copy.
- **A chain of Shortcut invocations is assessed at the least-trusted Shortcut's tier, not the first one's** (found by PR #25's review, finding F1). `InvokeShortcutCapabilityAdapter.assessRisk` demotes to tier 1 for a Shortcut with a clean observed success and stays tier 2 otherwise, and it picks its step with `.first(where:)` — so a chain whose *first* Shortcut was trusted assessed the whole plan at tier 1, which under `RiskApprovalPolicy.default` is `.autoRun`. Both Shortcuts then ran with no confirmation at all, including one Sonny had never observed succeed. Now tier 2, lightweight confirmation.
- **A chain running two saved routines assesses both routines' nested plans** (also F1). `RunRoutineCapabilityAdapter` resolves its routine from the first `.runRoutine` step, so `assessNestedPlan` was never called for the second one: that routine's file collisions — and every other dynamic condition inside it — did not exist as far as the approval gate was concerned, while `executeChain` ran it anyway. A second routine whose nested draft collides with an existing file now raises tier 3 and surfaces the collision reason.

Behavior preserved (required, no blanket claims):
- **Every non-chain plan assesses byte-identically.** `assessmentSegments` returns `[plan]` unless `workflow(in:)` says `.chain`, so single-step plans, `[scan, zip]`, `[scanDocx, convertDocxToPDF]`, and the Hacker News preset all take the exact path they took before. `existingZipOutputEscalatesToTierThreeAndLogsRiskEvent`, `existingHackerNewsMarkdownOutputEscalatesToTierThree` and `existingWebResearchMarkdownOutputEscalatesAndMarksDataLeavingDevice` pass unmodified.
- **The approval copy is unchanged for every plan that was already assessed correctly.** `approvalCopy` is still built from the whole resolved plan, and its `metadata` argument is still deduplicated by capability id in step order — segments preserve step order, so iterating segments then steps visits the same steps in the same order as the old whole-plan `capabilityAdapters(in:)` call and produces the same list. `appsOnlyWorkspaceChainedWithALocalSaveDoesNotClaimDataLeavesDevice`, `openingAnAppsOnlyWorkspaceDoesNotClaimDataLeavesDevice` and `runRoutineWrappingOpenURLReportsDataLeavingDevice` pass unmodified.
- **A chain of *different* operations still unions every escalation, in step order** — pinned by `chainOfDifferentOperationsStillUnionsEveryEscalation`, which exists so the dedupe cannot degrade into collapsing genuinely distinct collisions.
- **Chains with nothing to escalate stay at tier 2** — `chainedDraftsWithNoExistingOutputsStayAtTierTwo` guards the opposite failure, a segmented walk inventing escalations.
- **`AgentRunner` is untouched**, as is `RiskApproval.swift`, `UnattendedTrustAdvisory.swift`, and all of `Sources/MacAgent/` (the ticket's never-touch list). The advisory's accuracy improves for free: it reads `effectiveTier` from this same call, so a routine whose second step collides is now correctly reported as needing approval.
- **No adapter's own `assessRisk` needed changing.** Every override picks its step with `.first(where:)` on its own operation, which is correct once it is handed a segment; the only adapter edited was `WebResearchMarkdownCapabilityAdapter`, and only where it read *across* the two kinds of output it owns.

Architectural decisions / pitfalls discovered (required, write "none" if true):

**The correct segmentation for assessment is "mirror the executor's dispatch", not "always call `segmentPlans`".** `segmentPlans(in:)` splits any plan, but `preview`/`execute` only use it for `.chain`; a non-chain multi-step plan (`[scan, zip]`, the HN preset) is deliberately handed to one adapter whole because the adapter reads across those steps. Calling `segmentPlans` unconditionally would have split `[fetchHNHeadlines, writeMarkdown]` into two segments the executor never runs separately, and assessed a default output path that nothing writes. The invariant worth stating: **assessment must see exactly the units execution sees — no more and no fewer.** That is also why `shouldChainWhenRepeated` did not need to change (SONNY-29's stop-and-report trigger): for the workflows it excludes, execution and assessment both stop at the first occurrence, so nothing is written unassessed.

**`WebResearchMarkdownCapabilityAdapter`'s both-kinds branch is deliberately kept although segmentation makes it unreachable from the executor.** `outputURLsForRiskAssessment` now checks the preset output *and* the research output instead of answering `isHackerNewsPreset` first and returning one of them. Once chains are segmented, no caller hands this adapter a plan carrying both kinds (`.webToMarkdown` maps to a different `Workflow`, so any plan holding both is a chain, and `segmentPlans` never absorbs a `.webToMarkdown` step into the preset's segment). It is kept because a future change to how segments are cut would otherwise quietly restore the under-assessment. **Honest limitation: this branch is therefore not pinned by a test** — mutation-checking it (restoring the either/or) leaves the whole suite green, and only reverting it *together with* segmentation turns `webResearchSegmentOfAHackerNewsChainIsAssessedForItsOwnCollision` red. Pinning it directly needs a `CapabilityExecutionContext` built in a test, which nothing in this suite does today. `resolveDefaultOutputs`'s equivalent fix *is* reachable and *is* pinned, because the executor calls it with the whole unsegmented plan.

**"One URL per kind" is the correct granularity inside that adapter, not "one URL per step".** An earlier draft assessed every `.writeMarkdown` and `.webToMarkdown` step. That over-assesses: `segmentPlans` absorbs consecutive `.writeMarkdown` steps into one preset segment, and `hackerNewsSpec` writes only the first, so a second step's output would gate on a file nothing ever writes. Same reasoning applies to `resolveDefaultOutputs` — resolving a second `.writeMarkdown` step would advertise a destination that is never written.

**Segmenting the walk fixes three distinct classes of under-assessment, not one — and only the first was obvious.** The ticket was written around escalations (a second step's file collision). But the aggregation reads two more things off each segment's assessment, and both were being read off the wrong segment: (1) `defaultTier`, which is *not* a constant per capability — `InvokeShortcutCapabilityAdapter` returns tier 1 or tier 2 depending on the named Shortcut's observed history, so a chain's baseline tier depends on which step you look at; (2) whatever an adapter computes by recursing, which for `.runRoutine` is an entire nested plan reached through `assessNestedPlan`. The second is the worst of the three, because nothing about the outer plan hints that a whole sub-plan went unassessed. **The reusable rule: when an adapter's `assessRisk` derives anything from step-specific state — not just escalations, but its baseline tier or a nested assessment — a first-match walk under-assesses every occurrence after the first.** Both were found by PR #25's review, not by the implementing session, precisely because the ticket's framing pointed at escalations.

**A per-segment walk multiplies an adapter's assessment side effects.** `InvokeShortcutCapabilityAdapter.assessRisk` shells out to `shortcuts list` (it carries a comment about doing this once per execute), so a chain invoking three Shortcuts now runs three enumerations at assessment time instead of one. That is the price of assessing each named Shortcut rather than the first one three times, and it matches what `executeChain` already does — but any adapter whose `assessRisk` is expensive or observable should now assume it is called once per segment.

Known limitations / deferred scope:
- **Two discovery tickets filed, untriaged, neither implemented here** (WORKFLOW.md step 5 — a session never implements a ticket it created for itself). **SONNY-34**: repeated steps of a non-chaining workflow (`.largestFiles`, `.docx`, `.hackerNews`, `.webResearch`) are silently dropped — "zip the largest files in A and in B" creates one archive and reports success. It is under-*execution*, not under-assessment, so SONNY-29's invariant already holds for it and fixing it is the `shouldChainWhenRepeated` reclassification SONNY-29 named as module C's. **SONNY-35**: `CreateLocalDraftCapabilityAdapter` and `LargestFilesZipCapabilityAdapter` resolve only the first matching step's default output path, so a second draft with no explicit destination stays `nil` in the prepared plan and its timestamped default path is re-derived independently at assess and execute time — the same defect class fixed here for the web-research adapter, in two adapters SONNY-29's scoped requirements did not name.
- **Escalations still carry no structured segment/step attribution.** The ticket asked for escalations "attributed to its segment/step so the approval panel can name which step raised what"; that is satisfied through the reason strings, which already embed the concrete path or name ("Draft output already exists at …/b.md."), and both surfaces render exactly those strings. Adding a field to `CapabilityRiskEscalation` was rejected: it is a public `Codable` type, and rendering it needs `Sources/MacAgent/`, which is on this ticket's never-touch list.

Open questions (required, write "none" if true): none.

Next branch: none opened by this session. SONNY-31 and SONNY-24 are the next two tickets in this session's pre-assigned serial sequence, each on its own branch, serial through merge because all three touch the executor/assessment area.

### Branch: feature/sonny-12-branch-b-planning

Status: complete
Date: 2026-08-04

Spawned SONNY-36 through SONNY-42, all user-approved as a batch and attached to module "B — workspace restriction scope". No discovery tickets filed: everything found was either in-scope planning material or already covered by an existing ticket (SONNY-29/30/31/32/33).
Reviewed by: fresh session (per WORKFLOW.md step 7) on PR #26, the review's record living wholly on SONNY-12's comments — the PR body's own post-review note is stale, appended before the F12–F19 fix landed and still calling those findings open (backfilled here 2026-08-07 under SONNY-61). Verdict: "the planning work is sound and the ticket contracts are unusually strong" — nineteen findings, none invalidating the plan, all nineteen closed before merge: three missing halves of recorded decisions (F1–F3), a factual error repeated in six places (F4), seven contract gaps that would have let a requirement ship untested (F5–F11), and eight records-and-nits (F12–F19). Spec sections covered: none amended here. Row B's spec sections are now *named* (§6.9, §10.4, §11.2, §11.3, §9.2, §9.3) and the amendment itself is contracted as SONNY-42 on the implementation branch, not done on this planning branch.
