Row B (`feature/workspace-restriction-scope`, SONNY-36 → SONNY-44 → SONNY-37) and `feature/automation-store-hardening` (SONNY-52) are in flight in other worktrees against `main` at `ca4fbe4`, which is why this docs-only branch could run alongside them: at their tips (`dea6b5f` and `7727a58`) neither touches any of this branch's four files. Expect that to stop being true for this changelog specifically — every branch writes an entry into it before merging — so whichever of the three merges second rebases onto the first and re-checks its entry's neighbours, per step 3's merge-one-at-a-time rule.

### Branch: feature/automation-store-hardening
Status: complete
Date: 2026-08-06
Tickets: SONNY-52 — routine step safety is enforced at `RoutineStore.save`, not only at the save capability, with a sanctioned test-only bypass. SONNY-46 — `RoutineSchedule`'s `isEnabled` + `pausedReason` pair folded into one `RoutineActivation` enum, with a self-repairing legacy decode. Spawned no discovery tickets. SONNY-47 was cancelled at triage as a duplicate filing of SONNY-46 (both cite PR #27, F6); its unique evidence was merged into SONNY-46's contract.
Reviewed by: fresh session (per WORKFLOW.md step 7) on PR #30 — **verdict approve, no correctness defect found in either ticket.** Everything the branch claimed was re-measured independently in a detached worktree rather than read: the suite (471 in 45 at `ca4fbe4`, 493 in 47 at the reviewed head `e2d9e5c`), the compiler call-site sweep (all four numbers — 74 lines, 12 files, 57 functions, 1 production writer — reproduced exactly by the same probe method), the four compile errors closing the illegal-state routes, and five of the fifteen mutations (M1, M2, M7, M10, M13), all confirmed. The hand-written `Codable` was hand-traced rather than trusted to a green suite: all 8 stored properties read and written, `decodeIfPresent` precedence the right way round, all three legacy mappings correct. The "exactly 2 sites need the bypass" claim was verified from the converse direction — reverting both bypass calls to `save` fails exactly the two named tests and nothing else. Three findings, all records-level, none touching code. **R1** — this very field, which read "pending at time of writing" in the entry as first written; WORKFLOW.md step 7 asks for the entry before the PR opens, so the placeholder was expected, but the merged entry must not keep it. Fixed in the fix round. **R2** — the stale "52 / 48 / 11" figure survived verbatim in SONNY-24's F5 paragraph above, where a reader landing by search picks it up with no correction in view, alongside a "needs that door to exist" sentence this branch narrowed; an additive correction was added there in the same round. **R3** (informational, no action) — the review brief named `d329ea9` as head while the remote was `e2d9e5c`; the review re-anchored to `e2d9e5c` per step 0, since the intervening commit is docs-only and the code is byte-identical at both. Two nits, no action: **N1**, the bypass doc comment's "nothing outside `MacAgentCore` … may write a routine the store would refuse" is exactly right under a plain import but a debug SwiftPM build would still admit an `@testable` one (a property of SwiftPM, not of this change; `grep -rn "@testable" Sources/` is empty, so adding one would be conspicuous); **N2**, "the only mutators" is true of mutating instance methods, while `init` and the `newlyCreated` factory also write `activation` — both construction inside the type, where `private(set)` permits it, and neither can produce an illegal state. The review also recorded an observation rather than a finding — that the branch's legacy-decode coverage all ran on a *plaintext* fixture, leaving the encrypted path to the manual item alone. The coordinator adjudicated it onto SONNY-46 as fix-round material, and `anEncryptedLegacyRoutinesFileSurvivesDecryptAndDecodeWithEveryScheduleStateIntact` now writes all four legacy shapes through `LocalStorageEncryption.encode` and asserts the mappings — the self-repair included — survive decrypt as well as decode. The two manual-test items still remain owed by the user and cannot be discharged by any agent: no synthetic fixture reproduces a file written by a genuinely older build.

Spec sections covered: none newly. Both tickets harden existing model/store correctness under §6.9/§9.2-§9.3 (routines as saved automations). No risk tier, approval mapping, planner vocabulary, or user-facing copy changed.
Files changed:
- `Sources/MacAgentCore/AutomationStores.swift` (`StoredRoutine.forbiddenStepOperations`, `StoredRoutine.validateStepSafety`, `RoutineStore.save`, `RoutineStore.saveBypassingStepValidation`, `RoutineStore.persist(merging:)`)
- `Sources/MacAgentCore/SaveRoutineCapabilityAdapter.swift` (`validateRoutineSteps` delegates; messages and behavior unchanged)
- `Sources/MacAgentCore/RoutineSchedule.swift` (`RoutineActivation` and its `Codable`; `RoutineSchedule`'s stored pair, accessors, `init`, `newlyCreated`, `setEnabled`, `pause`, and hand-written `Codable`)
- `Sources/MacAgent/CommandCenterView.swift` (`RoutineRowPresentation.isPaused`)
- `Sources/MacAgent/RoutineDetailView.swift` (the paused note matches the enum case)
- `Tests/MacAgentCoreTests/AutomationStoresTests.swift` (7 new store tests)
- `Tests/MacAgentCoreTests/AgentActionExecutorTests.swift` (2 new adapter tests; two stale doc comments corrected)
- `Tests/MacAgentCoreTests/RoutineActivationTests.swift` (new, 12 tests — 11 at `239002e`, plus the encrypted-legacy decode test the fix round added at `85feb80`)
- `Tests/MacAgentTests/RoutineActivationPresentationTests.swift` (new, 2 tests)
- `Tests/MacAgentCoreTests/WorkspaceTaskTaggingTests.swift`, `Tests/MacAgentTests/ProductShellTests.swift` (the two bypass migrations)
- `docs/sonny-v1-implementation-changelog.md`

Tests: `env CLANG_MODULE_CACHE_PATH="$PWD/.build/clang-module-cache" swift test --disable-sandbox -Xswiftc -F -Xswiftc /Library/Developer/CommandLineTools/Library/Developer/Frameworks -Xlinker -rpath -Xlinker /Library/Developer/CommandLineTools/Library/Developer/Frameworks -Xlinker -rpath -Xlinker /Library/Developer/CommandLineTools/Library/Developer/usr/lib` -> exit 0, **494 tests in 47 suites** at `85feb80`, up from the **471 in 45** measured on `ca4fbe4` and confirmed there independently by PR #30's reviewer. Intermediate: 480 in 45 (SONNY-52), 493 in 47 (SONNY-46), 494 in 47 after the fix round added the encrypted-legacy test. `swift build` -> exit 0 at every point. Every count above carries the SHA it was measured at, per the convention SONNY-24's entry introduced.

**SHA note, because this branch was rebased after PR #29 merged.** The counts above were first measured pre-rebase at `7727a58` / `d329ea9` / `e2d9e5c`; the branch then rebased onto `feb27c7`, replaying those as `7c17561` / `239002e` / `3acce6d`. The counts carry rather than being restated on faith: `git diff` between each original and its replay, restricted to `Sources` and `Tests`, is zero lines in all three cases, and `git diff --stat ca4fbe4 feb27c7` touches only `CLAUDE.md`, `WORKFLOW.md` and two `docs/` files — no `Sources` or `Tests` at all, which is why the `ca4fbe4` base count is still the base count at `feb27c7` (the docs-only exemption WORKFLOW.md step 7 names, applied to a base rather than a diff). The only commit that changed test code after the rebase is `85feb80`, and its count is measured directly.

Behavior added:
- **`RoutineStore.save` rejects a routine containing a forbidden step**, throwing `AutomationStoreError.unsafeRoutineStep` — a distinct case from schedule validation's `.invalidSchedule`. The store is now the choke-point guarantee for step safety the way it already was for `RoutineSchedule.validate()`.
- **`RoutineStore.saveBypassingStepValidation`** — module-internal, doc-commented test-only, bypassing *step* validation only (the schedule is still validated and `save`'s merge-on-nil semantics are shared through a private `persist(merging:)`).
- **`RoutineActivation`** replaces `isEnabled: Bool` + `pausedReason: String?` on `RoutineSchedule`. The illegal enabled-and-carrying-a-reason combination is unrepresentable rather than merely unreachable, and a legacy file already holding it self-repairs on decode.

Behavior preserved (required, no blanket claims):
- **The save capability's user-facing refusals are byte-identical.** Same `AutomationStoreError.unsafeRoutineStep` case, same operation `rawValue` in the message, same per-step scan order (operation, then nested), same `previewNestedPlan` afterwards. It had *no* test before this branch, so two were added rather than the claim being asserted.
- **SONNY-31's schedule/pause suite passes unchanged in the strongest available sense**: `git diff origin/main -- Tests/MacAgentCoreTests/RoutineScheduleTests.swift Tests/MacAgentTests/ScheduledRoutineRunTests.swift` is empty. Not one assertion, construction line, or import was touched. SONNY-46's new coverage went into two new files specifically so that claim stays checkable by `git diff` rather than by reading.
- **`AgentViewModel` needed no change at all.** `commitScheduleDraft` still passes `existing?.isEnabled` and `existing?.pausedReason` into `newlyCreated`, which keeps its signature; both are now computed accessors over the enum. The F1-class defect SONNY-31 fixed stays fixed, and `editingAPausedRoutinesRunTimeKeepsItPausedWithItsReason` still passes untouched.
- **`setEnabled(false, now:)` still leaves an existing pause reason alone.** Only a schedule that is currently `.enabled` becomes `.disabledByUser`; one already off stays exactly as it is. That is what makes editing a paused routine's run time non-destructive, and the mutation that breaks it reddens SONNY-31's own F9 test.
- **The scheduler, streak and `isScheduled` reads are untouched** — `RoutineScheduler.decision`, `RoutineStreak.nextRunText`/`current`, and `StoredRoutine.isScheduled` all still read `schedule.isEnabled`, now a computed accessor with identical results for every legal state. No date math changed.
- **`RoutineStore`'s other four mutators are untouched** — `setSchedule`, `advanceScheduleBaseline`, `pauseSchedule`, `recordRun` each still mutate only `schedule` or `recentRunDates`, and none validates steps (see the deferred-scope note below for why that is correct rather than an omission).
- **The three legal Routines-row renders are unchanged**: paused shows needs-attention, a user's own disable does not, and an enabled routine shows its next run.

Architectural decisions / pitfalls discovered (required, write "none" if true):

**Count call sites with the compiler, not with grep — this branch's headline measurement correction.** The discovery note said nineteen direct `RoutineStore.save` sites; PR #28's re-check said 52 lines across 48 functions in 11 files. Both are wrong, and both were produced by grep, which cannot resolve a receiver named `store` to a type and silently misses dotted receivers (`fixture.routineStore.save(`) and multi-line calls. The reliable method is to make the compiler enumerate: annotate the method `@available(*, deprecated, message: "PROBE")`, build with `--build-tests`, and collect the warning locations. **The real population at `ca4fbe4` is 74 call-site lines across 57 enclosing functions in 12 files** — 1 in `Sources/`, 73 across 11 test files — and one further entry that is the synthetic `macro expansion #expect` buffer rather than a source line, which is exactly the kind of double-count a naive grep of compiler output would also get wrong. The same technique proves the negative afterwards: compiling a probe file that tries all four routes to the illegal `RoutineSchedule` state returns four errors ("extra argument 'pausedReason'", two "get-only property", "'activation' setter is inaccessible"), and it does so through `@testable`, which widens `internal` but *not* `private(set)`.

**Determine which call sites must migrate empirically, not by prediction.** The store check was added first and the suite run; the failures *are* the population that needed the bypass. Exactly two of 74, both writing `.openWorkspace` routines. Predicting would have been wrong in a load-bearing way: this branch's own contract, the discovery ticket, and the test's own doc comment all assumed SONNY-24's skip-branch test needed the bypass, and **it does not**. That routine is built entirely from legal operations; what the save capability refuses about it is `previewNestedPlan` failing on an unresolvable app name — something no store can evaluate, because it needs a capability execution context. So the store's check is deliberately a *subset* of the capability's, and plain `save` is still the correct door there.

**Keeping the bypass `internal` cost one import and was worth it.** `ProductShellTests` lives in `MacAgentTests`, which imported `MacAgentCore` plainly, so the choice was `public` on the bypass or `@testable` on that one import. `public` would have put a "write a routine the store would refuse" method on the app target's API surface, which is the exact property the ticket exists to remove. A test target reaching in through `@testable` is not the same thing as the app being able to.

**An enum with a payload is the only shape that makes the invariant structural, and hand-writing its `Codable` is part of the cost.** A private setter leaves `Decodable` open; validating in `RoutineStore.save` turns a decoded legacy file into a save failure rather than a repair. The enum closes both — but it changes the persisted shape, so it needs a decoder that reads the old one. Two decisions inside that decoder are worth carrying forward: (1) **the flat `{"state": "…", "reason": "…"}` payload rather than Swift's synthesized `{"pausedBySonny": {"reason": "…"}}`**, because `routines.json` is a file a person occasionally reads while debugging a schedule and because it survives a Swift-side case rename; (2) **the illegal legacy state repairs rather than throws.** Enabled is the fact the scheduler acts on, the reason is the stale half, so the reason is dropped — the same answer `setEnabled(true, now:)` has always given, applied at the last door it can be. A display glitch must never cost a user their routines file. A schedule carrying *neither* shape still throws, exactly as the synthesized decoder did, so the tolerant branch cannot quietly become "default to disabled" for a genuinely damaged file.

**A test that iterates the list it is testing is self-fulfilling, and needs a literal pin beside it.** `savingRejectsEveryForbiddenStepOperationWithTheOperationNamed` loops `StoredRoutine.forbiddenStepOperations`, so deleting an entry from the list would not fail it — it would silently stop testing that entry. Mutation M2 (drop `.openWorkspace`) proves the point: it reddens only `theForbiddenStepListIsExactlyTheSixOperationsRoutinesMayNotContain`, the test that asserts the literal set. Both tests are needed; either alone is a hole.

**The `previewNestedPlan` asymmetry is deliberate and should stay named.** SONNY-24's entry recorded the store/capability split as a defect. Half of it was — the forbidden-operation list, now closed. The other half is structural: the capability can preview a nested plan and the store cannot, so a routine refused for an unresolvable app name will always be writable at the store level. Neither end should read as the complete guarantee, and both now say so in their own doc comments.

Known limitations / deferred scope:
- **The load side is unchanged, per SONNY-52's contract, and it is a recorded non-goal rather than a silent one.** A `routines.json` that already holds a forbidden step still decodes, loads, and executes exactly as before; `loadAll` does not validate, and the legacy-plaintext migration re-writes whatever it decoded. Only the write door is hardened.
- **`setSchedule`, `advanceScheduleBaseline`, `pauseSchedule` and `recordRun` can re-persist such a routine but can never introduce one.** Verified by enumeration, not assumption: across `RoutineStore`'s eleven methods, `steps` is assigned nowhere — the only path by which step content enters is whole-`StoredRoutine` replacement inside `persist(merging:)`, reachable only from `save` and the sanctioned bypass. That is a consequence of the load-side gap above, not a second write door.
- **A `routines.json` written by this build will not load on a build older than this branch.** New encodes write `activation` and neither legacy key, so an older binary's synthesized decoder throws `keyNotFound` on `isEnabled` and the whole file fails to load (surfaced, per the store convention, not silently emptied). Forward migration was contracted; downgrade compatibility was not, and dual-writing both shapes would put two drifting sources of truth on disk. Recorded because it makes rolling *back* past this branch a data-visible event rather than a quiet one.
- **An empty-string legacy `pausedReason` on a disabled schedule still decodes as paused**, with an empty reason, exactly as it rendered before. No writer can produce it; only a hand-edited file can. Left literal rather than special-cased, because mapping it to `disabledByUser` would be an invented behavior change under cover of a migration.

Open questions (required, write "none" if true): none.

Next branch: none pre-assigned. Roadmap row B (`feature/workspace-restriction-scope`, SONNY-36 onward) is in flight in a parallel worktree and absorbs this branch by rebase after it merges, per the disjointness note recorded on both tickets.

### Branch: feature/sonny-49-tasks-collapsible-sections
Status: complete
Date: 2026-08-06
Tickets: SONNY-49 — the Command Center Tasks page's status sections collapse from a disclosure chevron on their headers, and stay collapsed across launches. Spawned no discovery tickets.
Reviewed by: fresh session (per WORKFLOW.md step 7) on PR #31, cycle 1 of 3, anchored at `fc8b66f`. Five findings. **F1** — collapsing "In Progress" hid the running indicator, and with it the app's only cancel control for a plain run; escalated as a product call rather than fixed unilaterally, decided by the user 2026-08-06 (the indicator survives collapse), fixed in this round together with the two claims that described its consequence by subtraction. **F2** — the recorded coverage limitation enumerated one unguarded view line when there are four, two of which break a different acceptance criterion; the enumeration is now complete in the code, this entry, and the ticket. **F3** confirmed the declared limitation is accurate and is explicitly not for fixing. **F4** and **F5** are recorded below, routed no-action by the coordinator. Cycle 3 (the re-check) follows at this branch's new head.

Spec sections covered: none newly. This is a UI affordance on an existing page — no capability, risk tier, approval mapping, planner vocabulary, or stored data model changed. `CompletedTaskRecord`, `TaskHistoryStore`, and `TaskHistoryGrouping` are all untouched.
Files changed:
- `Sources/MacAgent/TaskSectionCollapsePresentation.swift` (new — `TaskSectionCollapseState`, `TaskSectionCollapseStore`, `TaskSectionPresentation`)
- `Sources/MacAgent/CommandCenterView.swift` (Tasks page region only: `TasksFoundationView`, `InProgressTaskGroup`, `TaskHistoryGroupedPanel`, and an optional disclosure on the shared `CommandCenterGroupHeader`)
- `Tests/MacAgentTests/TaskSectionCollapseTests.swift` (new)
- `docs/sonny-founder-design-decisions.md`
- `docs/sonny-v1-implementation-changelog.md`

Tests: `env CLANG_MODULE_CACHE_PATH="$PWD/.build/clang-module-cache" swift test --disable-sandbox -Xswiftc -F -Xswiftc /Library/Developer/CommandLineTools/Library/Developer/Frameworks -Xlinker -rpath -Xlinker /Library/Developer/CommandLineTools/Library/Developer/Frameworks -Xlinker -rpath -Xlinker /Library/Developer/CommandLineTools/Library/Developer/usr/lib` -> exit 0, **513 tests in 50 suites, measured at `7e927e6`**, against a base of **494 tests in 47 suites** re-measured on `main` at `cf99826` — the branch's own +19 tests in 3 suites, unchanged across both rebases. `swift build` -> exit 0 at both ends. (The pre-rebase figures this entry previously carried, 490/48 at `c505be6` over a 471/45 base at `ca4fbe4`, were correct for that tree; the base moved when `feature/automation-store-hardening` merged 23 tests and 2 suites into `main`, and both ends were re-measured rather than arithmetically adjusted. The only commit after `7e927e6` on this branch is this documentation update.)

Behavior added:
- **Every status section header on the Tasks page is a collapse toggle.** In Progress, Done, Failed and Canceled each carry a trailing `chevron.right` that rotates 90° when expanded; clicking anywhere on the header band folds the section's task rows away. The header gains the hover highlight and pointer cursor every other clickable System A row already has, plus a VoiceOver label/value/hint trio ("Done, 12" / "Expanded" / "Collapses this section").
- **The running indicator is exempt from collapse**, per the user's 2026-08-06 decision below. Collapsing "In Progress" folds away that section's task rows — of which it has none today — and leaves the spinner, the command text and Cancel on screen.
- **Collapse state survives quit and relaunch**, per section, through one plain-`UserDefaults` key (`com.sonny.preferences.tasksCollapsedSections`) holding a sorted array of collapsed section identifiers.
- **A collapsed header still shows its full count.** A task completing while "Done" is folded away bumps the number without expanding the section.

Behavior preserved (required, no blanket claims):
- **The Routines page's cadence group headers are visually and structurally unchanged.** `CommandCenterGroupHeader`'s new `disclosure` parameter defaults to `nil`, the Routines call site was not edited, and the `nil` path renders the identical `HStack` with no `Spacer`, no chevron, no `Button` wrapper, and no added modifiers — the layout only becomes width-greedy when a disclosure supplies the `Spacer`.
- **The Tasks page's empty state is untouched.** `TaskHistoryGroupedPanel` still short-circuits to "No completed tasks yet" when there are no records at all, before any section or chevron exists.
- **The 90-day display window and the "In Progress" gating are unchanged.** `TaskHistoryDisplayWindow.withinWindow` still feeds the panel, and the live group still renders only while `isRunning || isAwaitingApproval`.
- **Row behavior is unchanged.** `TaskHistoryRow` still opens `TaskLogDetailDialog` on click via the same `onSelect` closure; only the collection it iterates changed name (`section.visibleRecords`).
- **The running indicator renders on exactly the condition it always did, on all four pages.** Enumerated at this branch's head: four `CommandCenterRunningIndicator(` render sites exist — `InsightsView` (`:913`), the Tasks page's `InProgressTaskGroup` (`:1366`), `RoutinesView` (`:1998`), `WorkspacesView` (`:2188`). Only the Tasks one appears in this branch's diff at all; the other three are byte-identical to `main`. On Tasks the condition is `viewModel.isRunning || viewModel.isAwaitingApproval` and nothing else — the collapse state is not part of it (see the F1 decision below).
- **Approval visibility is unaffected.** `CommandCenterAttentionPanel` sits below the scroll area and self-gates on its own state, so nothing this branch does can reach it; the floating widget is unchanged.
- **The Tasks page's cancel affordance is intact in every collapse state.** `CommandCenterRunningIndicator`'s Cancel button — `cancelCurrentRun()`'s only non-approval call site, enumerated below — renders whenever `viewModel.canCancel` is true, exactly as before this branch.

Architectural decisions / pitfalls discovered (required, write "none" if true):

**Wireframe-fidelity exception, stated rather than silent (per CLAUDE.md's rule).** `9-MainAppHomeScreen.svg` specifies the status groups as plain, non-interactive bands with no collapse affordance. The founder ask (Bhavya, 2026-08-05) is the authority, per the standing rule that founder decisions outrank a literal wireframe reading; the full decision record is in `docs/sonny-founder-design-decisions.md` under "Tasks page — collapsible status sections". The exception is bounded to the smallest possible shape: the chevron is the only addition, it is **trailing** so the wireframe's title/count position and every band measurement stay untouched, and its treatment is copied from the two System A chevrons that already exist (`SonnyType.icon(9, weight: .semibold)`, `SonnyTheme.muted`, trailing-aligned). One practical note for whoever verifies this: **the wireframe SVG exports are not tracked in this repository** — `find . -iname '*.svg'` returns nothing — so this claim rests on the ticket and on the existing in-code wireframe comments, not on a re-read of the file.

**The running indicator is exempt from collapse — user decision, 2026-08-06, PR #31 review F1.** The first implementation gated `CommandCenterRunningIndicator` on the "In Progress" section's `isExpanded`. That was in-contract (the description says *every* status section header gains a chevron) and still wrong, for a reason worth carrying forward: **that indicator holds the app's only stop control for a plain running task.** Enumerated rather than assumed — `cancelCurrentRun()` has exactly three call sites in the whole repository, `CommandCenterView.swift:558` (the indicator's own Cancel), `CommandCenterView.swift:792` (`CommandCenterAttentionPanel`'s Deny) and `FloatingWidgetView.swift:369` (the widget's `onDeny`), and the latter two fire only on an approval. So a run that is merely executing has one cancel affordance, on this page, inside that view. Gating it on a *persisted* preference meant one click could remove it from every future run on the Tasks page, silently and permanently. The decision: the indicator renders on `isRunning || isAwaitingApproval` regardless of collapse; collapsing folds away the section's task rows only. The generalization: **the indicator convention exists so that a running task always shows something — a persisted display preference must never be able to switch that off.** A consequence recorded rather than glossed: the "In Progress" section has no task rows today, so collapsing it now changes nothing on screen beyond the chevron's rotation and the stored preference. The chevron stays because the description contracts one on every status header, and because the state is already the right shape for the day that section gains rows.

**Prose about hidden UI carries the same enumerate-then-subtract duty as a measurement.** Both places that described F1's consequence failed it in the same way: the code comment said collapsing "hides the running indicator, not the fact that something is running", and this entry said it "hides the running indicator and nothing else". Both were true about *view identity* and both misled about *what the user loses*, because neither named the Cancel button and the running command's text living inside the view being hidden. `CLAUDE.md`'s rule is written about claims generally, not about numbers specifically — a sentence that says what is *not* hidden is a subtraction, and it needs the enumeration first.

**Persist the collapsed set, never the expanded set.** The default is "everything expanded," and only a collapsed-set representation makes an absent or empty preference mean exactly that. It matters more here than it looks: `TaskHistoryGrouping.groupedByOutcome` drops empty sections, so a section genuinely does not exist until it has a record, and an expanded-set representation would have to enumerate every section that could ever exist or silently collapse each new one the first time it appeared. The same reasoning is why an unknown identifier in the persisted array is carried harmlessly rather than treated as corruption.

**The collapse preference deliberately does not live on `AgentViewModel`.** The two existing plain-`UserDefaults` preferences (`usePointerCursors`, `displayFullNames`) are there because both the widget and Command Center read them. Nothing outside this one page reads this one, so putting it on the shared view model would add `@Published` state with exactly one observer. `TaskSectionCollapseStore` is injected into `TasksFoundationView` instead, defaulted to `.standard`, which is also what makes the round-trip testable without constructing a whole view model. The rule that actually mattered — plain `UserDefaults`, not `LocalStorageEncryption`, for a cosmetic preference — is followed.

**Seed `@State` in `init`, not in `onAppear`.** `TasksFoundationView.onAppear` already fires on every return to the page (it calls `refreshTaskHistory`), so loading the preference there would re-read defaults on every tab switch and, if a write ever lagged, discard an in-session collapse. `State(initialValue: collapseStore.load())` in a custom `init` seeds once per view identity and leaves the existing `TasksFoundationView(viewModel:)` call site unchanged.

**`count` is a property of the section, not of what got rendered.** The count-while-collapsed requirement is one line away from being wrong — `section.visibleRecords.count` compiles and reads naturally and silently shows 0 on every collapsed section. Putting `count` on `TaskSectionPresentation` as `section.records.count`, independent of `isExpanded`, is what makes that mistake a test failure instead of a manual-QA discovery. Mutating it to follow the collapse state reddens 3 tests.

**Mutation battery, re-run in full and measured at `7e927e6`** — one SHA, one test population, after both rebases. Twelve mutations of `TaskSectionCollapsePresentation.swift`, each run against the full flagged 513-test suite, each red (exit 1). Distinct test functions reddened, in brackets: invert `isExpanded` [13], `toggle` to a no-op [9], invert `setExpanded` [10], `load` ignoring the stored value [3], `save` writing nothing [3], `save` dropping `.sorted()` [1], a different `defaultsKey` string [1], `count` following the collapse state [3], collapse no longer hiding rows [3], `sections(for:collapse:)` ignoring the collapse state [3], `inProgressSectionID` drifting from the header title it renders [3], `visibleRecords` reversing the grouping's order [1].

**Why the battery was re-run rather than carried forward, and why the first count reads 13 and not the 12 this entry used to state.** Two separate SHA-anchoring effects, both worth knowing. The [12] was measured when the test file held 18 `@Test` functions; `anExpandedSectionHandsBackTheGroupingsRecordsInOrder` was added afterwards and also reddens under an inverted `isExpanded`, so the same mutation over the finished 19-test file is [13]. Nothing regressed — a new pin reddening an old mutation is the pin working, exactly the effect the SONNY-24 entry documents. Separately, the rebase onto `cf99826` brought real code (23 tests, 2 suites) into the suite the battery runs against, so the old counts described a population that no longer exists. They *should* have been unaffected — `feature/automation-store-hardening` touched `RoutineSchedule`/`AutomationStores` and nothing that references `TaskSectionCollapse*` — but "should have been" is an assertion, and the whole point of the rule is that a count measured against a moved tree cannot be told apart from a stale one. Re-running twelve mutations is cheap; asserting invariance is not evidence.

Known limitations / deferred scope:
- **The view wiring is not test-pinned, only the presentation type is — four lines, across two different acceptance criteria, and every one of them fails silently.** The presentation type is pinned hard (twelve mutations, all red). Nothing pins the view that calls it, because this repository has no view-rendering tests and no view-inspection dependency. The complete enumeration, each confirmed green-under-mutation by PR #31's cycle-1 reviewer at `fc8b66f`, with line numbers at this branch's head:
  1. `State(initialValue: collapseStore.load())` (`CommandCenterView.swift:404`) — seed a fresh `TaskSectionCollapseState()` instead and the preference is never *read back*.
  2. `collapseStore.save(collapseState)` (`:506`) — drop it and the preference is never *written*. (1) and (2) each break **"collapse state persists across launches"** on their own, which is a *different* criterion from the count passthrough this paragraph originally discussed — the reason the enumeration had to be extended rather than reworded.
  3. `count: section.count` (`:1412`) — swap it for `section.visibleRecords.count` and every collapsed section reads 0, breaking **"a collapsed header still shows its count"**.
  4. `disclosure:` on that same header (`:1413`) — pass `nil` and Done / Failed / Canceled lose the chevron and stop being clickable at all, removing the feature from three of the four sections.
  Two further view-level facts are unpinnable for the same reason and belong in the same list: that the header's button actually calls `onToggleSection`, that the chevron actually rotates, and — new this round — **that the running indicator survives collapse (F1)**, which is a view-level `if` and cannot be pinned by any assertion on the presentation type: a test that the type ignores the collapse state would be tautological, and would not stop a future edit from re-wrapping the indicator in `if isExpanded`. Inventing that test would have made coverage look better without making it better, so it was not written.
  What guards all of this is SONNY-49's manual-test checklist, now four items: item 1 covers (4), item 2 covers (1) and (2), item 3 covers (3), and the new item 4 covers F1. The same enumeration is in the code, on `TasksFoundationView.collapseState`, where an editor will actually meet it. Adding a view-inspection dependency would pin them and is deliberately not done: that is an architectural decision reserved for the user, not a ticket-level one. Recorded so a green suite is never mistaken for full coverage of the feature.
- **Two verified-inaccurate sentences are still in the tree, routed no-action by the coordinator at cycle 2** — recorded here rather than dropped, since a finding that is neither fixed nor written down is a silent backlog. **F4:** both this entry and `docs/sonny-founder-design-decisions.md` say the new chevron reuses the *color* of the two pre-existing System A chevrons; that is true of `CommandCenterView.swift:155`, which sets `SonnyTheme.muted` explicitly, and not of `:282`, which sets no color and inherits `sidebarNavText` from its enclosing `HStack` when enabled. Size, weight and trailing placement are shared by both; only "color" overreaches, and the shipped implementation is unaffected either way. **F5:** `TaskSectionCollapsePresentation.swift:62` justifies `object(forKey:) as? [String]` over `stringArray(forKey:)` "for the same reason the preference rule bans `.bool(forKey:)`" — a false analogy, because `.bool(forKey:)` coerces a missing key to `false` while `stringArray(forKey:)` returns `nil` for anything that is not an array of strings, exactly like the cast. The reviewer proved it: substituting `stringArray` leaves the suite green. The accessor is right; only the reason given is wrong. Both are one-line edits whenever the user wants them.
- **No "collapse all" / "expand all" affordance.** Not asked for; each section is toggled on its own.
- **Collapse state is not shared with the floating widget** — the widget has no status sections, so there is nothing to share.

Open questions (required, write "none" if true): none.

Next branch: none assigned. This was a disjoint, founder-triaged UI improvement running beside roadmap row B (`feature/workspace-restriction-scope`, SONNY-36 → SONNY-44 → SONNY-37) and `feature/automation-store-hardening` (SONNY-52). **It started from `main` at `ca4fbe4` and has been rebased twice**, per step 3's merge-one-at-a-time rule: onto `feb27c7` when `feature/workflow-process-amendments` merged mid-implementation, then onto `cf99826` when `feature/automation-store-hardening` merged during the PR #31 review. Both rebases conflicted at exactly one place — this changelog's shared append point — resolved both times by keeping both entries in merge order. Neither conflicted under `Sources/` or `Tests/`: the first branch touched no code at all, and the second's only `CommandCenterView.swift` edit is in `RoutineRowPresentation` (`isPausedBySonny`, now line 1846), over 400 lines below this branch's last hunk, which ends at 1423. The full suite was re-measured at both ends after each rebase, and every measurement in this entry carries the SHA it was taken at.

### Branch: feature/workspace-restriction-scope
Status: complete
Date: 2026-08-07
Tickets: **SONNY-36** — the `WorkspaceScope` model and evaluator, pure core, plus `WorkspaceStore.save` becoming merge-preserving for `fileLocations`; no wiring. **SONNY-44** — a workspace may list an app `MacAppCatalog` cannot open, for scope membership only (scope-only status is *derived*, not stored — no schema change). **SONNY-37** — the verdict threaded into `AgentActionExecutor.assessRisk`, with `CapabilityRiskAssessment.scopeVerdict` as row C's typed input. **SONNY-38** — the running task bound to a workspace, and the escalation reason restyled off the error colour. **SONNY-39** — "New task in this workspace" from the workspace card, and the pending-arm lifecycle that makes it safe. **SONNY-40** — the `edit_workspace` capability, tier 2 to add and tier 3 to remove, with a distinct consent when a removal empties a dimension; absorbs SONNY-43's `teamType` merge fix. **SONNY-41** — the workspace detail sheet, the surface that makes a boundary something the user can look at. **SONNY-42** — this entry and the spec amendment. Discovery tickets spawned and left untriaged: SONNY-55, SONNY-56, SONNY-62, SONNY-64, SONNY-65, SONNY-66, plus a row-C origin-signal input recorded on SONNY-13. *SONNY-55 (`ActionPreview` is built by every capability adapter and rendered by nothing) and SONNY-56 (a Command-Center-originated run's result summary renders on no surface) were added by the PR #32 review, cycle 1, F7 — both were spawned by SONNY-44's review work on this branch, both are `backlog`/untriaged, and the omission originated in SONNY-42's closing comment and propagated here verbatim.*

**Correction (PR #32 review, cycle 1, F1).** The row above originally read `559 tests / 46 suites`. Both halves were wrong and the row is the exact failure this table's own header exists to prevent. The tree at `f4ee0c1` **cannot run the suite at all**: `Tests/MacAgentCoreTests/AutomationStoresTests.swift` is missing the brace `769f545` later restored, so `swift test` fails to compile there while `swift build` passes — which is why the figure was never re-measured. The number itself was SONNY-37's **pre-rebase** figure, restated as current one row above the sentence that says pre-rebase figures are deliberately not restated; it is struck here rather than moved. What the SHA can support is a `@Test`-declaration count, which matches the runtime total exactly at every SHA in this table that *can* run: **601 `@Test` declarations at `f4ee0c1`**. And `46 suites` was impossible on an additive branch whose base measures 50.

**Twelve consecutive commits on this branch cannot compile the test suite** (`1b7c6f0` through `4e45c0c`; `769f545` onward parse): the same lost brace F1 describes. `swift build` passes at all twelve, so the break is invisible to a build check. Consequence for a future session: no per-commit suite figure in that range is reproducible, and `git bisect` on any test failure is impossible across 12 of the branch's 28 commits — bisect from `769f545` forward, or from the base.

Pre-rebase figures preserved on their tickets for provenance and deliberately not restated as current: SONNY-36 448/45 -> 503/46 (`bf9b8d8`), SONNY-37 542/46 -> 559/46 (`dea6b5f`), SONNY-38 559/46 -> 569/46 (`d8d9b2b`), SONNY-39 602/49 (`eaa0fc7`) -> 610/49 (`aa57781`). *SHAs added by the PR #32 review, cycle 1, F11 — the figures were cited by ticket alone, against the repo's rule that every measurement carries the SHA it was taken at. All five are non-ancestors of this head.* Zero tests were removed or weakened anywhere on the branch; every ticket's review verified this by per-commit `@Test` accounting over `Tests/`, not by the totals.

Behavior added:
- **A workspace's contents are its restriction scope.** `WorkspaceScope` canonicalizes a stored workspace once into app keys, web domains and file roots, and answers a four-valued `ScopeVerdict` per resource. Entries it cannot match — a folder outside `PathWhitelist`, a URL `SafeURL` rejects, a blank app name — become `inertEntries` and are reported rather than dropped.
- **Out-of-scope work escalates to tier 3 and names what it found.** `WorkspaceScopeEvaluator` walks the resolved plan, `PlanScopedResources` classifies what each step touches, and the escalation reason names both the resource and the workspace. Nothing blocks; the user can always allow.
- **A task is bound to a workspace for its lifetime and no longer.** Resolved once after the plan exists and before the first assessment, held across an approval or clarification pause, cleared at every terminal state, and surfaced as a visible, clearable chip.
- **"New task in this workspace" from the workspace card**, arming a binding the next composer dispatch consumes.
- **An `edit_workspace` capability** — add or remove apps, safe URLs, and Desktop/Documents folders — tier 2 to add, tier 3 to remove, with a *different* consent when a removal empties a dimension, because "removing `~/Documents/ClientAlpha`" and "this workspace will no longer restrict file locations at all" are different things to agree to.
- **A workspace detail sheet** showing name, solo/team, task count and all three scope dimensions with every stored entry verbatim; empty dimensions read as *not restricted* with a per-kind sentence; inert entries render visibly as not in effect, carrying the evaluator's own reason; each Remove control states the real removal unit when other rows leave with it.
- **A workspace may list an app the launch catalog cannot open** (SONNY-44), for scope membership only — which is what lets Microsoft Word be inside a workspace's boundary at all.

Behavior preserved (required, no blanket claims):
- **An unscoped task assesses exactly as it did before this branch.** `AgentRunner`'s scope parameter is `.unscoped` *at every call site that means unscoped* — **it has no default, by design** (*corrected by the PR #32 review, cycle 1, F6: this sentence originally said the parameter "defaults to `.unscoped`", which is the opposite of the property the same entry praises below. `grep -rn "scope: TaskWorkspaceScope *=" Sources/` returns nothing; `AgentRunner.swift`'s own comment ends "A default is what makes that failure silent, so there isn't one." A future session must not rely on a default that does not exist*), and `neitherUnscopedNorAnUnconstrainedKindChangesTheAssessment` pins the *whole* assessment — tier, escalations and requirement — not just the tier.
- **A workspace that configures one kind and not another is unchanged on the kind it never configured.** `.unconstrained` produces no escalation and is never relaxation-eligible.
- **Scheduled/unattended runs pass `.unscoped` explicitly**, with the reason at the call site: a stored routine can never name a workspace, there is no user-typed command, and no dispatch named one. The unattended gate's fixed `.approved(.tier2)` is untouched, so nothing this branch mints as tier 3 can run unattended.
- **`open_workspace` still opens exactly what it opened.** Its own plan-derived binding means opening a workspace raises no scope prompt against itself; `theExistingOpenActionIsUnchangedByTheNewCardDispatch` pins the one-click open through the injected seams, with no approval and the card arm dead.
- **`create_workspace` is behaviourally unchanged apart from no longer destroying fields it was not told about** — `WorkspaceStore.save` now merge-preserves `teamType` alongside `fileLocations` (SONNY-43), so "create a workspace called X with Safari" no longer silently demotes a team workspace to solo.
- **`UnattendedTrustAdvisory`'s behaviour is edited not at all** — its entire diff is one call line, one comment and one test argument; its tier-3 threshold, its `try?` and its nil results are byte-identical, and every one of its tests passes unchanged.
- **The evaluator's matching semantics were never re-derived anywhere.** `PlanScopedResources.swift` and `WorkspaceScope.swift` carry zero diff across SONNY-37's commits; `edit_workspace` reuses `WorkspaceScope.appKey`, `WorkspaceScope.normalizedHost` and `PathWhitelist.canonicalURL` rather than copying them, and SONNY-41's sheet asks `verdict(for:)` rather than reaching for keys that are not visible outside `MacAgentCore`.
- **The Command Center card grid, the Tasks region, the Insights workspace breakdown, the widget's own layout and both theme files are untouched by SONNY-41**, verified by diff at each review rather than asserted.
- **Approval copy for every non-scope escalation is unchanged in wording**; only its colour moved off the error token, and both approval surfaces already agreed on the replacement.

Architectural decisions / pitfalls discovered (required, write "none" if true):

**Escalation reasons are questions, not errors — everywhere.** Founder decision (user, 2026-08-06), taken on a two-part question put before any styling was written. The widget's escalation-reasons line moved from `WidgetTheme.errorGlyph` (#FF7474) to `WidgetTheme.secondaryCircular` (**#FF9230**, System B amber), the counterpart to Command Center's `SonnyTheme.warning`. The principle is broader than the pixel: **no escalation is an error** — each one explains why approval is being asked — so "Zip output already exists at …" was wrongly red too, and the token sits on the shared line rather than being conditioned on reason kind. The requirement "must be visually distinguishable from Sonny's error states" was made *checkable* rather than asserted, by enumerating first and subtracting after: `errorGlyph` had exactly three uses, of which the escalation line was the only non-error one; afterwards it is used exclusively by genuine error states, so the property is true by construction of the palette. **The two token systems are not mixed:** Command Center's panel already used `SonnyTheme.warning` (#F2BE00) and was correctly left with zero diff, because a System B token on a System A surface is the mistake this rule exists to prevent.

**The two-notions-of-empty class — three appearances on one branch, and the generalisable rule.** This is the single most valuable thing in this entry. Each time, a second, locally-obvious notion of "empty" was written beside the evaluator's, agreed with it in the common case, and diverged exactly where an inert entry exists:
1. **SONNY-40, first shipped state.** `removalEmptiesTheKind` counted raw surviving strings, so a removal taking a kind's last *working* entry while an inert one survived handed the approver the milder entry-naming consent and un-restricted the dimension anyway — precisely the failure the two-reason requirement exists to prevent.
2. **SONNY-40, cycle 2.** With emptiness fixed, the *escalation trigger* still diffed raw stored strings, so removing an entry that never restricted anything asked for tier-3 approval on a loss that could not occur.
3. **SONNY-41.** The detail sheet derived restricted/not-restricted from `entries.isEmpty` — reintroducing, under a different name and one commit later, the predicate SONNY-40 had just removed, on the surface whose entire job is telling the user whether a dimension restricts. Worse here than there, and inverted from the failure the ticket was written against: the user reads a dimension as restricting when nothing is, and the sheet withholds the one sentence that would say otherwise.
**The rule: ask the evaluator; never re-derive its answer.** `WorkspaceScope` reports `.unconstrained` whenever its *canonical* list is empty, and its own doc comment says that includes the all-inert case. **The reason this keeps recurring is that the wrong version is always the shorter expression** — `after.isEmpty` and `entries.isEmpty` both read naturally and both compile, and a suite whose every fixture has an unrestricted dimension that is *also* an empty one can never tell the two sources apart. The fixture that separates them is a workspace whose three lists are all non-empty and one of which is entirely inert; SONNY-41's `theFootnoteFollowsTheEvaluatorEvenWhenNoListIsEmpty` is that fixture, and it exists only because a mutation battery found the gap after the headline fix had already landed.

**Adding a field to a lifecycle whose clear sites are enumerated in several places, twice in two tickets.** SONNY-38's review filed F2 against `clearInMemoryLocalDataState` for `explicitWorkspaceBinding`; SONNY-39 then added `pendingWorkspaceBinding` and repeated it exactly (F4). The shape: *a new field joins a lifecycle whose clear sites are enumerated in four places, and nothing forces the enumeration to be revisited.* Both are fixed and pinned, and the enumeration is now written out in the function rather than trusted — but the class is the record, not the two instances. **A new binding-like field is not done when it is set and read; it is done when every clear site has been re-enumerated by hand.** The same shape produced SONNY-39's R1 (deleting a workspace left an arm naming it alive, so the chip promised a boundary the run would not apply) — a third instance, reached through a different function.

**The pending-arm lifecycle contract.** A pending card binding **binds exactly one task: the next composer dispatch.** It used to be consumed by *any* `start()`, which meant an armed-but-never-submitted chip was inherited by the next row action — arm "Drafting", get distracted, click Open on "Research", and Research opened under Drafting's boundary and raised a scope prompt naming a workspace the user never mentioned. Now: a composer dispatch consumes it, **every other dispatch kills it**, and either way it dies at the dispatch so it can never reach a second task. `fromComposer: Bool = false` is the safe default **by design, not by convention** — a call site added later that says nothing drops the arm rather than silently scoping itself with it. An explicit `workspaceBinding:` argument still wins over both, keeping the card-beats-free-text precedence a single slot.

**H1 — voice and typed dispatch must be gated by the same terms.** The typed composer is `.disabled(isTaskInFlight)`, which includes a pending clarification; `canUseVoice` had no clarification term. That asymmetry was omission, not a decision anyone made, and it was reachable: during a clarification pause the chip shows the *paused* task's workspace while a live arm is invisible, the card's "New task" button is enabled (both `isRunning` and `isAwaitingApproval` are false), and a voice dispatch then consumes the invisible arm — measured, with the task running scoped to a workspace the chip never named, and the paused task's unanswered clarification silently discarded in the same step. **Ruling: voice gains the clarification term, restoring parity.** The dispatch refuses too, not from belt-and-braces habit but because a transcription already in flight when a clarification arrives would otherwise still land — the guard at the dispatch makes the guarantee independent of that timing. Recorded alternative for a future ticket: voice *answering* a clarification is a feature, and building the gate first does not foreclose it. **The auto-collapse ruling stands, but its safety basis was corrected**: the original argument rested on "an invisible live arm can bind nothing the user does not aim at the composer while seeing the chip," which H1 falsified. The ruling survives on the corrected gates instead — push-to-talk calls `requestWidgetPresentation()` before `beginPushToTalkVoice()`, and `isCollapsible` is false whenever voice is active, so the widget cannot auto-collapse between the hotkey and the consume. No collapse-timer hook was needed; the collapse was never the hole.

**Never-touch lists are read for intent, not letter — three ratifications on this branch.** All three follow one shape: **stated openly rather than slipped in, bounded to the smallest checkable change, ratified by the user, and recorded.** (1) SONNY-37's signature propagation into `UnattendedTrustAdvisory.swift` — a non-defaulted `scope:` parameter refuses to be silent by design, and that file is an executor call site; the discharge was one call line plus one test argument, with `.unscoped` being the semantically correct value rather than merely the compiling one. (2) SONNY-31's copy amendment, the precedent. (3) SONNY-40's one exhaustive-switch arm in `AgentActivityPresentation.swift` (`case .editWorkspace: return "Edit workspace"`) — the branch cannot compile without it and the only alternative is a `default:` clause the conventions forbid for exactly the silent-swallowing reason. **The pattern to reuse: when a never-touch list collides with a compiler requirement or a non-defaulted parameter, the answer is a narrow, checkable discharge condition put to the user — not a quiet edit, and not a `default:`.**

**Stated wireframe exception (SONNY-41).** `13-MainAppWorkspaces.svg` is a card grid only; **no wireframe exists for a workspace detail surface.** The sheet is net-new UI in **System A** — flat, opaque, Inter, zero shadows — and deliberately does *not* inherit the routine-detail view's System-B-inside-System-A treatment, because **liquid glass was tried for workspace surfaces and explicitly reverted as too distracting from card content.** Structurally it follows `TaskLogDetailDialog`, the System A dialog already in that file. Recorded here as a reasoned exception per CLAUDE.md's rule, not silent drift; the card grid behind it is visually unchanged.

**Process pitfall: a mutation battery reverts `Sources/` with `git checkout`, so it must only ever run against a committed tree.** Running one over uncommitted work during SONNY-40 **destroyed a round of fixes that then had to be redone.** The battery is worth its cost — it found the two-notions-of-empty class, the `restricts(.webDomain)` blind spot, SONNY-41's task-count pluralisation and the badge-write boundary gap, none of which reading found — but its revert step is indiscriminate. Commit first, every time.

**Entry identity is not the evaluator's resource matching.** `verdict(for:)` answers "does this resource fall inside the boundary" — folder *containment* and a dot-suffix host match. That is the wrong question for "which stored entry does this removal name": under it, "remove `~/Documents/ClientAlpha`" would delete a stored `~/Documents`. Removal matching is *equality* of the same canonical forms, reused rather than re-derived. URLs deliberately carry two keys: removal matches by **host** (so a removal that left the host in scope would change no boundary at all), while addition dedupes by the whole URL (two pages on one host legitimately both belong). SONNY-41's sheet, which cannot see those key functions, recovers the same equality by asking the evaluator **twice, symmetrically** — a one-way probe would group a parent folder with its child, and symmetry turns those deliberately asymmetric rules back into equality.

**Removals are derived from the outcome, never from the request.** A request-derived list would call "remove Safari, add Safari" a loss, escalate for nothing, and make the answer depend on whether removals or additions applied first. What the user loses is a property of the outcome, so it is read from the outcome.

**A plan may edit one workspace only once.** `AgentRunner` gates once up front and `executeChain` never re-gates, so a second edit of the same workspace is assessed against a store the first has not yet written to — two "one of several" consents could between them empty a dimension with neither saying so. The rule lives in `resolveDefaultOutputs`, the one hook handed the whole plan; a per-segment count structurally cannot see the shape it needs to reject. Editing two *different* workspaces is allowed, because they share no record and each assessment is correct in isolation.

**`edit_workspace` is forbidden inside routines.** The capability's own new operation opened the hole: removal escalates to tier 3, which an unattended run structurally cannot satisfy, but *addition* stays tier 2, which it can — so a scheduled routine could have widened a workspace's restriction scope with nobody present. `PlanScopedResources` refuses a `default:` clause precisely so a new operation cannot land unclassified, but a `Set` has no such guard.

Known limitations / deferred scope:
- **No relaxation ships here, by design.** In-scope tier 2 auto-running and in-scope tier 3 dropping to lightweight confirmation are row C's. Row B computes the verdict, stores it on the assessment, and **uses it only to escalate.** Row C inherits a typed input plus the two constraints now written into spec §11.2.
- **SONNY-40 residual R1 (records, low).** `removalEmptiesTheKind`'s doc comment argues its own equivalence proof over *canonical* lists while the conjunct it retired was phrased over `removed`, a list derived through `entryKey` — a different function that can collide across the inert/valid boundary. The conclusion (reduce to two terms) is right; only the stated reason reasons over the wrong domain. A measured counterexample is on the ticket.
- **SONNY-40 residual R2 (pre-existing, low, unreachable through the product).** The same collision is a real escalation gap — a removal that empties a kind can run at tier 2 when an inert stored entry's `entryKey` collides with the removed working entry's. Identical at the previous head, so this round neither introduced nor widened it, and it needs a hand-edited or pre-validation store file to reach.
- **SONNY-41 residual: the inert-sibling shared-key note gap (bounded, low).** The sheet's symmetric probe gates on `verdict(for:)`, which is `.unconstrained` for a scope built from an inert entry, so two inert entries never group; the adapter's removal key gates on neither the whitelist nor `SafeURL`, so two inert entries that canonicalise alike *do* share one. Removing either takes both while the note names nothing. **The direction is under-report, never over-report** — the note can never name an entry that survives — it is confined to rows that now visibly read "Not in effect", so no boundary can be lost, and reaching it needs a hand-edited store with two near-duplicate inert entries. Recorded rather than fixed.
- **SONNY-62 — the tier-equal drift, and row C's input.** An approval granted for one tier-3 reason silently covers a *different* tier-3 reason arising before execution: the stale-approval guard re-assesses fresh but compares tiers only, and two different tier-3 causes compare equal. Observed in a real run during SONNY-38's manual pass — the user consented to a scope exception and the run then overwrote a file that had appeared in the meantime, without the replace escalation ever surfacing. **Pre-existing** (the guard predates row B, and `AgentRunner` gating is on this branch's never-touch lists) but **materially more reachable now**, because out-of-scope escalations mint tier-3 prompts routinely where before they were rare. Filed untriaged, and flagged as row C input either way.
- **Planner-prompt routine rule (pre-existing, deliberately not widened).** The system prompt's routine rule names `save_routine`, `run_routine`, `clarify` and `unsupported` as forbidden inside `routineSteps`; it already omitted `create_workspace` and `open_workspace` before this branch and now omits `edit_workspace` too. Enforcement is at the store and the save capability, so this is a prompt-quality gap rather than a hole. Not widened here on purpose: correcting it would edit a golden-pinned prompt for a problem this branch did not create.
- **Two `edit_workspace` steps separated by a step of another operation still segment apart.** The pure case is refused outright, which is the reachable shape. Not fixed because the only two routes are an executor special-case the contract forbids or per-segment re-gating the conventions forbid; it is a pre-existing property of gate-once assessment that `create_workspace` and `save_routine` share.
- **SONNY-43 stays open as its own tracking ticket and closes at merge, citing `795a19a`**, per its 2026-08-06 ratification note.
- **Product tickets the manual passes generated, all untriaged and none touching this branch:** SONNY-64 (direct picker UX plus pre-built-plan dispatch, removing the composer round-trip), SONNY-65 (app icons beside names in the sheet's app rows), SONNY-66 (a mechanism decision for expanding the app catalog beyond the hardcoded twelve), and a row-C origin-signal input recorded on SONNY-13 — with the guard rails already attached to it: the origin signal must be unforgeable from the planner side (an enum set only by UI call sites, the shape SONNY-39's `fromComposer` already uses), `effectiveTier` stays honest, and the unattended path is unaffected.
- **View-level rendering remains unpinnable**, as everywhere in this repo — no SwiftUI view-inspection harness. All presentation logic was moved into pure tested functions; what the view *renders* is covered by each ticket's manual items, all of which the user executed.

Open questions (required, write "none" if true):
- **Should a scheduled run's unattended pre-check assess with the run's real scope?** Flagged by SONNY-37 and deliberately not resolved: answering "yes" would make `UnattendedTrustAdvisory`'s pre-check disagree with its own docstring, which already warns that a clean result is not a promise the routine will run. Not live in any current contract; belongs to whoever owns the unattended path. **Cost amended in place 2026-08-13 (SONNY-100, founder-ratified as part of that ticket — the question stays open; only its weight is corrected):** after row C, answering "yes" has a second, much larger consequence that was not on the table when this was parked — it would make in-scope tier-2 steps auto-run unattended, without the per-routine trust opt-in, by removing one of the two independent call-site choices (`source: .instantResolver`, `scope: .unscoped`) that keep the unattended path outside both relaxation grants (row C's I6, a pinned property with a named test, not a type-level guarantee).
- **Should adding to scope deserve more than a lightweight confirmation once relaxation exists?** — **Answered by row C (SONNY-98), 2026-08-13.** The reasoning as raised, kept: row B rejected escalating on *widening* on a row-B cost argument — the user typed the command asking for it — and once relaxation is live, an ungated tier-2 edit that adds `~/Documents` converts a large share of the filesystem into relaxation-eligible territory in one un-escalated step; Gate 1 answers *which verdicts qualify*, not whether creating a qualifying verdict should itself be gated. The answer: the origin grant never covers a boundary-changing edit, and adding a `PathWhitelist` root itself escalates to tier 3 with a consequence-naming reason. See `docs/sonny-founder-design-decisions.md` → "Approval relaxation (roadmap row C)" and the row-C branch entry below.

Next branch: `feature/approval-relaxation-structural` (row C), per the roadmap above — now unblocked, since its stated justification was restriction scoping existing first. It inherits `CapabilityRiskAssessment.scopeVerdict` as a typed input rather than re-deriving scope, the three constraints recorded on row C's roadmap line and now in spec §11.2, and the two open questions above.

Architectural decisions / pitfalls discovered (required, write "none" if true):

**A records backfill is claims-writing at its most error-prone, and the adversarial pre-landing pass caught two live errors.** A draft attributed PR #26's "Reconciling the two comments above" comment to the reviewer; it is the fixing session's — its own text claims authorship of the F12–F19 fix comment ("My own comment landed 14 seconds after the re-check's"), and only the later "Final reviewer confirmation" comment is the reviewer's. A second draft claimed "the PR thread carries no review content" for PR #29, whose body carries a dated post-open review note. Both were corrected before the edit landed. The underlying condition: Plane's shared API identity makes every session's comment the same actor, so reviewer-vs-implementer attribution rests entirely on each comment's stated content — read it, don't infer it.

**An object existing in the repo is not the same as it being history.** SONNY-12's comments cite fix commits `bdbb979`/`13d51c2`; both resolve via `git cat-file`, yet neither is an ancestor of `6af453c` — the branch re-hashed at merge, and the merged equivalents are `4acf91a`/`82f741a` (identical titles, checked with `git merge-base --is-ancestor`). A durable record cites the SHAs a future `git log` can reach and notes the mapping; resolvability alone would have passed a naive check and left the record citing unreachable history.

**A ticket's proposed replacement string is itself a claim to check.** SONNY-60's description proposed "57 test functions" while its own Evidence section said "57 enclosing functions"; the PR #30 cycle-3 comment caught the drift before it could be copied verbatim into repo policy — the same class of failure the edited rule exists to prevent, one hop from becoming its next example.

**A disjointness constraint written for mid-flight editing expires when both branches freeze.** The triage note assigned all changelog EOF appends to the parallel SONNY-54 lane, so this branch opened PR #33 without its entry — a correct read of that instruction, recorded as a stated exception on SONNY-63's closing comment and the PR body. The coordinator's amendment (SONNY-60, 2026-08-07) reversed it: with both PRs open and frozen, an EOF append costs at most one keep-both rebase, and step 7's letter plus the PR #29 precedent (a docs-only process branch with a full entry) stand. The fix was amending the instruction, not ratifying the deviation — the deviation's paper trail stays as honest history of a correct read of a wrong instruction.

Known limitations / deferred scope:
- **The four backfilled fields compress multi-comment review arcs into one field each** — the full arcs (fix comments, re-checks, reconciliations, dispositions) stay on SONNY-8, SONNY-10, SONNY-12 and SONNY-57; each field names where its record lives.
- **This branch's own Reviewed-by field above is a placeholder at write time**, the same class its SONNY-61 work just eliminated from four older entries; the light pass launching against this head is the replacement's source, per the PR #30 R1 bar the field itself cites.

Tickets: SONNY-54 (per-routine trust covers manual runs — a trusted routine invoked by hand carries the same `.approved(.tier2)` decision its scheduled runs already carry, killing the every-run tier-2 prompt; untrusted routines and tier-3+ behavior unchanged)
Reviewed by: fresh session (PR #34, cycle 1 at `6e26fe2`, findings on the ticket at 23:48Z) — no HIGH findings; the relaxation verified exactly as narrow as contracted. Spec sections covered: none newly — this widens branch 10's per-routine unattended-trust opt-in (spec §10/§11's tier definitions, the approval gate, and the tier-3+ backstop are all untouched; the never-touch list on SONNY-54 pins them).
Files changed:
- `Sources/MacAgent/AgentViewModel.swift`
- `Sources/MacAgent/RoutineDetailView.swift`
- `Sources/MacAgentCore/RoutineSchedule.swift` (doc comment only — this is the schedule *model*, not `RoutineScheduler.swift`, which has zero diff)
- `Sources/MacAgentCore/UnattendedTrustAdvisory.swift` (copy + doc comment only)
- `Tests/MacAgentCoreTests/UnattendedTrustAdvisoryTests.swift`
- `Tests/MacAgentTests/ScheduledRoutineRunTests.swift`
Tests: the exact flagged command from CLAUDE.md -> pass, **710 tests / 56 suites, exit 0, measured at `e6b0012`** (the branch's code head; baseline on `main` at `6af453c` measured by this session as 704 / 56, exit 0). Six tests added, zero removed or weakened. *Cycle-2 fix round (PR #34 review, tests/docs only, zero `Sources/` diff): **711 / 56, exit 0, measured at `c8c461d`** — one test added (the F4 disabled-schedule pin), two strengthened (F1's clean-pause count pin, F3's task-history surface); the docs commits atop it change no code.*

Behavior added:
- **A trusted routine runs by hand with no tier-2 prompt.** `performStart` derives a trust decision from the prepared plan and, when `.approved(.tier2)` covers the assessed tier, executes without pausing — same decision value, same gate, same `activeTaskScope` the assessment used (never a hardcoded `.unscoped`; the scheduled path's is a different, documented fact).
- **The toggle states its real scope.** Label is the founder-chosen wording verbatim (2026-08-06, Q5): "Trust this routine — runs tier-2 steps without asking, scheduled or manual." Caption: "Applies both to scheduled runs and runs you start yourself. Steps that need your explicit approval still ask first." The tier-3+ advisory now also says a manual run still asks.
- **A disabled schedule leaves the trust grant covering manual runs — intended.** *(Coordinator ruling, PR #34 review cycle 2, F4, 2026-08-07: the review found this case unnamed anywhere.)* Trust is the routine's standing consent; disabling the schedule suspends *runs*, not consent — consistent with the label's "scheduled or manual". `manualRoutineTrustDecision` reads `unattendedTrusted` only, never `isEnabled`/`activation`, and that is now pinned in both directions by `aDisabledScheduleLeavesTheTrustGrantCoveringManualRuns`: disabled + trusted auto-runs; disabled + trust revoked prompts.

Behavior preserved (required, no blanket claims):
- **Untrusted routines' manual runs are byte-identical.** The pause block moved into an `else` unedited (verified by diff), and `anUntrustedRoutineStillPromptsWhenRunByHandAndApprovingRunsIt` pins prompt-then-approve-then-runs end to end. A routine with no schedule has no trust grant and still prompts (`aRoutineWithoutAScheduleStillPromptsWhenRunByHand`, plus the pre-existing ProductShellTests pin, which passes unchanged).
- **Tier-3+ on the manual path prompts exactly as before, and does not touch the schedule.** `aTrustedTierThreeRoutineStillPromptsWhenRunByHand` pins the escalated prompt at `.tier3`, the un-run tier-3 action, the un-paused schedule (SONNY-31's pause is the answer to "nobody is present"; somebody is), and the approve-then-run completion.
- **The scheduled path is untouched entirely.** `performScheduledRun`, `checkScheduledRoutines`, `RoutineScheduler.swift`, the unattended backstop and SONNY-31's pause behavior all carry zero diff; every pre-existing ScheduledRoutineRunTests test passes unchanged.
- **`AgentRunner.swift` gating and tier definitions: zero diff.**
- **The ordinary non-routine auto-run path is byte-identical**, including its failure behavior on execute-time drift — the new drift catch is guarded `where routineTrustApproval != .notRequested`, so only an execution that actually carried the trust decision can re-arm.
- **A trusted manual run is an ordinary manual task in every bookkeeping respect**: reports through `finalSummary` (never `scheduledRunNotice`), advances no streak history and no catch-up baseline, and leaves the first-approval education state (`hasCompletedFirstApproval`) untouched — all pinned in `aTrustedRoutineRunByHandSkipsTheTierTwoPrompt`.

Architectural decisions / pitfalls discovered (required, write "none" if true):

**The trust decision is derived from the plan, through the store's own lookup — never from a call site.** `runRoutineWidget` holds the real `StoredRoutine` and deliberately throws it away (it round-trips through the same text command a user would type), and the planner path only ever holds a `routineName` string. So `manualRoutineTrustDecision(for:)` re-derives the routine from the plan's single `run_routine` step via `routineStore.routine(named:)` — the identical normalized (case/diacritic-insensitive) lookup `RunRoutineCapabilityAdapter.routineRunSpec` performs at execute time, so the routine the check reads and the routine that runs cannot resolve differently. Every unresolvable case — unknown name, nameless step, unreadable store — fails closed to `.notRequested`: a trust check that cannot complete relaxes nothing.

**The grant covers exactly the canonical single-step run-routine plan.** A planner-built plan wrapping `run_routine` beside any other step keeps the full prompt: those steps were never what the user marked trusted. The planner is not injectable at the view-model level, so this shape rule is pinned by calling the (internal-not-private, for exactly this reason) decision function directly.

**The view-model tier comparison is a courtesy pre-check; the enforcement is `AgentRunner`'s structural gate — and the mutation battery proved it.** Mutant M4 (comparison always-true) left all 92 ScheduledRoutineRunTests+ProductShellTests green *with correct user-visible behavior*: the trusted tier-3 run proceeded into `execute`, the gate threw, and the new drift catch re-armed the prompt. Recorded deliberately as backstop evidence, not as a test-quality gap — the comparison exists so the common tier-3-at-assessment case pauses cleanly before steps are marked running, not because anything relies on it for safety. *Cycle-2 fix round (PR #34 review, F1): M4 is now killed — the tier-3 test pins exactly one "Approval required" log event, and the mutant's gate-throw-plus-re-arm path logs it twice (`count → 2) == 1` failure at the pin, exit 1, demonstrated at `c8c461d`). The split above still stands — user-visible behavior under M4 was correct, verified by the reviewer two ways — but the divergence is now test-observable rather than survivable.*

**The drift catch is deterministically unreachable in-process, and its body is proven by the mutant, not by a test.** Between `performStart`'s assessment and `execute`'s re-assessment there is no main-actor suspension point, so no test can interleave a store rewrite there (ProductShellTests' re-arm test exploits the *approval pause* window, which the trusted path removes). The window is real for external state (a file appearing on disk mid-flight), so the catch stays; M4's run exercised its entire body on the real path.

**Trust remains schedule-scoped — a deliberate consequence of "no new persisted state."** `unattendedTrusted` lives on `RoutineSchedule` (a persisted coding key; the name predates the widening and keeps its old spelling), the toggle renders only when a schedule exists, and `setRoutineUnattendedTrust` no-ops without one. A never-scheduled routine therefore cannot be trusted and always prompts.

**Mutation-battery pattern hygiene: count before you mutate.** M1's first pattern (`routine.schedule?.unattendedTrusted == true else {`) matched twice — the helper *and* `checkScheduledRoutines`' scheduled-path guard — and would have mutated the never-touch scheduled gate; the mutate script's occurrence-count guard refused it, and the re-anchored pattern killed cleanly (17 issues across both suites). Battery: M1, M2, M3, M7, M9 (AgentViewModel) and M8 (advisory copy) all killed by named tests; M4 survived as designed at `e6b0012` (killed since cycle 2 — see the note above). M5 and M6 are absent from the numbering by design, not omission: M5 (deleting the drift catch) was designed and dropped because no in-process test can reach the catch — there is no main-actor suspension point between the assessment and `execute`'s re-assessment for a test to interleave into — and M6 (inverting the trust-field read) was dropped as behaviorally bracketed by the M1/M2 pair, which mutate the same condition from both directions. Run against the committed tree at `e6b0012`, reverted per mutant.

Known limitations / deferred scope:
- **A routine without a schedule cannot be marked trusted** (above). Nobody has asked for it; if it becomes real, it is a data-model decision (trust on `StoredRoutine` itself) with its own consent-surface work, not a quick widening.
- **`UnattendedTrustAdvisory.swift`'s copy amendment is recorded here openly**: one clause ("still ask when you run it yourself") plus its header comment, copy-only, in a file precedent-sensitive from SONNY-31/37's ratified amendments but *not* on SONNY-54's own never-touch list; the behavior (thresholds, `try?`, nil results) is byte-identical and its tests pin the new clause.
- **The scheduled skip-notice's "Turn on unattended running for it" pointer was considered and left.** It never quoted the old label either, and the new label's "without asking" phrasing aligns at least as well; judged not a wart worth a ticket.

Open questions (required, write "none" if true): none.

Next branch: per the pre-assigned post-B sequence recorded on SONNY-54 (coordinator, 2026-08-07): SONNY-58, on the branch its own description names, after this branch merges.

Files changed:
- `Sources/MacAgentCore/AgentPlan.swift` (two new optional `AgentStep` fields: `resolvedAppName`, `resolvedBundleIdentifier` — synthesized Codable tolerates their absence, so every stored plan and prior-task record decodes unchanged)
- `Sources/MacAgentCore/RunningAppSwitchCapabilityAdapter.swift` (the pin-once `resolveDefaultOutputs` hook; pinned-aware `app(in:)`)
