Behavior added:
- **The verdict is about the app that will actually be switched to.** The adapter's new `resolveDefaultOutputs` hook runs in the resolve phase all three executor gates (`prepare`, `assessRisk`, `execute`) pass through first, resolves `RunningAppMatcher.bestMatch` exactly once, and pins the winner's display name and bundle identifier into the step. Classification reads the pin as `ScopedResource.resolvedApp`, matched bundle-identifier-first with a name fallback that deliberately never catalog-resolves — an app that merely *calls itself* "Chrome" cannot spend the cataloged Chrome's membership.
- **Execution activates the pin or fails naming it.** A pinned step is looked up by exact bundle identifier; if the app quit, execution throws `noMatchingRunningApp` with the pinned name — it never re-fuzzy-matches the query onto something the assessment never scored.
- **The consent surfaces name the resolved app.** Escalation reasons and the approval's involved-resource line carry the resolved display name ("Xcode is not part of the Writing workspace."), never the query string and never a bundle identifier.

Behavior preserved (required, no blanket claims):
- **An unpinned step classifies, previews, and executes exactly as before.** The raw-name classification branch is byte-identical, the adapter's `bestMatch` path survives for direct callers, and the classification-table test plus `switchRunningAppFallsBackToTheSearchQueryWhenNoAppNameIsGiven` pass unchanged — the fail-closed direction: a query that never resolved earns nothing a resolved app would have to spend.
- **The unresolvable-query failure surface is identical**: the same `noMatchingRunningApp(query)` the adapter's preview used to throw from inside `prepare`, thrown by the same `prepare` call, now from the resolve phase — earlier and once, with no pin left behind.
- **Every other operation's resolve phase is untouched** — the new block is additive, shaped exactly like the seven existing ones *(count corrected by the PR #35 review, cycle 1, F2: originally "six"; `git show c46bf89:Sources/MacAgentCore/AgentActionExecutor.swift | grep -c '.resolveDefaultOutputs(in: resolvedPlan'` → 7 — zip, docx, write-markdown, web-to-markdown, local-draft, shortcut, edit-workspace — the count-first-then-write rule applied after the fact)*.
- **Tier defaults, `AgentRunner` gating, the evaluator's web/file matching, `.app` matching for every other operation, and the instant resolver's plan construction: zero diff.**
- **The pre-existing adapter test passes unchanged** (`runningAppSwitchUsesTierOneRunnerPathWithoutCatalogAllowlist`): the partial query "Not" still previews and activates Notion — now via the pin rather than a second resolution.
- **One deliberate verdict-flip class, in both directions, and it is the ticket's point**: a query that catalog-resolves to a listed app while the running match is a different app flips false-`.inScope` → truthful-`.outOfScope` (new prompt, correct); a partial query resolving to the listed running app flips false-`.outOfScope` → truthful-`.inScope` (a prompt disappears — a *correction of a false escalation*, not a relaxation mechanism: row B still relaxes nothing on `.inScope`, and the flip is earned by binding the verdict to the real resource, never by loosening a comparison).

Architectural decisions / pitfalls discovered (required, write "none" if true):

**Pin once, first resolver wins — and the idempotency is load-bearing, not hygiene.** The resolve phase runs at every gate, so a hook that re-resolved would re-open the exact assessment-versus-execution divergence the pin closes, at the gate boundary where it is least visible. The pin-once guard is what makes "the resolution the approval classified" and "the identity execution activates" the same value by construction. The contract's required definition of behavior-when-resolution-would-change falls out: it cannot change — the pin is immutable after `prepare`; the one residual (the pinned app quitting mid-run) fails closed with the pinned app's name, pinned by `executionActivatesThePinOrFailsClosedWhenThePinnedAppQuit` with a deliberately tempting decoy (an app that would best-match the query) running.

**The anti-imposter rule: a resolved app's name half never catalog-resolves.** `verdict(for: .resolvedApp)` matches `bundle:` first and falls back to `nameFallbackKey(displayName)` only — running `appKey` on the display name would catalog-resolve "Chrome" to the real Chrome's bundle key and hand any app using that display name the workspace membership the user granted to the cataloged app. The name fallback therefore only ever matches stored entries the catalog itself could not resolve, for which name identity is all anyone has. `appKey`'s two constructors were split into shared `bundleKey`/`nameFallbackKey` helpers so the key literals exist once — a drifted prefix would silently unmatch every stored entry of that half.

**`switch_running_app` is structurally single-sourced, which is what makes the pin total.** *(Amended 2026-08-09 by the `feature/planner-vocabulary-routing` branch, at PR #39's cycle-1 review, F5 — the sanctioned in-place pattern this paragraph already uses below. **Two of the three clauses that follow are no longer true.** SONNY-68 removed the operation from `plannerVisibleCases`, so it is in the planner's schema and is no longer constructed only by the instant resolver; the decode-layer block on the pin fields is untouched and still holds, and so does "routines cannot carry it through any product path" — but now because `StoredRoutine.forbiddenStepOperations` refuses it rather than because the planner has no word for it. The pin is still total, for a reason this paragraph did not have to state: a planner-emitted step arrives unpinned and is pinned once at the resolve phase like any other, and the routine ban is what keeps the nested per-gate path — the one with no shared prepared plan — out of reach. See that branch's entry at this file's end.)* The operation is excluded from the planner's JSON schema (`AgentOperation.plannerVisibleCases`), blocked again at the decode layer (`AgentPlanDecoder.stepKeys` omits both pin fields, enforced recursively through `routineSteps`), and constructed only by the instant resolver, single-step; routines cannot carry it through any product path. The recorded corner — a store-tampered routine carrying one (the `RoutineStore.save`-validates-nothing class, PR #28 F5) — has **two variants, and the entry originally enumerated only the milder one** *(completed by the PR #35 review, cycle 1, F1 — the repo's own enumerate-before-you-subtract rule, applied to this paragraph)*: (a) an **unpinned** tampered step resolves per nested gate independently, with no shared prepared plan to pin through; (b) a tampered step with the pin fields **pre-set** is the opposite — the pin-once guard honors the stored pin identically and confidently at every gate, so no cross-gate divergence ever surfaces it. Sharpest sub-case: with a workspace listing an uncataloged app entry (a `name:` key), a tampered `resolvedAppName` matching that entry plus a foreign `resolvedBundleIdentifier` earns `.inScope` silently, renders the display name on the consent line, and activates the other app by exact lookup. The class is unchanged: tamper-only, behind the encrypted store, the same recorded PR #28 F5 class — pre-SONNY-58 tampering could already lie via `appName`, so reachability is not regressed; the new fields make the tampered lie cleaner (the attacker picks the activated bundle id directly, and the consent name and activated app split perfectly). Hardening (strip or reject pre-set pins at routine-store load) is deliberately not this branch's scope — filed as a discovery ticket per step 5, coordinator-adjudicated.

**The truthful-flip constraint, stated for row C.** This ticket makes verdicts more truthful, never more permissive: both flip directions above are the same correction (bind the verdict to the resolved resource), and the disappearing-prompt direction is justified *only* by that binding. Row C consumes `.inScope` as relaxation input; what this branch guarantees it is that a `switch_running_app` `.inScope` was earned by the app execution will actually activate.

**Mutation battery: 8 designed, 8 killed, against the committed tree at `ffaeeb7`, reverted per mutant** (filter: the two touched suites, 69 tests; every mutant compiled and ran the full pair, exit 1). M1 classifier-ignores-pin → killed by the headline, partial-query, and classification tests; M2 executor-never-pins → killed by four tests including both execution pins; M3 pin-once-idempotency-dropped → killed by the re-resolve and quit tests; M4 execution-falls-back-to-fuzzy-rematch → killed by the quit test, whose running decoy (an app that would best-match the query) exists for exactly that mutant; M5 name-half-catalog-resolves → killed by the imposter test alone, likewise its purpose-built killer; M6 bundle-half-dropped → killed by the bundle-first and partial-query tests; M7 value-renders-bundle-identifier → killed by the rendering and headline tests; M8 approval-line-ignores-pin → killed by the headline's approval-copy assertion.

Known limitations / deferred scope:
- **The unpinned classification and execution paths remain**, reachable only by direct adapter/classifier callers (tests) and the tampered-routine corner above — both keep pre-SONNY-58 behavior, which is the fail-closed direction.
- **The pin freezes resolution at prepare time by design.** A user who approves a tier-3 escalation and takes long enough that the pinned app quits gets a failure naming that app rather than a fresh match — decided over re-resolving, because a silent re-match is the bug this ticket exists to remove.

Open questions (required, write "none" if true): none.

Next branch: per the pre-assigned post-B sequence recorded on SONNY-54 (coordinator, 2026-08-07): SONNY-59, on the branch its own description names, after this branch merges.

Architectural decisions / pitfalls discovered (required, write "none" if true):

**The substance is the plan document, not here.** `docs/sonny-v1-vision-planner-integration-plan.md` §E1–E13 is the durable ratified-decision record; §A–D are the analysis and the option menu behind each choice. What follows is only what a changelog reader needs to decide whether to go read it, plus the pitfalls a future session would otherwise re-derive.

**The mandate (founder, on SONNY-69, 2026-08-08).** The experiment graduates from throwaway spike to v1-integration input; reshaping the roadmap in its service is intended, not incidental. Two constraints not relaxed: the spike code never merges as-is, and product vision actions route *through* the risk/approval engine, not around it. Ratified refinement of the merge question (E13): the spike is a reference to be *reworked* into real, engine-routed, test-covered code — built after the cleanup sequence below merges — never merged as-is; the founder expects heavy rework ("it was never good enough").

**The thirteen ratified decisions, compressed (full text: plan §E).** Production A/B for the planner, OpenAI default until a benchmark passes (E1); the open-weights planner lands behind a formalized provider-agnostic router, not a second hardcoded `performStart` branch (E2); the vision loop is one capability (E3) that pauses mid-session only on consequential actions — a lightweight confirmation for anything affecting another person, explicit approval for anything destructive (E4); vision is auto-*proposed* when the planner falls short — no separate "enable vision" step, but one up-front approval of the envelope before anything fires, so D5(a)'s trigger with D5(b)'s consent rather than either option as written (E5); made safe by per-app vision-control consent with terminals never controllable (E6, which also resolves SONNY-66's mechanism as per-app consent); unattended vision never (E7); the roadmap splits — screen-*seeing* + transparency infra pulled forward, screen-*acting* stays as rescoped Power Mode (E8); a two-mode model — low-friction Normal (post-hoc sent-data log, no pre-send screenshot) and a global Safe mode (asks before every task, shows every screenshot pre-send), with the "data leaves device" label relocated to Safe mode only (E9, reshaping SONNY-32); auto-blur secrets before sending and never obey on-screen instruction text (E9); screenshots to Cerebras no-retention only, never Google's free tier (E10); a benchmark for both models gating any default change, its two halves owned separately by SONNY-70 (vision) and SONNY-72 (planner) (E11); cleanup-first sequencing (E12); the spike reworked-not-merged and last (E13).

**The safety-critical unknown, named so it is not a surprise later.** E4's "pause only on consequential actions" requires the vision model to recognize, *before* it clicks, that a button sends a message or deletes something — a runtime judgment that can be wrong both ways (miss a real Send, or over-nag). Its accuracy is the single number **SONNY-70** — the vision half of E11's benchmark: screenshot-reading + action-coordinate accuracy over a labeled offline fixture set — must establish; "feels accurate" is not a basis for shipping this gate. SONNY-70 is therefore a hard gate on the whole **action** half, not a metric to collect afterward: no vision-action ships until its numbers clear. **E11's two halves have separate owners and neither absorbs the other** (corrected by PR #37's review, F3 — this entry originally called SONNY-70 a "two-model" benchmark and hung the planner-default gate on it too). SONNY-70 is deliberately vision-only; "no multi-model leaderboard" is its own non-goal, and it produces no plan-quality number, so it structurally cannot discharge E1's "never flip the default to Cerebras without proof." That gate is **SONNY-72** (filed by the fix round, Backlog, untriaged): plan-quality parity, structured-output rejection rate with a live nullable-union re-check, end-to-end latency, and the reliability envelope — plan §A5's four numbers, seeded by SONNY-69's own never-run acceptance criteria, and the place §D11's unanswered "who runs it, against which command set?" now gets settled. One further thing a session inheriting either ticket must know: **SONNY-70 was filed as throwaway experiment tooling** ("never merges to main, no PR, no changelog entry"), so treating its numbers as a v1 ship gate promotes its role beyond its written contract — recorded on SONNY-70 itself by the fix round rather than left implicit here.

**Two conscious spec deviations, recorded for the inheriting branches.** (1) **§14.4A** mandates a pre-send content preview for full-screen captures and any tier-2+ action; E9's Normal mode ships tier-2+ vision screenshots with only a post-hoc log (which still meets §14.5's baseline — §14.5 is a timing-agnostic content checklist that delegates the *when* outward, so the post-hoc log still owes every item on it), moving the pre-send preview into the new global Safe mode. §6.13's own timing paragraph states the same rule by cross-reference, so it is folded in here rather than counted separately. (2) **§11.3** (User-Facing Approval Copy, spec `:1671`) makes "Whether data leaves the device" one of five *mandatory* lines on every approval surface, and `RiskApprovalCopy.lines` (`RiskApproval.swift:119-127`) renders exactly those five, `Data leaves device: yes/no` among them; E9 removes that line from all normal surfaces and shows it only inside Safe mode, while E4 keeps Normal mode's tier-2 confirmations and tier-3 approvals — so a §11.3 mandate comes off live approval surfaces. That is the reason SONNY-32's job changes from fixing the label's honesty to removing it from normal surfaces. **This deviation was originally filed here against §6.13, which never contained the line — §6.13's ten items are inspector contents; the mandate is §11.3's. Corrected by PR #37's review (F2), in this entry and in plan §B6/§E9.** Both are the founder's calls, recorded as conscious amendments, not slips. Separately (not counted among the two, and not a violation): §16.5's resolved provider list (OpenAI + Anthropic, BYOK skipped) is *extended* by a third first-party provider — Cerebras-served open-weights gpt-oss-120b — behind the same router; it is not BYOK and does not reopen §7.9. Also not counted, and flagged rather than resolved: §16.5 places the router in the hosted-backend milestone (row 12) with credentials server-side, so E2's client-side stand-in is an open placement tension the plan carries in §A2 and §C's row-12 disposition, not a ratified deviation.

**Read the experiment diff at its real merge-base, not against current `main`.** The experiment branch (`experiment/cerebras-gpt-oss-vision`, `85fa63d`…`58e8cf5`) forked at `6af453c` (Merge PR #32), before SONNY-54/58 and the SONNY-60/61/63 docs batch merged. `git diff 99f2fd2..58e8cf5` shows 17 phantom files and 935 phantom deletions that are pure stale-`main` noise (every one of the 17 enumerated and blob-hash-compared by the light pass; all byte-identical between `6af453c` and `58e8cf5`). The true diff is `git diff 6af453c..58e8cf5` = 8 files, 1231+/6−; the experiment removed no routine-trust or scope logic. Any future read of it must anchor at `6af453c`.

**The same stale fork makes line-number citations SHA-sensitive in both directions, and the review tripped on it.** "Byte-identical between `6af453c` and `58e8cf5`" is exactly *not* "byte-identical with `99f2fd2`" — a phantom file is unchanged by the experiment while `main` moved underneath it, so the same construct sits at different line numbers in the two trees. `AgentPlan.swift`'s planner-visible-`enum` is `:408` at `99f2fd2` and `:394` at `58e8cf5`. A verifying agent read that file at the spike SHA while checking a §A1 claim anchored at `99f2fd2`, reported the correct citation as wrong, and the finding survived an adversarial refutation round because the refuter re-ran the same check at the same wrong SHA — independence does not help when both passes inherit the same unstated assumption. Caught only by re-running the check at both SHAs before applying the edit, which had already been made and had to be reverted. Two rules follow: a claim about `main` is verified at `main`'s SHA and a claim about the spike at the spike's, never whichever tree is checked out; and §A1 now names its anchor SHA in the text so the ambiguity cannot recur. This is the mirror image of the merge-base pitfall above — same root cause, opposite direction.

**The engine already has every containment primitive the reworked loop needs; none has been exercised by a perception-action loop.** The single `AgentRunner.execute` gate, the tier/requirement model, `ScopeVerdict.opaque` (the class for resources unnameable before execution), the resolve-phase pin discipline (SONNY-58's bundle-id pin — whose anti-imposter rule and single-sourcing guarantee any new vision pin must replicate, see SONNY-67), and the stale-approval re-arm are all live and daily-exercised. The reworked loop *extends* the engine (a per-envelope gate plus an engine-owned, tested in-loop pause on the consequential-action class), never bypasses it — the spike's zero-test, stdout-only loop is the negative example that constraint exists to replace. Full grounding: plan §B and the risk-engine research it cites.
