Accepted consequence, explicitly: selection-driven zips/scans escalate in apps-configured workspaces that do not list Finder."

Behavior preserved (required, no blanket claims):
- **A step that names its own folder is unchanged.** With no `contextSource`, all four operations classify exactly as before — pinned by asserting the plain form of every one of the four as the selection-driven expectation *minus Finder*, so the two forms cannot drift into agreement.
- **`contextSource` on any other operation still changes nothing.** Only these four cases read it; the 31-case table's probe step sets the field for every operation and the other 27 rows are byte-identical.
- **Nothing became opaque.** A selection-driven step still reports `isOpaque == false`: its folder is a named, knowable resource by assessment time, and marking it opaque would make every plan containing a selection-driven zip permanently non-relaxable — the outcome SONNY-45 explicitly rules out ("not 'make these opaque'").
- **No relaxation, in either direction.** The change only ever adds a resource, so the plan verdict can only move toward `.outOfScope`; a workspace that *does* list Finder keeps the same verdict, tier and (empty) escalation list it had before, pinned by its own counter-test.
- **`get_finder_selection`, `reveal_in_finder` and `open_generated_artifact` have zero diff** — including the Launch-Services carve-out, which this change deliberately does not touch (see below).

Architectural decisions / pitfalls discovered (required, write "none" if true):

**The ticket's stated trigger cannot be observed at the moment it would be needed, and implementing it literally would have shipped a dead branch.** SONNY-59 and SONNY-45 both describe the condition as "`contextSource == .finderSelection` **and no input path was supplied**", adding that the classifier knows both at assessment time. It does not. The only production path into `PlanScopedResources.classification` is `AgentActionExecutor.assessRisk` -> `scopeFindings` -> `WorkspaceScopeEvaluator.evaluate`, and `assessRisk`'s first statement is `resolveDefaultOutputs`, which runs `FinderSelectionResolver.pinningSelectedDirectoryInput` and writes the resolved folder into `inputPath` on every matching step. `selectedDirectoryPath` either returns a path or throws, so there is no third outcome that reaches classification with `inputPath` still empty — a populated `inputPath` is evidence the selection **was** read, not evidence it was not. The two-condition form is therefore unfalsifiable-looking and inert: it fires on no real plan while passing every classifier test written from an unresolved step, because such tests construct steps the resolve phase never touched. The classifier keys on `contextSource` alone; the residual over-report (a step reaching classification with both a real `inputPath` and `contextSource`, where the resolver returns the supplied path and never talks to Finder) is the direction this file already takes for Word, and its reachable form is the cross-step pooled shape bounded under "Known limitations" below — *not* a per-step planner violation, which is what the first version of this entry wrongly claimed excluded it. **This is recorded as the pitfall rather than the fix because the general lesson is the trap, not this instance: a classifier condition written against the plan as the planner emits it, evaluated against the plan as the gates see it, is a branch that reads as coverage and is not.** The measurement is in the mutation battery below — the literal reading is mutant M4, and the executor-level test reports it as `escalations → []`, `effectiveTier → .tier2`, `scopeVerdict → .inScope`.

**`convert_docx_to_pdf` is in scope for this ticket even though its context paragraph names only three operations.** The founder decision authorizes "the selection-driven paths", not an operation list, and `DocxConversionCapabilityAdapter` pins `[.scanDocx, .convertDocxToPDF]` and falls back to `convertStep?.contextSource` in its own `spec` — so a convert step is a genuine selection reader, including alone, since a plan carrying only that operation still routes to that adapter through `AgentActionExecutor.workflow(for:)`. Enumerating before subtracting is what surfaced it: the fourth operation is invisible if the three named in the ticket are taken as the population. Note this is a different resource from SONNY-45 item 2's "RELAX on `convert_docx_to_pdf`", which is about Word's conditionality and remains untouched.

**The `open_generated_artifact` warning three cases below is about a different site, and this change is on the other side of its own rule.** That comment (SONNY-37's F4 discharge, landed in SONNY-40) records that the death of the unlistability argument is *not* licence to report the Launch-Services handler. The rule that survives there is drives-versus-resolves: an app the capability drives by name is a resource; an app the system resolves for a file already inside the boundary is not. `FinderSelectionResolver` drives Finder by name over Apple Events, which is the reported side of that exact rule — the same rule the branch-B plan states at `docs/sonny-branch-b-plan.md:204` while its own resource table (lines 220-221) lists scan/zip/docx as file-only. That table is the stale artifact this branch corrects in code; it is left unedited because branch B's plan is a frozen historical planning document, and this entry is the durable record of the correction.

**Mutation battery: 5 designed, 5 killed, against the committed tree at `02732dc`, reverted per mutant** (the full flagged suite per mutant, each reporting `Test run with 727 tests in 56 suites failed`; the restored tree re-ran green at 727 / 56, exit 0). M1 Finder dropped from the scan/zip/docx case → killed by 5 tests, 15 issues, including the executor escalation test and the 31-case table; M2 Finder dropped from `convert_docx_to_pdf` only → killed by 2 tests (the table and the four-operation classifier test), proving the fourth operation is covered independently rather than by association; M3 Finder reported unconditionally → killed by the plain-form half of the classifier test and by the mixed-plan attribution test (`["scan", "zip"]` vs `["scan"]`), which is what stops the fix from escalating every explicitly-named folder; M4 the ticket's literal two-condition reading → killed by 4 tests, 16 issues, the executor-level test showing the dead branch directly; M5 selection-driven steps additionally marked opaque → killed by 5 tests, including the counter-test's `.inScope` roll-up and the table's opacity assertion. **M5's issue count is construction-dependent and two measurements exist**: 5 tests / **9 issues** as run by this session at `02732dc`, which marked only the `.scanSelectLargestFiles, .createZip, .scanDocx` case opaque, and 5 tests / **11 issues** as reproduced by PR #38's cycle-1 review at `4adf94a` (docs-only delta, so the same code tree), which marked the `.convertDocxToPDF` case opaque as well and therefore tripped two extra table assertions. Same five killing tests, same kill; the delta is the mutant, not the coverage. Recorded rather than reconciled to one number because an issue count without its mutant text is not reproducible — the tests killed are the durable measurement.

Known limitations / deferred scope:
- **The over-report is accepted, not fixed — and its true class is cross-step, not the per-step shape first recorded here.** Any step reaching classification with both `contextSource` and a non-empty `inputPath` names Finder even when `selectedDirectoryPath` returned the supplied path and never contacted Finder. The reachable form is produced by pooling, not by a malformed step: `pinningSelectedDirectoryInput` takes `primary` from the first non-empty `inputPath` among the plan's matching steps and `contextSource` from the first non-nil among them, *independently*, so a scan carrying an explicit path with no `contextSource` beside a zip carrying `contextSource` with no path resolves from the scan's path with zero Finder contact, back-fills it into the zip, and the zip is reported as driving Finder. Both steps individually satisfy `OpenAIPlanner.swift:162`. **The first version of this entry bounded the residual with "a plan shape the planner is instructed not to produce" — that bound was unsound**, because per-step planner compliance says nothing about a cross-step pool; PR #38's cycle-1 review executed the shape (`finderReads=0`, tier 3, `.outOfScope`) and the correction is recorded here rather than quietly replaced. What genuinely bounds the cost is direction: always an extra escalation, never a silent blessing or an under-report, so no workspace boundary can be widened by it. Exactness belongs to the resolver, the only place that knows whether Finder was contacted — filed as **SONNY-73** (Backlog, untriaged), which also has to decide whether clearing `contextSource` or pinning a provenance marker (SONNY-58's pattern) is right, since the second changes what an *unresolved* plan classifies as.
- **Attribution in a mixed plan is per declaring step, not per plan.** `pinningSelectedDirectoryInput` reads the selection once for the whole plan and pins every matching step, so in a plan where only one step carries `contextSource` that step is the one naming Finder. Where the selection really was read, this is attribution only — the plan-level roll-up is a maximum, so the verdict is identical and only the finding's `stepID` differs from a plan-scoped attribution. **It is not attribution only in the pooled shape above**: there the escalation has no Finder read behind it at all, which is SONNY-73's subject, not this one's. Pinned deliberately by `aMixedSelectionDrivenPlanNamesFinderOnceOnTheStepThatDeclaresIt`.
- **Prepending Finder can displace a later resource from the approval message, and that is pre-existing design, not a regression.** `scopeEscalations` dedupes in first-seen order and truncates at `prefix(3)` (`AgentActionExecutor.swift:433-439`), so on a plan with four or more distinct out-of-scope resources a previously-named one can now fall outside the list. No verdict and no tier changes — only which three reasons the prompt spells out — and the cap is deliberate, documented at its own definition, and already covered by `repeatedAndSurplusOutOfScopeResourcesAreDeduplicatedAndCapped`. Recorded so the preserved-behavior list above is exact; no action taken. (Raised as L3 by PR #38's cycle-1 review, which requested no change.)
- **`docs/sonny-branch-b-plan.md`'s resource table (lines 220-221) now under-describes the shipped classifier.** Left as-is per the frozen-historical-document rule above; this entry is the correction of record.

Open questions (required, write "none" if true): none.

Next branch: per E12's ratified cleanup sequence, the planner-vocabulary/routing bugs (SONNY-68 + SONNY-48), then SONNY-64, then the bug cluster (SONNY-28/30/34/35) — all existing tickets, for the user to assign. Row C planning (SONNY-13) is unblocked by this branch merging.

### Branch: feature/planner-vocabulary-routing
Status: complete
Date: 2026-08-09
Tickets: **SONNY-68** (a switch phrasing carrying a workspace clause routed to `edit_workspace` — an app-switch command became a boundary edit nobody typed; fixed at both ends, the resolver's and the planner's) and **SONNY-48** (the core permitted a snippet step inside a routine that no product path could author, because the planner had no snippet vocabulary; direction A, the planner learns `save_snippet`). Second item of E12, the ratified pre-vision cleanup sequence (`docs/sonny-v1-vision-planner-integration-plan.md` §E12), and the work its §A4 calls "fix the vocabulary architecture, not port its holes". Neither ticket carried a `Branch:` line, so this session chose one branch for the pair and recorded the choice on SONNY-68 at claim: the two are one subject — which operations the planner may name — and SONNY-48's fix lands inside the pin SONNY-68 adds. One discovery ticket filed: **SONNY-74** (Backlog, untriaged) — the prompt's `routineSteps` exclusion sentence names five of the eight operations the core rejects.
Reviewed by: fresh session (per WORKFLOW.md step 7) on PR #39 — **cycle 1 at `de0ca95`, verdict NOT clean: one BLOCKER, two MAJORs and five MINORs, every blocking finding in the resolver half and all three sharing one root cause.** The reviewer re-measured both ends independently (727 / 56 at `48d0150`, 744 / 58 at `de0ca95`, exit 0 each), reconciled the +17 by test-function name (25 added `func` lines − 7 helpers − 1 rename, none parameterised), re-implemented the whole 16-mutant battery from its descriptions in a separate detached worktree and reproduced 16/16 killed plus N1 surviving plus M5's first form confirmed a genuine no-op, and built a probe harness that tried and failed to break the one-recognizer-two-consumers invariant across both word orders, multi-character folds (`Straße`, the `ﬁ` ligature, Turkish `İ`), names containing the noun, tie-breaks, boundary negatives and the remainder-versus-full-command asymmetry. **F1 (BLOCKER)** — the unconditional article trim ran ahead of `looksLikeRunningAppName` and disabled two of its eight leading stop words on that path, so nine measured phrasings that reached the planner at base were captured as doomed app queries, the worst of them workspace-opening asks; the code comment asserting the opposite was false for exactly those two words, and the new prompt rule closed the same intent at the planner end. **F2 (MAJOR)** — "switch to the Notes app" returned `nil` at base and was asserted inside a test named for unchanged behaviour. **F3 (MAJOR)** — the clause rejoin was not punctuation-aware, so a typed full stop produced `appName "code ."`; pre-existing in kind for bare queries, newly reached by clause-carrying ones. **F4–F8 (MINOR)** — a false "the prompt's rules otherwise follow registration order" universal (23 of 24 do; `create_local_draft` at `:158` precedes `open_generated_artifact` at `:159`), the SONNY-58 entry's now-false structural claim, a re-cased fixture, six coverage gaps, and naming/visibility leftovers. **All eight fixed in cycle 2** at `eda5e74`; a full cycle-3 re-check follows, since the round touches production code.

Spec sections covered: none newly. This makes an existing capability reachable by the phrasings users actually use (§4A's instant commands and §9's planner seam) and makes an existing core permission collectable; no tier, no gate, no approval surface changed.
Files changed:
- `Sources/MacAgentCore/InstantCommandResolver.swift` (`runningAppCandidate(from:)` and `edgePunctuationTrimmed(_:)`, called by `prefixedRunningAppQuery` before the plausibility guard; the ordering contract stated on the former)
- `Sources/MacAgentCore/WorkspaceTaskTagging.swift` (`workspaceClause(in:workspaceStore:)` and its `WorkspaceClause` result — internal, not public, since nothing outside the module consumes them — the second clause word order, the `FoldedText` index map, a literal-noun pre-check before the store read; `freeTextWorkspaceName` now delegates)
- `Sources/MacAgentCore/AgentPlan.swift` (`.switchRunningApp` and `.saveSnippet` out of `plannerVisibleCases`' exclusion list, the rule that list now follows recorded on it, three schema field descriptions extended — `searchQuery` and `draftContent` for `save_snippet`, `appName` for `switch_running_app`)
- `Sources/MacAgentCore/RunningAppSwitchCapabilityAdapter.swift`, `Sources/MacAgentCore/SnippetSaveCapabilityAdapter.swift` (a real `AgentTool` each)
- `Sources/MacAgentCore/SnippetExpansionCapabilityAdapter.swift` (the comment recording that its empty `plannerTools` is a decision)
- `Sources/MacAgentCore/OpenAIPlanner.swift` (two new rule lines at their adapters' registration positions, `switch_running_app` added to the `routineSteps` exclusion sentence)
- `Sources/MacAgentCore/AutomationStores.swift` (`.switchRunningApp` in `forbiddenStepOperations`, and the paragraph distinguishing what a routine may contain from what a routine can be authored to contain)
- `Sources/MacAgent/AgentViewModel.swift` (one doc-comment line: `resolveTaskScope` described only the old clause order)
- `Tests/MacAgentCoreTests/SwitchInWorkspaceRoutingTests.swift` (new, 13 tests including the base-parity table), `Tests/MacAgentCoreTests/SnippetRoutineAuthoringTests.swift` (new, 3 tests), `Tests/MacAgentCoreTests/PlannerBoundaryTests.swift` (+2 tests, both goldens, one schema assertion inverted twice), `Tests/MacAgentCoreTests/WorkspaceTaskTaggingTests.swift` (+6 tests), `Tests/MacAgentCoreTests/ToolRegistryTests.swift`, `Tests/MacAgentCoreTests/AutomationStoresTests.swift`
Tests: the exact flagged command from CLAUDE.md -> pass. Baseline on `main` at `48d0150`: **727 tests / 56 suites, exit 0**. At `461326e` (SONNY-68): **741 / 57, exit 0**. At `d1917a3` (SONNY-48): **744 / 58, exit 0**. At `eda5e74` (cycle-2 fix round, branch head): **751 tests / 58 suites, exit 0**. Twenty-four tests added net; **zero removed and zero weakened** — `git diff 48d0150..eda5e74 -- 'Tests/*' | grep '^-' | grep -v '^---'` returns exactly six lines at every one of those heads: one renamed test signature, one golden prompt sentence, and the four `!contains(…)` assertions that became positives for the two operations that gained vocabulary. (The cycle-2 round renamed one of this branch's own tests and moved assertions between them; neither shows as a removal against base, because the lines it touched were added by this branch in the first place.)

Behavior added:
- **A switch phrasing that names a workspace resolves to a switch, scoped to that workspace.** "switch to code in the workspace Switch" and its three recorded siblings now instant-resolve to `switch_running_app` carrying only the app name, while the clause continues to bind the task's workspace through `WorkspaceTaskTagging` — so SONNY-58's verdict binding decides the consent from the app that will actually be activated. In scope it is tier 1 and silent; out of scope it is a tier-3 approval naming the resolved app. **This is the first time the scoped switch is reachable from command text at all**: SONNY-58's own manual pass had to be restaged through SONNY-39's card arm because every command-text phrasing fell through to a planner that could not express the intent.
- **The planner can say `switch_running_app`.** A phrasing the resolver never claims — "bring me back to Slack", a focus verb the stop-word guard rightly rejects — now has a truthful operation to become, with a prompt rule that names the misroute directly: a phrase such as "in my research workspace" says where the task belongs, not what to change.
- **"in my X workspace" binds a workspace.** The free-text recognizer previously matched only "in [the|my] workspace X", so the fourth recorded misroute named a workspace Sonny never saw. Escalation-only in every direction: an unscoped task escalates for no boundary at all, and a scoped one only escalates when a resource sits outside it.
- **A typed full stop no longer strands itself in the query.** Edge punctuation comes off the candidate on both paths, so "switch to code in the workspace Switch." activates Code rather than failing to find `code .`. This also fixes an older kind the branch did not create: the bare "switch to chrome." asked for `chrome.` at `48d0150` too, because `RunningAppMatcher.normalize` folds spaces and not punctuation.
- **A workspace named as the thing to switch to is still an `open_workspace` request.** The prompt rule forbids turning a switch or focus request into `edit_workspace` or `create_workspace` and says so explicitly, rather than forbidding `open_workspace` alongside them as it first did — which would have closed the one correct reading of "switch me to my Research workspace" at the planner end while the resolver's own defect closed it at the other.
- **A routine can be taught to save a snippet.** `save_snippet` is a registered tool and in the schema at both nesting levels, so "teach Sonny a routine that saves my welcome snippet" plans, validates and saves — collecting the promise `SnippetSaveCapabilityAdapter.assessRisk` had already been written to keep for scheduled runs (SONNY-31).

Behavior preserved (required, no blanket claims):
- **Every phrasing the resolver already claimed still resolves the same way**, asserted rather than assumed: "switch to chrom", "switch to fin", "switch to Safari", "switch to Notion". The two guards that keep non-app objects off the running-app matcher are untouched and still pinned — "focus on writing my essay" still leads with a preposition (only articles are stripped, never prepositions) and "activate dark mode" still ends in "mode".
- **A clause naming a workspace that is not saved is not subtracted.** The recognizer matches saved names only, so such a command falls to the planner exactly as before, where an `edit_workspace` on a name matching nothing already becomes a clarification rather than a boundary change. The widening is aimed at the destructive case and nothing else.
- **Routine authoring is unchanged.** `switch_running_app` is named in the prompt's `routineSteps` exclusion sentence, so the planner keeps emitting `open_app` inside routines as it did before; no routine that saved before this branch fails to save after it.
- **Direct snippet use is untouched.** `snippet save ;x = y` and exact-trigger expansion still resolve instantly without the planner, and `expand_snippet` is still absent from the schema and from every planner tool — asserted, not assumed.
- **No tier moved and no gate changed.** No adapter's `defaultRiskTier`, no escalation rule, no approval requirement and no consent copy is touched anywhere in the diff.

Architectural decisions / pitfalls discovered (required, write "none" if true):

**The clause was never noise, and treating it as noise would have swapped a loud bug for a quiet one.** The obvious repair — strip "in the workspace X" so the app name fits under the three-word ceiling — is only safe because that same phrase is what `WorkspaceTaskTagging.freeTextWorkspaceName` feeds to `AgentViewModel.resolveTaskScope`, for the instant-resolver branch as much as the planner branch. Had the resolver subtracted a phrase nothing else consumed, a user naming a workspace would have got an unscoped run and no sign of it. **The invariant the fix is built on, and the one to keep if this code is touched again: the resolver removes from the app query exactly the clause that binds the task's scope — one recognizer, two consumers.** It is what forced the second word order into scope; recognizing "in my X workspace" was not opportunistic tidying but the only way to make the invariant true for all four recorded commands.

**A negative that only one of two readers can see is not a boundary, it is a coincidence.** Both tickets are the same defect in different directions — SONNY-68's operation was invisible to the planner while the resolver was assumed to cover it, SONNY-48's was permitted by the routine validator while the planner had no word for it — and in both cases the disagreement was invisible from either side alone. The exclusion list and the empty-`plannerTools` adapters happened to name the same six operations at `48d0150` with nothing requiring it. `PlannerBoundaryTests.plannerExclusionsAgreeWithEmptyToolAdaptersAndWithInstantResolverCoverage` now asserts all three views — excluded from the schema, no planner tool, reachable through the instant resolver — as one set, and drives the third leg from real resolver commands rather than a list, so a resolver change that closes a front door fails there. This is §A4's "add the missing pin", and it is the piece that makes the next SONNY-48-shaped question a decision instead of a discovery.

**Making an operation planner-visible makes it routine-nestable, and the nested path has no shared prepared plan.** The schema builds `routineSteps` from the same `plannerVisibleCases` enum as top-level steps, so the two questions cannot be answered separately. `RunRoutineCapabilityAdapter` calls `routineRunSpec` again from `preview`, `assessRisk` and `execute`, rebuilding the nested plan from the store each time, and each rebuild re-runs `resolveDefaultOutputs` on a fresh unpinned plan — so a nested `switch_running_app` step would be resolved and pinned twice, independently, reopening the assessment-versus-execution divergence SONNY-58 closed. At the top level that cannot happen because `AgentRunner` threads one `preparedRun.plan` through both gates, which is precisely what makes SONNY-58's pin total. `.switchRunningApp` therefore joined `forbiddenStepOperations` — preserving today's reachability exactly (routines could not carry it through any product path before either) and following the existing planner-visible-but-routine-forbidden shape that `create_workspace`, `edit_workspace` and `open_workspace` already have. **The general rule for the next operation that leaves the exclusion list: decide the nested question at the same time, or the schema decides it for you.**

**`.saveSnippet` and `.expandSnippet` are asymmetric on purpose, and the asymmetry is now written down at both ends.** Saving is an automation primitive with a scheduled-run story already built for it; expansion's `execute` returns the snippet text as the run summary and types it nowhere, so a routine-nested expansion produces text with no consumer. Symmetry would have bought prompt surface and no user story. Recorded on the expansion adapter's empty `plannerTools`, on `plannerVisibleCases`, on `forbiddenStepOperations`, and asserted by `snippetExpansionIsStillNotSomethingThePlannerCanAskFor` — four places, because the ticket's actual complaint was that the emptiness read as an omission from every one of them.

**Subtracting a matched phrase from folded text needs an index map, not a second regex.** `normalized(_:)` folds a whole string and gives no way back to the caller's characters, so a range found in the folded copy cannot be cut out of the original — and lowercasing the survivor would have put the wrong words in the summary the user reads. `FoldedText` folds character by character while recording each folded character's source index, and folds the pattern side through the same function, so the two sides of a comparison cannot fold differently. The alternative considered and rejected was a separate literal search in the resolver: that is a second recognizer, and two recognizers of the same phrase are how the resolver and the scope binder would drift apart again.

**A guard is an ordering contract, and moving work in front of one silently repeals part of it.** The first version of this branch narrowed the switch candidate — clause, then leading article, then a trailing " app" — and only then handed it to `looksLikeRunningAppName`. Two of that guard's eight leading stop words are `the` and `my`, which are also the two articles the trim removed, so on this path those rejections stopped existing. Nothing failed: the guard still had six stop words, a word ceiling and a "mode" rule, the code comment said "neither trim touches the guard's own rejections", and the example it picked to prove that was one of the cases still working. What the two dead words were protecting turned out to be **workspace-opening phrasings** — "switch to my Research workspace", "switch to the workspace Switch" — which reached the planner's `open_workspace` rule at `48d0150` and were captured as doomed app queries after the change; and because the new prompt rule as first worded also told the planner never to turn a switch or focus request into `open_workspace`, the same intent was closed at both ends by two edits that each looked local. **The lesson generalises past this function: when you insert a normalisation ahead of a predicate, the predicate's rejection set is part of the contract, and the only honest way to keep it is to enumerate the rejections and measure each one at the base commit.** That is what the base-parity table in `SwitchInWorkspaceRoutingTests` now is — all eight stop words, the ceiling and the "mode" rule, each with its `48d0150` outcome recorded beside it. The shipped mechanism makes the contract structural rather than disciplined: with no clause recognised the candidate is the remainder, untouched, so the guard cannot be partially repealed at all; the one normalisation that remains is gated on a clause and is a *naming form* — `[the|my] NAME app`, where the trailing noun is what declares the phrase names an app — rather than a rule about articles. (PR #39 review, cycle 1, F1.)

**A test whose name says "unchanged" is a claim about the base commit, and it has to be measured there.** `theExistingResolverBehaviourIsUnchangedBy…` asserted that "switch to the Notes app" resolves to `Notes`; at `48d0150` that command returned `nil`. The assertion was true of the head and false as a statement about preservation, and it read to any future reader as evidence the widening was a no-op. Preserved-behaviour tests in this branch now carry the base measurement per case and assert only cases that really are unchanged; everything the branch added has its own named test. Same class as the SONNY-58 entry correction this repo already ran once — a claim that reads as verification of old behaviour when it is really an assertion of new. (PR #39 review, cycle 1, F2.)

**A mutant that survives is either a coverage hole or a bad mutant, and the difference is worth one more run.** The cycle-2 battery's M22 replaced `FoldedText`'s index map with a naive one-entry-per-character version and **survived** the multi-character-fold test written in the same round. The test was the problem, not the coverage bar: every expansion in it sat *after* the clause, where an off-by-one only shifts the cut's far edge and the whitespace trim absorbs it. Moving an expansion into the text that must survive — "switch to Straße in the workspace Alpha" — makes the same map cut a character off the wrong side, and M22 dies. Recorded because the first battery had the mirror-image case (M5's no-op mutant, which needed the *mutant* fixed rather than the test), and the two together are the reason a survivor is never left unexamined.

**Mutation battery: 22 designed, 22 killed, plus one neutrality check that correctly survived.** Run against the committed tree per round (`461326e` for SONNY-68's eleven, `d1917a3` for SONNY-48's five, `b2a1856`/`eda5e74` for the cycle-2 round's six), each mutant reverted with `git checkout -- Sources/` and the tree status-verified clean before the next. SONNY-68: M1 resolver narrowing removed entirely → 6 killing tests; M2 clause subtraction only → 6; M3 the article and " app" trims only → 4; M4 `switch_running_app` re-excluded from the schema → the schema-shape test and the §A4 pin; M5 its `plannerTools` emptied → both goldens, `ToolRegistryTests` and the pin; M6 dropped from `forbiddenStepOperations` → the eight-operation list test; M7 the second clause word order removed → 5; M8 the new prompt rule deleted → the system-prompt golden; M9 the `routineSteps` sentence reverted → the same golden; M10 clause subtraction dropping the text after the clause instead of rejoining it → the clause-removal test; M11 the recognizer returning the raw typed name instead of the canonical saved one → 6, including four pre-existing tagging tests. SONNY-48: M12 `save_snippet` re-excluded → the schema test and the pin; M13 its `plannerTools` emptied → 4; M14 its prompt rule deleted → the golden; M15 `expand_snippet` given a planner tool, the boundary in the other direction → 5; M16 `.saveSnippet` added to `forbiddenStepOperations` → 6, including SONNY-31's own `aScheduledSnippetRoutineStillRunsOnItsSecondOccurrence`, which is the sharpest available evidence that SONNY-48's story and SONNY-31's are one story. **N1, the neutrality check:** removing the "does this command contain the word workspace at all" pre-check leaves the suite green, which is the evidence that it is an optimisation rather than a behaviour change — a mutant that survives on purpose, recorded as such so a later reader does not mistake it for a coverage hole. **Cycle-2 round:** M17 the shipped F1 defect reintroduced (the unconditional article trim ahead of the guard) → killed by the base-parity table and the naming-form test; M18 the naming form widened back into a bare article rule on the clause path → killed by the base-parity table, which is what proves the gate is load-bearing and not decoration; M19 the naming form removed outright → killed by 4, observation 2 among them; M20 edge punctuation no longer trimmed → killed by the punctuation test; M21 the prompt rule re-forbidding `open_workspace` → killed by the golden *and* by the both-directions test, which is the point of having both; M22 the index map replaced by a naive same-length assumption → **survived first, killed after the test was fixed** (see the pitfall above). **Two mutants were run twice, for opposite reasons, and both are recorded because a battery's value is entirely in whether its mutants actually mutate and its tests can actually fail**: M5's first form wrote `[] as [AgentTool] + [tool]`, the same array, so the *mutant* was a no-op and re-running it correctly killed it by four tests; M22's first run was a genuine survivor and the *test* was what needed strengthening.

Known limitations / deferred scope:
- **The planner half is planner judgment, and the tests pin only what can be pinned.** That `switch_running_app` exists in the schema, is described to the model, and carries a rule naming the misroute is asserted exactly; that the model *chooses* it for "bring me back to Slack" is not, and cannot be by a unit test. That is manual-test item 7 on SONNY-68 and, in the longer run, the kind of thing SONNY-72's planner benchmark exists to measure.
- **`focus on X in my Y workspace` still falls to the planner.** The leading-stop-word guard rejects "on …" by design — it is what keeps "focus on writing my essay" off the running-app matcher — so that phrasing is caught by the planner half rather than the resolver half. Deliberate: weakening the guard to claim it would trade a routing bug for a false-claim bug.
- **The prompt's `routineSteps` exclusion sentence still names five of the eight forbidden operations**, omitting `create_workspace`, `edit_workspace` and `open_workspace`. Adding `switch_running_app` to it was required by this branch; naming the other three would change what the planner attempts for "teach me a routine that opens my research workspace", a routine-authoring product decision this branch has no business making. Filed as **SONNY-74** (Backlog, untriaged).
- **Two normalisations reach the guard, and only one of them is a strict no-op against base.** Clause subtraction cannot change a clause-free command at all. Edge-punctuation trimming can, and does so deliberately (F3) — including in one degenerate way worth naming rather than discovering: a trailing punctuation *word* ("switch to x y z .") counts toward the three-word ceiling at `48d0150` and does not here, so a four-token remainder can become a three-token one. That is a word-count change, not a stop-word change, the base-parity table's subject is the stop words, and the direction is fail-closed either way — but "the no-clause path is byte-identical to base" would have been a blanket claim, and it is not one.
- **`AgentActionExecutor.previewNestedPlan` routes to `preview(plan:)`, which alone among the executor's entry points never calls `resolveDefaultOutputs`.** Moot today precisely because `switch_running_app` is banned from routines — nothing that needs pinning can reach it — but it is the same shape the ban exists to contain, one layer further in, and the next operation to leave the exclusion list has to look at it. Raised by PR #39's cycle-1 review as noted-not-filed; recorded here rather than turned into a ticket because there is no reachable defect to fix, only a trap to know about.
- **A scoped switch decrypts `WorkspaceStore` twice per submission** — once for the resolver's clause subtraction, once in `resolveTaskScope`. Accepted rather than cached: the two reads happen at different layers with different lifetimes, a shared cache would be new mutable state on a path whose whole virtue is that it has none, and the literal-noun pre-check already skips the read entirely for every command that cannot carry a clause. Named so the cost is a decision on the record and not an oversight.
- **The prompt's "For X, produce…" rules do not uniformly follow adapter registration order**, and this branch's own commit message and two ticket comments said they did. 23 of 24 follow; `create_local_draft` (`OpenAIPlanner.swift:158`, adapter 8) precedes `open_generated_artifact` (`:159`, adapter 7). Pre-existing, untouched, and the placement chosen for the two new rules is right either way — the defect was the unchecked universal, which is exactly CLAUDE.md's count-the-whole-population rule. Corrected on both tickets. (PR #39 review, cycle 1, F4.)
- **A workspace named the same as a verb still wins on the bare word.** With a workspace called "Switch" saved, the command "switch" is an exact saved-name match and quick dispatch opens the workspace before the running-app prefixes are consulted. Unchanged by this branch, now asserted in the fixture so it reads as precedence rather than a bug; the bare verb's clarification stays pinned in `RunningAppAndRecentArtifactsTests` against a store with no such collision.

Open questions (required, write "none" if true): none. SONNY-48's direction (A versus B) was the one open product question and the founder answered it — direction A, the planner learns `save_snippet` — as did the veto this session offered on `switch_running_app`'s routine containment, which was kept. Both exchanges are now recorded on their tickets as question-and-answer with dates, rather than only inside the prose of the session that acted on them: PR #39's cycle-1 review filed that single-witness shape as a convention finding, and it was right to (SONNY-48, F5).

Next branch: per E12's ratified cleanup sequence, **SONNY-64** (pre-built-plan dispatch, the shared vision/workspace-sheet prerequisite), then the bug cluster (SONNY-28/30/34/35) — all existing tickets, for the user to assign. Nothing in this branch touches vision, the planner A/B router (§A, SONNY-72's gate) or the experiment branch's Discord catalog entry.

### Branch: feature/prebuilt-plan-dispatch
Status: complete
Date: 2026-08-09
Tickets: **SONNY-64** (editing a workspace from its detail sheet round-tripped through the floating widget's composer, because no path existed to dispatch a plan a screen had already built; both halves — the dispatch path and the picker that uses it — land here). Third item of E12, the ratified pre-vision cleanup sequence (`docs/sonny-v1-vision-planner-integration-plan.md` §E12), and the one the plan elevates as a *shared* prerequisite: §B1's vision-envelope consent is the next caller of the same mechanism. The ticket was filed as an untriaged founder ask and carries **no `Branch:` line and no acceptance criteria** — its own scope sketch says "for planning, not binding" — so this session took the description's ask plus the constraints the record makes binding (SONNY-41's adjudicated capability rule, SONNY-13's origin guard rails, SONNY-39's arm lifecycle, SONNY-40's tier semantics) as the contract and chose the branch name. **Neither was posted to the ticket at claim time** — an earlier draft of this sentence said they were "recorded both on the ticket at claim", and the only comment on SONNY-64 when PR #40's review ran was the closing one. Both are stated in that closing comment's "Contract note", which was written at close and not at claim; the cycle-2 comment says so plainly rather than leaving the timing to be inferred from the timestamps. (PR #40 review, F2.) No discovery tickets filed.
Reviewed by: fresh session (per WORKFLOW.md step 7) on PR #40 — **cycle 1 at `7c058ae`, verdict not clean, no blocking correctness defect; cycle 3 at `f5cc36a`, verdict PASS with six residuals, four of which land in the post-cap records round and two of which are recorded and left** (C5, an absolute claim in a doc comment that a wider sweep falsifies — three user-visible literals in the view body, not one, the two the finding named having genuinely moved; and C6, line numbers cited in a ticket comment without the SHA they were measured at, correct at `7c058ae` and stale at `f5cc36a`). Neither is above the recorded-residual bar WORKFLOW.md step 7 sets, and the reviewer recommended against spending a fourth cycle on them. The reviewer re-measured both ends (751/58 at `dfafac6`, 783/60 at `7c058ae`, exit 0 each), reconciled the +32 by name, spot-checked the mutation battery from the code, and tried and failed to break unforgeability, the single funnel, consent parity, the R-1 fix and the pending-approval guard — all recorded under "what I tried to break and could not" so cycle 2 does not re-derive them. What it found was a decision record that did not exist (F1), a reachability story repeated six times that the code contradicts (F4), a behavioural loose end (F5), a convention miss the branch's own doc comment denied (F7), and coverage and precision gaps against standards the branch set for itself (F3, F6, F8–F12). **All twelve addressed in cycle 2**; four needed production lines and were fixed in-branch on the user's explicit call rather than deferred, so a full cycle-3 re-check follows. Before the PR opened, this session had also run its own adversarial pass (six lenses, each candidate attacked by three refuters); one finding survived and its branch-owned parts were fixed in `d6e97f0`.

Spec sections covered: none newly. This changes how an existing capability is reached, not what it may do: no tier, no escalation rule, no approval requirement and no consent string is touched anywhere in the diff.
Files changed:
- `Sources/MacAgentCore/AgentRunner.swift` (`PreparedPlanSource.directUserAction`; `prepareResolvedPlan(_:source:)` now stamps the origin onto the prepared run, and `prepare(command:)` hardcodes `.planner` with no parameter for a caller to override)
- `Sources/MacAgentCore/AgentActionExecutor.swift` (`PreparedAgentRun.source`, defaulted to the least-trusted `.planner`)
- `Sources/MacAgentCore/EditWorkspaceCapabilityAdapter.swift` (`WorkspaceScopeEditRequest`; `plan(for:)`, the six-branch field mapping; `removalUnits(kind:values:catalog:)` made public, plus a `catalog:` overload of the private `removalMatchKey` so the exported answer and the executed one are one function)
- `Sources/MacAgent/AgentViewModel.swift` (`prebuiltPlan` threaded through `dispatch` → `start` → `performStart` and branched *first*; `dispatchWorkspaceScopeEdit` replacing `composeWorkspaceScopeEdit`; `dispatch` now returns whether it was accepted and refuses while an approval is pending; `activeTaskPlanSource`; the auto-approval log line reading the run's source rather than `autoExecute`)
- `Sources/MacAgent/WorkspaceScopeEditPresentation.swift` (new — `WorkspaceScopeEditDispatch`, `WorkspaceScopeEditCommand`, `WorkspaceScopeAddPresentation` and its category table)
- `Sources/MacAgent/CommandCenterView.swift` (`WorkspaceScopeAddView`, the picker; `removeDispatch` replacing `removeCommand`; `kind` replacing the completable `addCommand` prefix; the sheet's `removalUnits` reconstruction deleted in favour of the capability's own)
- `Tests/MacAgentCoreTests/PrebuiltPlanDispatchTests.swift` (new, 13 tests), `Tests/MacAgentTests/WorkspaceScopeAddPickerTests.swift` (new, 13 tests), `Tests/MacAgentTests/WorkspaceDetailSheetTests.swift` (30 → 36 tests)
Tests: the exact flagged command from CLAUDE.md -> pass. Baseline on `main` at `dfafac6`: **751 tests / 58 suites, exit 0**. At `7c058ae` (cycle-1 review head): **783 tests / 60 suites, exit 0**. At the cycle-2 fix head: **790 / 60, exit 0**. Net +39 tests, +2 suites. **Five test functions disappear against base and all five are replacements, not removals** — `git diff dfafac6..HEAD -- 'Tests/*' | grep '^-' | grep 'func '` returns exactly those five, each the compose-era name of a behaviour that no longer exists because the API it tested was deleted. **"Rename" is the wrong word for all four and is corrected here**: every one had a materially rewritten body, because the API under test was gone; three of the four gained assertions, and one lost one. The list: `editAffordancesProduceKindSpecificEditWorkspaceCommands` → `…Requests` — which **dropped `#expect(presentation.apps.entries.allSatisfy { !$0.removeCommand.hasSuffix(" ") })` with no replacement**, an invariant about removals, which still exist. It is not replaced because the thing it guarded is gone: `removeCommand` does not exist at this head (zero hits repo-wide), and the sentence it protected was a string a planner had to read, where a stray trailing space changed what got parsed. The same sentence is now a display label on a dispatch whose fields are pinned directly, so nothing silent can slip through it. Named rather than folded into a category, because a reader who trusts the category skips exactly this check — `composingAScopeEditDropsAnArmedCardBindingFromAnotherWorkspace` → `dispatching…`, `composingAScopeEditDuringAClarificationPauseIsRefused` → `dispatching…`, `theClarificationContinuationCannotCarryAComposedEdit` → `…ADispatchedEdit`, and `composingAScopeEditFillsTheComposerAndWritesNothing` → `dispatchingARemovalRunsThePreBuiltPlanAndStopsAtTheSameTierThreeApproval`. **The last of those is not a rename and is worth stating exactly rather than folding into a "no coverage lost" claim**: its predecessor asserted that nothing ran (`isRunning == false`, `isAwaitingApproval == false`), which is the behaviour this ticket deliberately changes. What replaced it asserts strictly more about everything still true (the store untouched, the widget summoned) and adds the assessment, the consent string, the plan's fields and the run's origin; the two "nothing ran" assertions are gone because they are now false by design.

What changed for the user:
- **Adding to a workspace is a picker, not a sentence to finish typing.** The Add button opens a dialog listing the launch catalog's twelve apps in five functional groups (Browsers, Communication, Productivity, Media, Developer), each row a single click, with apps the workspace already lists marked "Already added" rather than offered again. URLs and file locations get a plain field in the same dialog.
- **An app Sonny cannot launch is still addable, and the dialog says what that means before you commit.** `WorkspaceScopeOnlyApps.scopeOnlyNote` — the capability's own sentence — appears live as the name is typed, rather than only afterwards in the preview and the result.
- **Remove acts on the row you tapped.** No composer, no retyping, and the shared-removal note beside the row now names every entry that will actually leave with it.
- **Nothing about the consent changed.** Adding still stops at the tier-2 confirmation, removing at the tier-3 explicit approval, and a removal that empties a dimension still raises SONNY-40's distinct "will no longer restrict … at all" wording. The approval is answered in the floating widget, which a sheet dispatch now brings forward.

Behavior preserved (required, no blanket claims):
- **The gate is the same gate**, structurally rather than by inspection: `prepare(command:)` and `prepare(plan:source:)` funnel into one private `prepareResolvedPlan`, and nothing downstream of it — `executor.prepare`, `assessRisk`, `approvalRequest`, `execute` — is given any way to learn how the plan was authored.
- **No direct store write was added.** `markWorkspaceAsTeam` is still the only one, still touching only `teamType`, and SONNY-41's test pinning that is untouched and still passing.
- **`edit_workspace`'s own logic is untouched.** Not one line of its tier assessment, escalation reasons, list arithmetic, validation or summary changed; the only edits to that file add the request type, the plan factory, and the `removalUnits` export.
- **The arm lifecycle holds.** A sheet dispatch is not `fromComposer`, so an accepted one kills any armed card binding rather than inheriting it — and a refused one leaves it alone, which is the pre-existing rule and is now pinned rather than merely true.
- **The clarification-pause family stays closed**, and stays closed *by inheritance*: the new door goes through the same `dispatch` → `canSubmit` choke point the composer's Send does, so the pause term applies without a fourth copy of the rule. Asserted through the new door anyway.
- **System A only.** The picker uses `SonnyTheme`/`SonnyType`/`SonnyRadius`, `SettingsAdaptiveControlRow`, `SettingsDivider` and `CommandCenterRowActionStyle`; no `WidgetTheme`/`WidgetType` anywhere in the diff.
- **The launch catalog is still twelve apps.** The picker reads it and groups it; it does not extend it. SONNY-66 still owns expansion, and SONNY-65's icons are untouched.

Architectural decisions / pitfalls discovered (required, write "none" if true):

**The seam already existed in the core and did not exist in the view model, and only one of those was obvious.** `AgentRunner.prepare(plan:source:)` has been public since the instant resolver shipped, which makes "dispatch a pre-built plan into the same gate" look like a solved problem. It is not solved one layer up: `performStart` had exactly two branches, both starting from `submittedCommand` — the instant resolver pattern-matches it, and anything it does not match falls through to `try OpenAIPlanner(...)`. `InstantCommandResolver` has no pattern for a workspace edit, so the *natural* implementation — write the sentence into `command` and call `start()` — sends a plan the screen already built to the planner to be re-derived from prose. It would have worked, produced the right approval most of the time, and quietly reintroduced the round-trip the ticket exists to remove. **The pre-built branch is therefore first, not last, and the ordering is the enforcement**: while `prebuiltPlan` is non-nil there is no path from `performStart` to a planner at all.

**A trust signal is only as unforgeable as its decoding path, so the origin was built to have none.** `PreparedPlanSource` is stamped by `AgentRunner.prepare` *after* decoding has finished, `AgentPlan`/`AgentStep` carry no source field for a planner response to populate, and `prepare(command:)` — the one entry point a model's text can reach — takes no source parameter and hardcodes `.planner`. That is asserted the only way worth asserting it: a hostile payload carrying `"source": "direct_user_action"` at both the plan and the step level is decoded and prepared, and still comes out `.planner`. Written as a decode-then-prepare round trip rather than as "this struct has no such property", because a future field would silently invalidate a shape test while this one keeps failing. The default is `.planner` for the same reason `fromComposer` defaults `false`: a construction site added later that says nothing must inherit *no* trust, not the strongest kind.

**The case is named for the mechanism, not the surface, and that was a deliberate reversal.** `workspaceSheet` was the obvious name and the wrong one: the integration plan makes this dispatch path shared plumbing, so it would need renaming the moment §B1's vision consent becomes the second caller — and a rename of a trust signal is the change nobody wants to be reviewing under time pressure. `directUserAction` says the thing that is actually true of every caller it will ever have: the user's own interaction constructed this exact plan and no natural language was interpreted on the way. A surface that later needs telling apart from this one adds its own case rather than overloading it.

**SONNY-64 builds the origin and reads it nowhere, and the record says so at both ends.** SONNY-13's own comment is explicit that building the relaxation belongs to row C's ticket set "not before it", so the carrier ships inert: nothing branches on `source` to change a tier, a requirement or a gate, and the only consumer added is `activeTaskPlanSource`, a read-only derived property. The one place the source *is* read is a log line — the auto-approval confirmation message, which previously read `autoExecute` and would have called a screen-built run a "Voice command". Unreachable for `edit_workspace`, whose floor is tier 2, and fixed anyway, because "unreachable today" is how a knowingly-wrong string survives to the day it is reachable.

**A picker over a catalog would have been narrower than the free text it replaced, which is the whole reason the free-entry field is not a fallback.** `EditWorkspaceCapabilityAdapter.validatedAppAdditions` rejects only a *blank* app name; an app `MacAppCatalog` cannot resolve is legal and deliberate (SONNY-44's scope/launch decoupling), and a dialog offering only the twelve would have silently removed the ability to put Xcode inside a boundary. The dialog therefore carries both halves, and the disclosure is `WorkspaceScopeOnlyApps`' own sentence rather than a picker-flavoured rewrite — so the moment the name is typed and the approval that follows it cannot say different things about the same app.

**Two lists describing one set is normally how an entry disappears; here it cannot, and a test is what makes that true.** The category table names apps by display name while `MacAppCatalog` owns which apps exist. The dialog is built by resolving the table's names *through* the catalog — so a name the catalog drops produces no row — and anything the table does not name falls into "More apps" rather than out of the dialog. `everyCatalogAppAppearsExactlyOnce` pins the totality, and a second test builds a deliberately unknown catalog to prove the leftover path is real rather than theoretical. SONNY-66 can widen the catalog without touching this file. The table's *order* is used within a group, not the catalog's, because the catalog's is incidental — filtering it produced "Finder, Notes, Calendar", which is where the catalog happens to have put them and not a grouping anyone chose. That was caught by a test literal disagreeing with the implementation, and the implementation was what changed.

**SONNY-41's R-1 was a second mechanism, and the fix was to delete it rather than teach it one more case.** The sheet computed "removing this also removes …" by probing `WorkspaceScope.verdict(for:)` symmetrically — a careful reconstruction of the adapter's removal keys, written that way because those keys were not visible outside `MacAgentCore`. It was right for every entry the evaluator considers live and structurally blind to every entry it does not: a lone inert entry makes its own single-entry scope `.unconstrained`, so the probe could never return `.inScope` for one, so two inert entries folding to a single removal key (`~/Downloads/Alpha` and `~/Downloads/Alpha/`, both outside the whitelist) drew as independent rows whose Remove button promised to take one and took both. Recorded by SONNY-41's cycle-3 review "for SONNY-42", which turned out to be a docs-only ticket, so it was never fixed. Exporting `removalUnits` from the adapter makes the disclosure and the deletion the same function by construction. **The general rule: when a surface has to predict what another module will do, the cheap fix is to reconstruct the rule and the correct fix is to export it — a reconstruction is only ever right in the cases someone thought of.** (Founder-ratified in-branch, 2026-08-09, rather than carried forward again.)

**The refusal that mattered was not the one the ticket asked for.** `start()`'s first branch turns a call made while an approval is pending into `approvePendingRun()` — correct for the widget composer's Send button, which is the control the widget relabels for exactly that job, and wrong for every programmatic caller. The sheet's buttons are `.disabled(isTaskInFlight)`, so this was unreachable by clicking; it was not unreachable by *timing*. **The reachability story this branch first told was wrong, and wrong in the flattering direction.** It said scheduled routines raise approvals with nobody watching. They cannot: `performScheduledRun` executes with `approvalDecision: .approved(.tier2)` and routes every `RiskApprovalError` to `pauseSchedule` plus a notice — SONNY-31's ratified notify-and-pause design — so it never writes `approvalRequest`. The claim appeared in six places and was inherited from a stale sentence in `.claude/rules/macagent-ui-conventions.md`, which is corrected too. The real routes are both foreground: a run the user started pausing at its approval (`performStart`), and `performApproval`'s stale-approval re-arm when a re-assessment lands above the approved tier. Either can arrive between a render and a tap, which is all a cheap structural guard on a trust boundary needs. **The lesson is the direction of the error**: a justification that makes a guard sound better-motivated than the evidence supports is the one nobody re-checks, because it is arguing for something already agreed. (PR #40 review, F4.)

**A guard placed in a shared helper is a claim about every caller, and this repo's H1 precedent is that such a claim costs one test per door — which first requires counting the doors.** The branch said the guard "also covers `openWorkspaceWidget` and `runRoutineWidget`". `dispatch` has **five** callers, and the enumeration matters in both directions. `dispatchTranscribedCommand` is behaviour-changed and was named nowhere: `canUseVoice` blocks *starting* a recording while an approval is pending, but an approval can land during the recording or the transcription, and the finished transcript then went straight into `start()` — so a sentence the user spoke about something else entirely would have landed as a silent **allow** on whatever tier-3 action was waiting. That is the strongest thing the guard does and the record did not contain it. `runRoutineWidget` was named but untested, its button living in `RoutineDetailView`. `retryLastCommand` is **unaffected** — its own `!isTaskInFlight` guard is a strict superset and fires first — and that answer is now measured rather than assumed, by a test that asserts the choke point's refusal line is *absent* and dies when that older guard is weakened (M17). All five doors are pinned. (PR #40 review, F3.)

**"`start` kills the arm unconditionally" was written in this branch's own doc comment and was false.** Both of `start()`'s early returns sit in front of the arm-clearing line, so a *refused* dispatch leaves an armed "New task here" binding alive. The behaviour is correct and pre-existing — the arm binds the next composer dispatch, and a refusal means none happened, so killing it would discard an intent the user still holds because an unrelated button was pressed at an unlucky moment — but the sentence was wrong and nothing pinned the rule. Both are fixed. The pin also caught the *second* overclaim, in the replacement sentence: "the arm stays visible" is wrong for exactly the window this path runs in, because `boundWorkspaceName` prefers the in-flight task's binding and only falls back to the arm, so while the refusing task is live the chip names *that* workspace and the arm reappears when it clears. The test asserts both halves.

**Mutation battery: 22 designed, 22 killed, one of them only after a coverage hole it exposed was closed.** Run against the committed tree (`5ef804f` for the first ten, `d6e97f0` for the self-review round's three, `3ed2570` for cycle 2's nine), each mutant applied by literal patch, the suite run, and the file reverted with `git checkout --` with the tree status-verified clean before the next. **M1** `prepare(command:)` stamping `.directUserAction` → 2 tests. **M2** the pre-built branch claiming `.instantResolver` → 1. **M3** the pending-approval guard removed from `dispatch` → 2. **M4** the widget no longer summoned on an accepted edit → 1. **M5** `removalUnits` grouping nothing → 16, the broadest, because the shared-removal note is read by a lot of the sheet's existing coverage. **M6** an app addition written into the removal field → 3. **M7** "already added" degraded to raw string equality, losing the `Google Chrome`/`Chrome` alias → 2. **M8** the scope-only disclosure suppressed → 2. **M9** `PreparedAgentRun.source` defaulting to `.directUserAction` instead of `.planner` → 1. **M10** the uncategorized-leftovers path removed → 2. Review round: **M11** the pending-approval guard narrowed to pre-built dispatches only → killed by the card-door test written for it. **M12** a refused dispatch killing the arm → 2. **M13** `dispatchWorkspaceScopeEdit` reporting failure on the accepted path → **survived**, and the survivor was the point: nothing held the contract the picker's dismissal depends on, so a dispatch that succeeded while reporting otherwise would have left the dialog stacked over the approval it had just raised. One assertion added, mutant re-run, mutant dead. **Cycle 2's nine, each with the test that killed it:** **M14** the pending-approval guard removed outright → all four door tests (sheet, voice, routine, card). **M15** the guard narrowed to pre-built dispatches only → voice, routine, card. **M16** the refusal trace removed from the choke point → the voice door test. **Its description here first said "the refusal trace", which overstates what it reaches**: F5 added *two* lines and M16 deletes only the cause-specific one on the approval guard. Cycle 3 deleted the other — the cause-neutral `canSubmit` line — and the suite stayed green, a measured survivor of exactly M13's shape one round later: a contract the fix was built around with nothing holding it. Closed by an assertion on the clarification-pause door, which is the door that actually emits that line. (PR #40 cycle-3 residual C1.) **M17** `retryLastCommand`'s own older guard weakened → `aRetryWhileAnApprovalIsPendingIsRefusedByItsOwnOlderGuard`, which is what turns "this door is unaffected" from an assumption into a measurement. **M18** the inert-entry exemption dropped → the new pre-built inert test plus its two typed-path siblings. **M19** the picker's "Already added" string changed and **M22** its free-entry accessibility label detached from the workspace → the copy-ownership test. **M20** the free-entry duplicate refusal removed and **M21** that refusal extended to URLs and folders → the two F12 tests, one per direction. **A process failure, and it is the second occurrence rather than a discovery** — recorded here as one lineage, because presenting it as a first-time finding is how a hazard gets two first-times and no ledger. **The precedent is this document's own SONNY-40 entry** (`docs/sonny-v1-implementation-changelog.md`, `feature/workspace-restriction-scope`): "a mutation battery reverts `Sources/` with `git checkout`, so it must only ever run against a committed tree. Running one over uncommitted work during SONNY-40 destroyed a round of fixes that then had to be redone. … Commit first, every time." Cycle 2's first battery run did exactly that again: executed against an *uncommitted* tree, the script's `git checkout -- <file>` revert silently deleted the four production fixes mid-run, producing three bogus "kills" whose real cause was the missing code. Caught when a filtered re-run failed to compile against symbols that should have existed; the work was restored, committed, and the battery re-run clean at `3ed2570`. **The mitigation added — a dirty-tree abort — lives in a session-local script and is therefore inherited by nobody**, which is why occurrence three would start from the same place as occurrences one and two. Filed as **SONNY-75** (Backlog, untriaged) — commit a shared mutation harness carrying that abort under `scripts/`, seeded by both incidents, with a survivor reported loudly and a mis-matched mutant refused rather than passing silently (this round produced four of the latter). The durable lesson is narrower than "commit first": *a revert mechanism that reads from HEAD is only safe if HEAD is what you think it is*, and a guard that lives outside version control is not a guard the next session has.

Known limitations / deferred scope:
- **No wireframe exists for this dialog, and none is expected.** The workspace detail sheet was already net-new UI with no wireframe (recorded on `feature/workspace-restriction-scope`); the picker inside it inherits that status. It follows the sheet's own System A language rather than a static reference, which is a stated exception under CLAUDE.md's fidelity rule, not a gap.
- **URL and folder additions are a plain text field, not a picker.** The ticket's own words are "apps categorized, eventually URLs/folders equivalents", which scopes them out; the user ratified widening this branch to cover them so the sheet is not left half-migrated (2026-08-09), and chose the field over a native folder chooser. A chooser would be a second thing that has to agree with `PathWhitelist` about "inside Desktop or Documents" — the duplicated-rule class this area has already paid for once.
- **Validation is not duplicated in the dialog, deliberately.** A malformed URL or an out-of-whitelist folder still dispatches and is refused by the capability, so the user learns from the same code the command line uses. The cost is that the refusal arrives as a run failure rather than as inline feedback; the benefit is one rule instead of two, and a test pins that a malformed value really is still submitted.
- **`TaskWorkspaceScope` contributes nothing to an `edit_workspace` assessment**, because `PlanScopedResources` classifies its list fields as contents being declared rather than resources being touched (`.none`). So the scope threaded through this dispatch is correct and currently inert for this capability — worth knowing before the shared path's next caller, where it will not be.
- **`activeTaskPlanSource` reports the most recently *prepared* run, not only an in-flight one.** A completed run leaves `preparedRun` set until the next one starts. The two readings coincide while a run is being assessed, which is where row C will read it; documented rather than narrowed, because narrowing it would mean a second slot to keep in sync and that enumeration has been missed twice on this class already.
- **The picker shows names, not icons.** SONNY-65's territory, untouched here.
- **The free-entry field's duplicate check covers apps only, and the wart that leaves is accepted rather than absent.** `verdict(for:)` answers containment rather than entry identity for URLs and folders — `api.github.com` is `.inScope` under a stored `github.com`, `~/Documents/Alpha` under a stored `~/Documents` — and both are genuinely new entries `edit_workspace` would add, so refusing on the verdict would block legitimate narrowing. **What that leaves reachable, stated plainly because an accepted wart should be: typing a URL or folder the workspace already holds — including a canonically identical one, a stored `~/Documents/Alpha` against a typed `~/Documents/Alpha/` — still raises a real tier-2 approval that resolves to "No change: the workspace already matches this edit."** That is the original F12 outcome, surviving for these two kinds. **The reason differs by kind, and this branch first gave only one of them for both.** For URLs it is a real API constraint: identity is `entryKey`'s bespoke `.webDomain` branch, private to `MacAgentCore`, and refusing would need a new export. For folders it is a choice: `entryKey`'s `.fileLocation` branch *is* `removalMatchKey`, `PathWhitelist.canonicalURL` is already public, and the already-exported `removalUnits` computes exactly that key equality — so exact-duplicate refusal with narrowing still allowed was available with no new export at all. One rule across both excepted kinds was chosen over two that differ by kind. Pinned in both directions. (PR #40 review, F12; corrected by its cycle-3 residual C3.)
- **The URL and folder dialogs still show no existing entries**, while the sheet behind them does. Noted by the cycle-1 review alongside F12; not built here, and not filed, because it is picker *design* rather than a review fix.

Open questions (required, write "none" if true): none. Three decisions were the user's, and **their provenance is on SONNY-64 as its own dated comment — the question as put, the options offered, and the answer given — not asserted here.** This line asserted all three on its own in the first draft, which is precisely what PR #39's review stopped one branch earlier (SONNY-48, F5) and which this entry sat 68 lines below. Two of the three widened the branch's scope beyond the ticket's own words, so the record of who chose them is the part that matters. (PR #40 review, F1.)

### Branch: feature/silent-drop-cluster
Status: complete
Date: 2026-08-10
Tickets: **SONNY-34** (repeated steps of a non-chaining workflow were silently dropped — only the first occurrence ran), **SONNY-35** (only the first matching step got its default output path resolved), **SONNY-28** (two same-named `.docx` files sharing an output folder converted onto one PDF), **SONNY-30** (`CreateWorkspace`/`SaveRoutine` risk assessment used `try?`, so a store load failure silently suppressed the replacement escalation). The fourth and final item of E12, the ratified pre-vision cleanup sequence — the bug cluster the `feature/prebuilt-plan-dispatch` entry names as "the last item before vision work begins". None of the four carried a `Branch:` line (all four are discovery tickets filed by earlier sessions under WORKFLOW.md step 5), so this session chose one branch for all four and recorded the choice, with per-ticket reasoning, on SONNY-34 at claim time. Three discovery tickets filed, all Backlog/untriaged: **SONNY-76**, **SONNY-77**, **SONNY-78**.
Reviewed by: fresh session (per WORKFLOW.md step 7), three cycles. Cycle 1 NOT CLEAN — two HIGH (orphaned bare units executing unrequested work; a zero-step plan reaching a guard recorded as unreachable), four MEDIUM, four LOW; all carried, none left as a recorded residual. Cycle 3 PASS with four residuals, all four closed in a final records-and-copy commit rather than left: the `DestinationKey` over-claim (bounded, and the behavioural gap filed as **SONNY-79**), the same over-claim surviving in `MockDocumentConverter`, a false premise about Swift string normalisation, and a load banner that had begun repeating itself.

Spec sections covered: §10/§11 (risk and approval) for SONNY-30's escalation and SONNY-28's non-escalation decision; §4A capability execution for SONNY-34/35. No tier definition, escalation kind, or policy mapping changed anywhere.
