Tests: `env CLANG_MODULE_CACHE_PATH="$PWD/.build/clang-module-cache" swift test --disable-sandbox -Xswiftc -F -Xswiftc /Library/Developer/CommandLineTools/Library/Developer/Frameworks -Xlinker -rpath -Xlinker /Library/Developer/CommandLineTools/Library/Developer/Frameworks -Xlinker -rpath -Xlinker /Library/Developer/CommandLineTools/Library/Developer/usr/lib` -> exit 0, **817 tests in 60 suites** at `52b8ba0`, up from the **790 in 60** measured on `main` at `899f524` before any code. `swift build` -> exit 0. Per-ticket, each measured at its own commit: `af99427` 799, `c62f3bf` 806, `57e4605` 812, `52b8ba0` 817. Fifteen mutations run across the four tickets (M1–M3, M4–M7, M8–M12, M13–M15); every result is on the relevant ticket's closing comment.

Behavior added:
- **A plan is a chain exactly when it holds more than one *unit* of work.** A unit is a maximal run of consecutive steps mapping to one `Workflow`, cut only where the run genuinely repeats itself — which is what one adapter call can service, since every adapter resolves its spec with `.first(where:)` per operation it owns. "Zip the 3 largest files in ~/Desktop/A and the 3 largest in ~/Desktop/B" now creates both archives; before, it created one and reported success.
- **`segmentPlans(in:)` is one uniform rule** instead of a switch anchored at `.scanSelectLargestFiles`/`.scanDocx`/`.openHackerNews`. Three consequences beyond the headline fix: a run led by a later member of its workflow is grouped (`[fetch_hn_headlines, write_markdown]` with no open step); **a repeated operation cuts the run only when what follows covers exactly the operations the unit already covers**, so `[scan, zip, scan, zip]` is two units while `[scan, scan, zip]` and `[open_hn, fetch, fetch]` stay one; and `shouldChainWhenRepeated` is deleted rather than edited, because counting units subsumes it. *(That middle clause read "a duplicated operation inside a run ends the unit — `[open_hn, fetch, fetch]` is two units, not one that drops the second fetch" until PR #41's review measured what that produced: two browser opens and two reported saves to one path. Corrected here and rewritten in the cycle-2 section below.)*
- **A repeated clarification is rejected** with `invalidPlan("Clarification must be the only planned step.")` rather than answering the first question and discarding the second.
- **Default output paths resolve per unit**, so every occurrence is named in the prepared plan, its preview, and the approval copy — and the path assessed is the path written.
- **Two units never resolve to one generated destination.** A generated path an earlier step of the same plan claimed is suffixed `-2`, `-3`, …, compared against in-plan claims only and never against what is on disk.
- **No two DOCX records *of one scan* ever share a destination**, compared the way the filesystem compares them — case-folded and Unicode-normalised, because the default macOS volume is case-insensitive APFS — with the rename reported in the run summary. The per-scan qualifier is load-bearing: two docx units in one chain scan separately, which is SONNY-76. (Both qualifiers were absent until PR #41's review; the unqualified sentence was false for `Report.docx`/`report.docx`, which aborted the batch exactly as before the fix.) `MockDocumentConverter` also refuses an occupied destination instead of replacing it, so both converters now fail the same way at the one moment that matters.
- **`WorkspaceStore.findWorkspace(named:)` and `RoutineStore.findRoutine(named:)`** — optional-returning lookups that throw only on a real load failure, the shape `SnippetStore.findExactTrigger` already had. `CreateWorkspaceCapabilityAdapter` and `SaveRoutineCapabilityAdapter` use them, so **an unreadable store now surfaces as a failure instead of suppressing the tier-3 replacement escalation. This is prompting added, not removed** — it is the only user-visible increase in interruption on this branch and it is deliberate.

Behavior preserved (required, no blanket claims):
- **`[scan, zip]`, `[scan_docx, convert]`, and the three-step Hacker News preset are each still one unit**, dispatched whole to one adapter — pinned by `aSingleScanAndZipPairIsStillOneUnitProducingOneArchive` and `theHackerNewsPresetIsStillOneUnitWritingOneMarkdownFile`. Splitting any of them would scan with no zip destination, or open and fetch three times for one preset.
- **Every mixed chain segments exactly as before.** `[open_hn, fetch, write, web_to_markdown]` is still two units; `[scan, zip, open_app]` still three. `webResearchSegmentOfAHackerNewsChainIsAssessedForItsOwnCollision`, `webResearchSegmentOfAHackerNewsChainResolvesItsOwnDefaultOutputPath`, `mixedWorkflowPlanExecutesAsChain`, `executorAssessRiskUsesHighestStaticTierInMixedPlan` and `zipThenRevealChainIsRiskAssessedAndGatedAsOneUnit` all pass unmodified.
- **Every SONNY-29 chain-segmented-assessment guarantee still holds** — `secondDraftInAChainEscalatesWhenItsOwnOutputAlreadyExists`, `chainedDraftsWithNoExistingOutputsStayAtTierTwo`, `chainedDraftsTargetingTheSameExistingFileRaiseOneEscalation`, `chainOfDifferentOperationsStillUnionsEveryEscalation`, `chainWhoseSecondShortcutIsUntrustedAssessesAtTheUntrustedTier`, `chainWhoseSecondRoutineCarriesACollisionEscalatesAndNamesIt`, all unmodified.
- **`edit_workspace`'s at-most-one-edit-per-workspace rule still runs against the whole plan**, after per-unit resolution reassembles it — `everyPlanShapeEditingOneWorkspaceTwiceIsRefused` and `aPlanEditingTwoDifferentWorkspacesProceedsAndAssessesEachCorrectly` unmodified.
- **A Finder selection is still read once and pinned across phases** — `finderSelectionInputIsPinnedOnceAcrossPrepareAndExecute` (`reader.callCount == 1`) unmodified.
- **An explicitly named destination is written exactly where the plan said**, never renamed — `twoDraftsExplicitlyNamingOneDestinationAreLeftExactlyAsWritten`.
- **The "output already exists" escalations still fire.** In-plan disambiguation never compares against disk, so a generated path colliding with a real file still raises tier 3 — `theDisambiguatedSecondDraftPathIsTheOneRiskAssessmentChecks`, and mutation M7 exists solely to keep that boundary honest.
- **A DOCX whose own PDF already exists is still skipped, not renamed and converted anyway** — `aDocxWhoseOwnPDFAlreadyExistsIsStillSkippedRatherThanRenamed`, plus the pre-existing `docxDryRunSkipsExistingPDFAndWritesNothing` and `docxExistingPDFSkipDoesNotEscalate`.
- **A first-time workspace or routine save is still tier 2 with no escalation, and a genuinely existing name still escalates to tier 3 and says so** — `creatingAWorkspaceThatDoesNotExistYetStaysAtTierTwoWithNoEscalation`, `creatingAWorkspaceThatAlreadyExistsStillEscalatesAndNamesIt`, `savingARoutineEscalatesOnlyWhenThatNameIsReallyTaken`, plus the pre-existing `replacingExistingRoutineEscalatesToTierThree` and `replacingExistingWorkspaceEscalatesToTierThree`.
- **`Sources/MacAgent/` is untouched.** So is `AgentRunner.swift`, `RiskApproval.swift`, `CapabilityAdapter.swift`, `PathWhitelist.swift`, and `SnippetSaveCapabilityAdapter.swift`.

Architectural decisions / pitfalls discovered (required, write "none" if true):

**SONNY-34's own suggested remedy would have hung the app.** The ticket offered two: reclassify the affected workflows as chaining, or reject the repeated plan. Reclassification is not merely insufficient — `previewChain`/`executeChain` re-enter `preview`/`execute` per segment, so flipping `.largestFiles` to chain-when-repeated makes a two-step `[scan, zip]` segment classify as `.chain` again, re-segment to itself, and recurse without bound. **A discovery ticket's suggested fix is a hypothesis from a session that did not implement it, and it gets tested against the code like any other claim.** What the ticket was really describing was a defect in how two rules related, not in either rule.

**Two rules that must agree is the bug, not the implementation of either.** `shouldChainWhenRepeated` answered "does repeating this workflow make a chain" while `segmentPlans` separately decided where the cuts fall. Five workflows sat in the boolean's false arm precisely because a boolean cannot express "several steps, but only one of each" — which is exactly what those five adapters accept. Deleting the boolean and deriving the answer from the cut leaves one definition of a unit. The check that made the deletion safe rather than bold: every workflow in the old true arm maps from exactly one operation, so for all of them "more than one step" and "more than one unit" were always the same sentence.

**The termination argument is load-bearing and is asserted at runtime, not just reasoned about.** Re-cutting a unit yields that same unit — the proof survives a unit containing an absorbed repeat and is written out in `segmentPlans`' doc comment — and that is what makes it safe to classify a multi-unit plan as `.chain` while the chain walk re-enters `preview`/`execute` per unit. `chainSegments(in:)` refuses the converse (exactly one unit) so a future disagreement between classifier and cutter throws instead of recursing. **Honest limitation: that guard is not pinned by a test and cannot be from outside** — reaching it requires mutating the source. Deleting it leaves the suite green. It is kept because the failure it converts is unbounded re-entry that ends in a stack-overflow crash, which is worth one comparison even unreachable. *(Two corrections from PR #41's review: the first draft of this paragraph said "pairwise distinct", which absorption made false, and said the failure "hangs the app" — the chain walk is synchronous, so it is native-stack growth ending in a crash. The unreachability claim was also asserted on an enumeration covering only the one-unit case; a zero-step plan reached the guard through all three gates. See the cycle-2 section.)*

**SONNY-29's invariant generalises to a third phase.** SONNY-29 made *assessment* see the units execution sees. SONNY-35 is the same statement for *resolution*: `resolveDefaultOutputs` was the last plan-walking path calling adapters with the whole plan. The reusable rule for this executor: **every phase that hands a plan to an adapter must hand it a unit — preview, execute, assess, and resolve alike.** Two hooks are genuine exceptions and are marked as such in the code: `edit_workspace`'s plan-shape rule (unenforceable from inside a unit) and a resolver's right to replace the plan with a clarification (`InvokeShortcutCapabilityAdapter` for an unknown Shortcut name — without that carve-out, per-unit resolution turns a friendly clarification into a hard `invalidPlan`, because a clarification must be a plan's only step).

**Fixing a first-match defect per unit exposes a same-second defect between units, and the second is easy to miss.** Once each unit resolves independently, two units generating the same timestamped default name resolve to one path and the second write destroys the first — the identical silent loss, one level in. `Timestamp.fileSafe` is second-resolution and two untitled drafts share the plan summary, so this is reachable, not theoretical. **The boundary that makes the disambiguation safe rather than a relaxation: compare against paths claimed within the plan only, never against what exists on disk.** Suffixing around a real file would silently delete the tier-3 collision warning the user is entitled to. Mutation M7 is that boundary's guard and kills exactly one test.

**A "keep the old whole-plan pass in front of the new per-unit pass" compromise was tried and rejected.** It preserved the Finder-selection read count within a phase, but reintroduced a wrong answer: on `[scan(A), scan(B), zip]` the whole-plan pass assigns the zip folder A's path while the zip belongs to B's unit, and the per-unit pass cannot correct an already-populated path. The property that actually matters — read once, pinned *across* phases — survives pure per-unit resolution, because a unit whose input is already pinned does not read Finder again.

**A capability that skips rather than overwrites has no collision escalation to give, and that asymmetry is an invariant worth writing down.** SONNY-28's review asked whether `DocxConversionCapabilityAdapter` should gain an `assessRisk` override to match its file-writing siblings. Answer: no. Every sibling escalation fires because that capability is about to overwrite its single output; this one skips an existing destination and renames a contested one, so after the fix there is nothing to raise a tier for. The reasoning is recorded in the adapter so the asymmetry is not re-filed as a gap at every audit. What remains worth saying — "a file was written under a name you did not ask for" — rides the run summary, following `CreateWorkspaceCapabilityAdapter`'s scope-only-apps precedent: both approval panels label an escalation as *what raised this above its default tier*, so a same-tier entry would render an informational line in warning colour under a heading that would then be false. **`ActionPreview` is rendered by nothing in `Sources/MacAgent/`** (verified by sweep, not assumed), so `AgentRunResult.summary` is the only free-text channel that reaches a person.

**A mock whose failure mode differs from the real one at the moment that matters is worse than no mock.** `MockDocumentConverter` wrote with `.atomic` (replaces) while `MicrosoftWordDocumentConverter` finishes with `moveItem` (throws). That single divergence is what made SONNY-28 read as "silent data loss" for a year of records when the loss was only reachable with no Word installed and `MAC_AGENT_MOCK_DOCX=1` — the ticket had to be repriced by a reviewer who noticed. Both refuse now.

**An environment variable read inside a computed property is an untestable code path.** `MockDocumentConverter.isAvailable` read `ProcessInfo.processInfo.environment` directly, so the refuse-to-overwrite branch was unreachable from a test without a process-wide `setenv`, which in a parallel suite either forces serialization forever or leaks into every other test. Defaulted init parameters are the house answer (`TavilySearchProvider`, `OpenAITranscriber`, `OpenAIPlanner` all do it) and cost nothing. One consequence, stated: the environment is now read once at construction rather than per access, which makes a run self-consistent rather than able to flip halfway through.

**A store lookup needs two shapes because it has two kinds of caller.** `workspace(named:)`/`routine(named:)` serve callers for whom absence is an error, so they throw either way — and a caller who only wants to know *whether* a name is taken then reaches for `try?`, folding a decrypt failure back into "not saved". Adding `findWorkspace(named:)`/`findRoutine(named:)` and expressing the throwing pair in terms of them puts the distinction next to the errors it distinguishes, so the next caller gets it right by default rather than by remembering. `SnippetStore.findExactTrigger` had this shape from the start, which is why it was the one site of the three that was never wrong.

**Throwing beat escalating conservatively for a reason specific to these two adapters:** both stores `loadAll()` before they write, so the save being gated cannot succeed against an unreadable store. A conservative escalation would ask the user to approve something that then fails anyway, with a less accurate reason than the real one.

**A mutation-checked test must not trap.** The first draft of `bothZipPairsOfARepeatedLargestFilesPlanCreateTheirOwnArchive` subscripted `result.previews[1]`; mutation M1 left one preview, so the index-out-of-range killed the whole test *process* and truncated the run to three reported failures instead of ten. Comparing whole arrays (`result.previews.map(\.writes) == [[…], […]]`) fails the one test instead. Any test written to be reverted needs assertions that survive the revert.

Known limitations / deferred scope:
- **Three discovery tickets filed, untriaged, none implemented here** (WORKFLOW.md step 5). **SONNY-76**: a two-folder DOCX conversion into one output folder still converts only the first same-named document and reports the second as "skipped, PDF already exists" — for a PDF the same run wrote seconds earlier. The within-scan fix cannot see across units; carrying claimed destinations between a chain's units is cross-unit state threaded through `executeChain`, `previewChain` and `assessRisk`. Not a regression: before SONNY-34 the second pair was dropped with no mention at all. **SONNY-77**: `DocxRecord.isMockDestination` is written and never read. **SONNY-78** (filed high): `AgentViewModel.resolveTaskScope` resolves a task's bound workspace with `try?` and falls back to `.unscoped`, so a corrupt store silently unbinds the workspace and every scope-based tier-3 escalation for that run disappears while the workspace chip still shows — the same failure direction as SONNY-30, a different escalation family, a different target, and it needs a UI decision because the deleted-workspace fallback it shares is legitimate.
- **`af99427` in isolation would have two `web_to_markdown` units resolve to one path.** SONNY-34 and SONNY-35 are two commits of one atomic change and the branch is only correct at `c62f3bf`. Recorded so no one bisects to the middle and reports it as shipped.
- **Exotic plan shapes now take the natural reading rather than a special case.** `[scan(A), scan(B), zip]` is two units — the zip pairs with the scan immediately before it — where it used to be one that dropped scan(B). The planner does not emit this shape; recorded so a reviewer meeting it knows it was considered.

Open questions (required, write "none" if true): none. No decision on this branch was the user's to make — all four tickets' contracts were self-consistent and none conflicted with the launch prompt, so nothing needed coordinator adjudication. The one thing worth the user's eye is not a question but a consequence: **SONNY-30 adds prompting**, and its manual items say so explicitly.

Cycle-2 fix round, and cycle-3 residual close (PR #41 review, 2026-08-10):

The cycle-1 reviewer confirmed the recursion argument, reproduced all fifteen mutations, and independently re-derived the `try?` population — those are settled. It also found two HIGH defects, and both were in the *new* rule rather than in what it replaced.

**A cut rule has two failure directions, and the first draft only defended one.** SONNY-34 replaced "any duplicate ends the unit" for the old anchored switch, which fixes the genuine repeat (`[scan, zip, scan, zip]`) and breaks the fragment (`[scan, scan, zip]`). The distinction matters here specifically because **no adapter gates on its companion step being present** — a bare `[scan]`, `[scan_docx]` or `[fetch]` manufactures a default and acts. Measured: a second archive nobody asked for; a PDF written into the source folder instead of the requested output folder; and, worst, `[open_hn, fetch, fetch]` opening the browser twice and reporting two saves to one path, the second silently over the first at tier 2 — the branch's own defect class, in the one shape SONNY-35's suffixing cannot reach, because neither fragment carries a `.write_markdown` step so no `outputPath` is ever resolved to compare. The rule is now **a repeat cuts the run only when what follows covers exactly the operations the unit already covers**; anything else is absorbed and the adapter's `.first(where:)` drops it, which is what the whole-plan call did before this branch and is right. The reusable lesson: *when a rewrite changes how work is grouped, the shapes worth testing are the ones the old rule handled correctly, not only the ones it got wrong.* The branch's own test for the rule used two complete units — the case where the new rule was right — and its doc comment and this changelog both cited the broken shape as an improvement.

**A negative claim is only as good as the enumeration behind it, and "cannot be pinned" is a negative claim.** `chainSegments`' guard was recorded as unreachable without a source change; a plan with no steps reached it through `preview`, `prepare` and `assessRisk`, turning a benign baseline no-op into a throw. The enumeration behind the claim had covered "exactly one unit" and never asked about zero. Zero now passes through — restoring baseline — and the guard refuses only the count that can actually recurse. **Rejecting an empty plan outright was considered and declined**: it may be the right product answer, but no ticket here asked for it, and it is reachable in practice (`RoutineStore.save` accepts a routine with no steps, and `RunRoutineCapabilityAdapter` previews and assesses that empty nested plan), so making it an error would be the same unrequested behavior change the other HIGH was about. Both the zero case and the empty-routine path are now pinned from the outside.

**Two documents whose names differ only in case are one file, and a `Set<String>` does not know that.** The docx uniqueness comparison was an exact Swift string match while the volume underneath it is case-insensitive APFS, so `Report.docx` and `report.docx` each claimed a destination that looked free, neither was flagged as renamed, and the run aborted at the converter — the precise pre-fix failure, plus pre-fix silence. Comparison now folds case through a shared `DestinationKey`, used by both uniquifiers. **They deliberately keep different policies** — the docx side must also avoid names already on disk, the executor side must never consult disk or it would suppress the tier-3 "output already exists" escalation — but they must agree on what "the same destination" means. The row-B prediction that `PathWhitelist`'s case-sensitivity lesson would resurface arrived here.

**A fold is a bound, not an approximation, and the difference is which direction it errs.** The first version of `DestinationKey` claimed to be "an approximation of the filesystem's own folding … deliberately the conservative side" — conservative meaning it might over-fold and never under-fold. Cycle 3 measured the opposite: `String.lowercased()` leaves `ß` alone and maps `SS` to `ss`, so `Straße.pdf` and `STRASSE.pdf` are two keys here and one file on disk, and that input class still aborts the batch. Filed as **SONNY-79**; the claim is now written as an explicit three-part bound (caught / already caught by `String` itself / not caught) in `DestinationKey`, and `MockDocumentConverter.convert`'s "collisions can no longer arise at all" — the one place the cycle-2 round's own "gone from all three places" claim was wrong about itself — carries the same bound. **Nothing is destroyed either way**: both converters refuse an occupied destination, which is exactly why this is a follow-up and not a blocker. The reusable point: *when a comment says which way an approximation errs, that direction is a claim to measure, not a reassurance to write.*

**Swift's `String` already compares canonical equivalents equal, so normalising before hashing does nothing.** `DestinationKey` ran `precomposedStringWithCanonicalMapping` and a comment credited it with handling NFC-versus-NFD; `"café" == "café"` is already true across forms because `String` equality and hashing are canonical-equivalence-based, so the call was a no-op for the `Set<String>` it served. Removed rather than kept as belt-and-braces: a call that reads like the mechanism and is not is worse than no call, and the useful fact — that the standard library covers this and the fold's job is case alone — is what the comment now says.

**A guard that appears twice in one function needs a mutation for each occurrence.** `unclaimedOutputPath` consults its claimed set on entry and again per candidate; M7 mutated the candidate half only, and the reviewer showed the entry half is the more dangerous one — teaching it about the filesystem bumps a first destination that already exists to `-2`, which is exactly how the escalation would be suppressed — and that the suite did not notice. Pinned now, by a test with a first generated destination that already exists.

**The branch's one deliberately added interruption said nothing a person could read.** SONNY-30 made a store load failure reachable from the approval gate for the first time; the string that reached the user was "The operation couldn't be completed. (CryptoKit.CryptoKitError error 3.)", because `AES.GCM` throws a type with no `LocalizedError` conformance and `AgentViewModel`'s generic catch renders `localizedDescription` into both the task error and task history. Fixed at the source rather than at the call site: `LocalStorageEncryption.decode` now wraps every read-back failure as `LocalStorageEncryptionError.undecodableLocalData`, whose description is `publishLocalStorageLoadError`'s own load/decrypt wording per `CLAUDE.md`'s gotcha, keeping the raw failure as an associated value for logs. Every store gains the honest copy, not just the two adapters. Also from the same finding: `findRoutine`/`findWorkspace` validate the name *before* loading, because `try loadAll()[normalizedName(...)]` reads as one line but evaluates the subscript's base first — so the recorded "a blank name still throws `.missingName`" was true only of a healthy store, which is not the store anyone reads that sentence about.

Records corrected in this round, each because it was measured false rather than because it read poorly: the `[open_hn, fetch, fetch]` example above; "pairwise distinct"; "hangs the app" (the chain walk is synchronous — native-stack growth ending in a crash); "no two DOCX records ever share a destination" (per scan, and case-folded); "that guard cannot be pinned from outside"; SONNY-35's "fixing two adapters would have left five" (the genuinely first-match resolvers are four — the two named plus `WebResearchMarkdownCapabilityAdapter`'s two `firstIndex` sites; `RunningAppSwitchCapabilityAdapter` already looped); and "six further `try?` sites in `AgentActionExecutor.swift`", which is seven at all three places it was written. One correction could not be made in place: commit `52b8ba0`'s message carries the "six", and pushed history is not rewritten here, so the correction lives on SONNY-30 and SONNY-78.

Also closed in this round, from the reviewer's non-finding notes: the positional `zip(_:_:)` mapping in `resolveDefaultOutputs` now asserts its own invariant, matching `chainSegments`' precedent rather than leaving one of the two unguarded; and `FakeDocumentConverter` refuses an occupied destination like both shipped converters, so the double stops being the only converter in the process that would silently clobber.

**Giving an error honest copy can make a banner say it twice.** SONNY-30's wrapped load failure took `publishLocalStorageLoadError`'s own sentence as its description — which was the point — but that function *also* hardcoded the sentence and then appended a per-source detail built from `error.localizedDescription`. So a single corrupt store rendered "Sonny could not load encrypted local data. A local data file exists but could not be decrypted or decoded. saved routines: A local data file exists but could not be decrypted or decoded.", and the per-source detail, which had been the one distinguishing thing in the banner back when the underlying errors were raw CryptoKit codes, degraded into a repeat of the line above it. The headline now names the kind of problem and each detail names its store and *its own* reason, which stays distinguishable when two stores fail for different reasons. **Every existing assertion on that banner used `contains`, which passes whether a sentence appears once or twice** — the new pin counts occurrences at exactly one affected store, since with two the sentence legitimately appears twice and a count would be pinning the store count instead of the defect. (PR #41 cycle-3, R4.)

Tests after the round: **827 in 60 suites**, exit 0, at `496bc2e` — 10 added, none removed or weakened. Six further mutations (N1–N6), one per fix, each killing exactly the pins it should; recorded on the tickets.

Next branch: none opened by this session. E12's ratified cleanup sequence ends here, so vision work (§B1's vision-envelope consent, which reuses `feature/prebuilt-plan-dispatch`'s mechanism) is what the roadmap points at next; nothing in this branch starts it. The three discovery tickets above are the user's to triage.

**The collision method is the entry's durable lesson: classify each ratified decision as accuracy-hedge vs. authority before touching it.** Every §E decision was re-read under "the models are always right": constraints that existed because a model might be wrong (eval bars, benchmark gates, per-session pre-screening) dissolve; constraints that exist because an action carries authority (consent to control an app, egress transparency, unattended-never, terminals-never) survive at full strength; and the two places the directive argued for loosening an *authority* constraint (the per-session envelope, the launch whitelist) went to the founder as named supersessions rather than being absorbed silently. Twelve rulings, one pass, recorded as §F.

**Enumerate before you dissolve.** The whitelist-dissolution tickets were written only after a compiler-adjacent sweep of every `MacAppCatalog` call site (87 lines / 21 files at `a51acd0`, union of type-name and property-name greps — the property-name grep alone surfaced a site the type-name grep missed). It also found `switch_running_app` already open-universe — a dissolution ticket nobody needs to write — and two adjacent rosters (`AppSearchURLCatalog`, kept deliberately as an audited-template boundary; `WorkspaceBrowserCatalog`, whose Arc/Firefox/Edge entries become live once launch membership stops pre-filtering its inputs).

**A quote is a quote or it is a paraphrase — the founder caught the difference at ratification.** The proposal attributed "never a security boundary, only a launchability roster" to `WorkspaceScopeOnlyApps.swift:14-18`; the file's real words at `:8` are "exactly one meaning — the allowlist of what Sonny may *launch*". Committed text (plan §F) now quotes the real sentence and marks the roster reading as paraphrase. Same class as the repo's markup-tolerant-grep rule: prose evidence gets verified against the source's literal bytes before it is cited.

**A ticket-label gap needs an explicit no-loss note.** The proposal's draft V-series skipped V4 (the §12.5 prompt-injection defense) because it was folded into the vision-session ticket — its three elements (untrusted-content wrapping per the repo's existing `UNTRUSTED_OBSERVED_CONTENT` mechanism, the automated red-team fixture, the conventions-doc update) all live in SONNY-92's contract. Reviewed by: pending — fresh session per WORKFLOW.md step 7.

Files changed (29 files across `242e82e`, `73c21fc`, `7f88940`, `84e418a`, `afa2a36`, plus the cycle-1 fix round `429f30b`, `2211fbd`, `42d1d96`, `e39ad8a`, `7944efe`):
- `Sources/MacAgentCore/InstalledAppResolver.swift` (new — the seam, its live Launch-Services source, and the fixed test source)
- `Sources/MacAgentCore/OpenAppCapabilityAdapter.swift` (new; replaces `OpenAllowlistedAppCapabilityAdapter.swift`, deleted)
- `Sources/MacAgentCore/MacAppService.swift` (catalog demoted to an alias table; `MacAppCatalogError` → `MacAppError`, `appNotAllowed` deleted, `notInstalled` added; icon resolver widened)
- `Sources/MacAgentCore/{CapabilityAdapter,AgentActionExecutor}.swift` (the `installedAppResolver` seam and its DI)
- `Sources/MacAgentCore/{OpenWorkspace,RunRoutine,CreateWorkspace,EditWorkspace}CapabilityAdapter.swift`, `WorkspaceScopeOnlyApps.swift`, `WorkspaceScope.swift`, `AppWebsiteActionDescriptors.swift`, `InstantCommandResolver.swift`, `DefaultCapabilityAdapters.swift`

- `Sources/MacAgentCore/{WorkspaceBrowserOpener,CapabilityAdapter}.swift` (two strings the dissolution left false, caught by a pre-PR sweep — see below)
- `Tests/MacAgentCoreTests/InstalledAppResolverTests.swift` (new) plus ten existing test files
Tests: **858 in 62 suites, exit 0** at `7944efe`, via the flagged command in `CLAUDE.md`. Baseline **828 in 60 suites, exit 0 at `0fdac1c`** — *measured*, in a detached worktree at that SHA, rather than inferred from the previous branch's entry: +30 tests, none weakened. **38 non-comment, non-blank deletion lines under `Tests/` branch-wide** at `4772ea5` (41 at `7944efe`, the fix round having rewritten more copy), essentially all of them rewritten copy assertions and renamed test declarations; the SONNY-84 window alone (`7f88940..84e418a`) has zero, which is the narrower claim that criterion asked for. Fifteen mutations across the three tickets, each recorded on its ticket with the tests it killed.

What changed for the user: **any app installed on this Mac now opens by name.** "Open Figma", "open Discord", "open Xcode" — previously all refused with "«name» is not in the allowlisted app catalog." — now launch, and an app that genuinely is not installed says so ("«name» isn't installed on this Mac.") instead of implying a permission problem. Workspaces launch their non-catalog entries instead of silently skipping them, a workspace naming Arc, Firefox or Edge finally opens its URLs in that browser, and a workspace row listing a non-catalog app shows the app's real icon. Nothing gained a new prompt: `open_app` is tier 1 before and after.

Behavior preserved (required, no blanket claims): the risk engine is untouched — `AgentRunner.swift`, `RiskApproval.swift` and the four-value verdict model are absent from the diff, `open_app` is still tier 1 with `app_opening` as its only requirement, and every plan still passes the same `prepare → assessRisk → execute` gate. `AppSearchURLCatalog`'s five templates are deliberately unwidened. `AgentPlan`'s schema, `AgentPlanDecoder.stepKeys`, `plannerVisibleCases` and the §A4 agreement pin are unchanged. No store schema, `Codable` shape or migration is touched. Every cataloged app keys, previews, groups and matches exactly as before — pinned by the entire pre-existing scope suite passing with zero assertions modified.

Architectural decisions / pitfalls discovered (required, write "none" if true):

**Replace the resolution authority; never delete it.** C12 said this and the code proves why. The catalog's name→bundle-id table is load-bearing for `WorkspaceScope`'s anti-imposter keys, so `appKey` is now three stages — alias table, then Launch Services, then the normalized raw name — and **the order is the security property, not a preference**. The alias stage stays *unconditional*: a workspace listing Chrome on a Mac without Chrome still keys to `bundle:com.google.Chrome`, because a stage that deferred to installation would drop it to `name:chrome`, where an app that merely calls itself Chrome would match. The third stage stays reachable for the same reason SONNY-44 gave: removing an entry from matching is the one outcome a boundary must never produce. Dropping it kills ten tests across **five** files (`AgentActionExecutorTests`, `EditWorkspaceTests`, `InstalledAppResolverTests`, `SwitchInWorkspaceRoutingTests`, `WorkspaceScopeTests`).

**An identity taken from the imposter cannot be used to exclude the imposter.** The resolver reads the Launch Services database and never `NSRunningApplication`. That negative is pinned *structurally* — a comment-stripped source scan asserting the resolution file calls no running-process API — because `CLAUDE.md`'s enumerate-before-you-subtract rule means a negative cannot be established from one inspected path. The same technique proves `appNotAllowed` and its sentence exist nowhere in `Sources/` or `Tests/` as code. Both sweeps strip comments first: several files legitimately record in prose what was removed, and a sweep that could not tell a comment from a call would make documenting an invariant break the test that pins it. (Both failed on exactly that on the first run.)

**A machine-dependent default is a test suite that passes here and fails there.** `InstalledAppResolver.shared` is live Launch Services in the app and a fixed source holding the alias table's roster under XCTest — the shape and reasoning of `LocalStorageEncryption.defaultKeyManager()`, whose `isRunningTests` probe it copies rather than shares. The roster is chosen over an empty source deliberately: it makes the test-process universe *byte-identical to the pre-dissolution catalog*, so 828 existing tests keep the answers they were written against while a test about the open universe injects the app it means. An empty default would have churned 110 `open_app` references across 23 test files; a live default would have made scope tests depend on whether this particular Mac has Xcode.

**Widen the runtime first, then the vocabulary — and never only the vocabulary.** SONNY-82 left the `open_app` tool description saying "Supported apps: …" on purpose, with the planner goldens as its never-touch list, so that the boundary was provable by diff. SONNY-83 then widened it. The reverse order would have been the worse half to ship alone; but so would stopping after SONNY-82, because that description reaches the model verbatim and a model honouring a stale list drops "open Figma" *at the source*, where no amount of runtime capability recovers a step that was never emitted. The description states the widening and then forbids the two failure modes negatively — do not substitute, do not drop — after `CreateWorkspaceCapabilityAdapter`'s own widened description, which solved the identical problem for workspace apps in SONNY-44.

**A capability's widening can silently disable a check that depended on its narrowness.** The instant resolver's app-name ambiguity boolean existed to step aside to the planner when a saved workspace or routine name collides with an app name. Because it asked the *catalog*, making Figma openable was exactly what made a workspace called "Figma" stop disambiguating from the app Figma — the collision it exists to catch became invisible to it at the moment the collision became real. Nothing failed; the check just quietly stopped firing. Worth generalizing: when a set widens, audit every predicate that was *reading* that set, not only the ones enforcing it.

**Run mutation batteries against a committed tree.** A battery here restored its mutations with `git checkout <file>` while the ticket's own edits to those files were still uncommitted, which reverted the work instead of the mutation and made one mutation read as "killed nothing". The tell was the result itself — a mutation that kills no tests is a finding, so it got chased rather than recorded. (SONNY-83's closing comment carries this too.)

**Sweep for the language a dissolution leaves behind, and sort it into history and lies.** A pre-PR sweep for surviving "allowlist" language over `Sources/` returns **24 matching lines across 13 files** at `84e418a`, the pre-fix tree, and **23 across 13** at `4772ea5` once the two live ones were fixed. (Pattern `(?<![A-Za-z])allow[\W_]{0,3}list\w*|Allow[\W_]{0,3}[Ll]ist\w*`, unit = matching *lines*. Both halves earn their keep: without the lookbehind, "sh**allow** **list**ing" is a hit; without the camelCase alternative, `OpenAllowlistedAppCapabilityAdapter` is not.) Nine of those lines are the search-URL boundary, which is deliberately still an allowlist. Of the fifteen that remained at `84e418a`, thirteen were history — files recording what was removed and why, worth keeping. Two were live claims that had quietly become false: the `app_opening` permission's *user-facing* description still said "Sonny may ask macOS to open an allowlisted app", and `WorkspaceBrowserCatalog`'s header still described its set as "which allowlisted apps count as browsers" while explaining its Arc/Firefox/Edge entries as anticipating a launch catalog that would grow. The second is the more interesting one, because the honest replacement is not merely a correction: that set is deliberately *still* a fixed five after the dissolution, since it answers "will a URL sensibly land in it" rather than "may Sonny open it", and probing Launch Services for `http` handlers would pull in every Electron app, mail client and PDF reader that registers one. A dissolution's cleanup pass is not find-and-replace — each surviving mention is either a record or a bug, and the two look identical to a grep.

**Scope-only narrowed; the disclosure had to narrow with it.** `WorkspaceScopeOnlyApps` used to mean "outside the catalog" and now means "not installed", so all three of its sentences changed from "isn't an app Sonny can launch" to "isn't installed on this Mac" — more actionable as well as more true, since the old wording left a user with an installed app nothing to do. The type survives intact: the SONNY-44 decoupling decision it encodes is untouched, and its class of problem did not empty out — a Mac without Word still needs Word listable for `convert_docx_to_pdf`'s implicit resource. Its fourth reader, the add dialog's live disclosure in `Sources/MacAgent/`, had to move with it or the dialog and the capability would say different things about the same app.

Known limitations / deferred scope:
- The live `LaunchServicesAppSource` matches on the application bundle's **file name**, not `CFBundleDisplayName`. That is the name macOS itself shows for effectively every app, and the alias table covers the known divergences; reading a plist per installed bundle on a path `WorkspaceScope` walks for every stored entry of every risk assessment was judged not worth it. An app whose Finder name differs from its bundle file name resolves only through the alias table.
- The installed-app index is cached for 15 seconds. Installing an app and immediately asking Sonny to open it can miss by up to that much.
- Launching by name in an open universe means an app installed under a name is the app that opens under that name. Stated in `InstalledAppResolver`'s doc comment and pinned by a test; it is tier-1 work under C12, and the preview discloses the bundle identifier and install location before it happens. It cannot leak *scope* membership, because `appKey` consults the alias table before the resolver.
- Non-catalog removal grouping improves only where the resolver proves one identity across two different strings (two copies of one app on disk). Spelling variants already grouped — both key forms fold through the same normalization — which is narrower than SONNY-84's contract wording implies, and is recorded on that ticket.

Open questions (required, write "none" if true): none.

**Cycle-1 review corrections (2026-08-13, appended — the claims above are corrected in place and recorded here).** PR #44's cycle-1 review returned 0 HIGH, 2 MEDIUM, 7 LOW; every item was fixed in-branch. The two MEDIUM findings are recorded as decisions above and on SONNY-83/84. The LOWs were all refuted *counts* — this entry's own numbers — and each is re-measured below with the method and the SHA it was measured at, because a bare corrected number is indistinguishable from the bare wrong one it replaces:

- **"ten tests across four files"** (the name-fallback mutation's blast radius) → **five** files. Re-measured by re-running that mutation at the fix-round head and grouping the failures by their declaring file; the fifth is `SwitchInWorkspaceRoutingTests.swift`, which the original grouping missed.
- **"zero non-comment deletions anywhere in `Tests/`"** → true only of the SONNY-84 window (`7f88940..84e418a`), which is where it was measured; **branch-wide it is 38** non-comment non-blank deletion lines at `4772ea5`. The sentence generalized a narrow measurement into a branch-wide one. Method: `git diff <base>..<head> -- Tests/`, `-` lines, minus `---` headers, minus comment-only and blank lines.
- **"nine existing test files"** → **ten**. Method: `git diff --name-status 0fdac1c..HEAD -- Tests/`, counting `M` entries.
- **"twelve hits"** for the pre-PR allowlist sweep → the sweep returns **24** matching lines across 13 files at `84e418a` (the tree it was actually run against) and **23** at `4772ea5`. Twelve was the residue *after* a filter that already excluded the search-URL lines and one line about to be fixed: the number described what was left to triage and was written as what the sweep found, with the filter never named. Re-measuring it twice produced two more wrong numbers before the right one, which is the part worth recording — a first pass read 24 at `4772ea5` because a markup-tolerant `allow[\W_]{0,3}list` also matches "sh**allow** **list**ing", and a second read 22 because adding a leading word boundary to fix that then dropped the camelCase `OpenAllowlistedAppCapabilityAdapter`. The pattern that is right in both directions is `(?<![A-Za-z])allow[\W_]{0,3}list\w*|Allow[\W_]{0,3}[Ll]ist\w*`, and it is now the prompt-vocabulary guard's pattern, with both halves' reasons written at the call site.
- **Manual-checklist attribution** — the aggregate was credited to SONNY-82 and SONNY-84; the fourth item is **SONNY-83's**, and SONNY-84 declares no manual items at all.

The pattern across all five is one habit, not five slips: a number measured in one scope, then written in a wider one. Every count above now carries its scope, its method and its SHA in the same sentence.

Manual checklist for the founder (aggregated from SONNY-82's three items and SONNY-83's one; SONNY-84 declares none):
1. Open an installed non-catalog app (Discord, Figma, Xcode) **by typed command and by voice**. Expect it to launch, with "Opened the «Name» app."
2. Ask for an app that is not installed. Expect **"«Name» isn't installed on this Mac."** — not a refusal about an allowlist.
3. Open a workspace listing an installed non-catalog app. Expect it to launch alongside the rest; a listed app that is not installed should be skipped, with the summary reading "… «Name» isn't installed and was not opened."
4. A command naming an installed non-catalog app — e.g. "open discord and check the news" — should produce a plan whose `open_app` step names Discord. This is planner judgment no unit test can pin; watch for the model substituting a nearby app it recognizes, or dropping the app step entirely.
5. Optional, not a ticket requirement: a workspace row listing an installed non-catalog app should now show that app's real icon rather than a generic glyph.

Architectural decisions / pitfalls discovered (required, write "none" if true):

**The substance lives in `docs/sonny-row-c-plan.md` and on the five contracts, deliberately.** That document is the frozen rationale — the evidence, the alternatives declined, and the adversarial pass that reshaped the design; the Plane tickets are what binds. Restating either here would create a third copy that drifts. What follows is only what a reader of this changelog needs in order to know whether to go read them. **The design in three sentences:** being inside a workspace's scope finally *reduces* prompting — in-scope tier 2 auto-runs and in-scope tier 3 drops to a lightweight confirmation (the founder charter of 2026-08-04) — plus a second, narrower grant for plans the user built field-by-field on a UI surface (`PreparedPlanSource.directUserAction`); both grants resolve in **one** exhaustive `(effectiveTier, grant)` mapping that also takes Safe mode (row H) and, later, per-app control consent (row I) as *inputs*, never as chained rules; and relaxation is a requirement override that never writes `effectiveTier`, `escalations` or `approvalCopy`, where only `.inScope` ever qualifies, out-of-scope and `.opaque` never relax, and escalate-never-block and re-arm-on-drift are both preserved.

**The founder's own friction complaint was structurally unreachable by the ratified charter — which is why the origin grant is load-bearing rather than gold-plating.** `edit_workspace` and `create_workspace` classify as `.none` scoped resources (`PlanScopedResources.swift:249-259` at `0fdac1c`), because a workspace's apps, URLs and file locations in an edit are *contents being declared*, not resources being touched. So a workspace-sheet edit produces zero scope findings, rolls up `.unconstrained`, and can never be `.inScope` — the verdict half of the charter cannot reach the 2026-08-07 complaint at all. Anyone later tempted to drop the origin term as an optional enhancement should read that paragraph (§1.1) before doing it.

**Half the ratified charter has no observable effect until branch 2, and the records are required to say so rather than claim a friction win.** `.lightweightConfirmation` and `.explicitApproval` render identically on every surface today: the single `switch request.requirement` in the whole `MacAgent` target merges them (`AgentViewModel.swift:813`), `WidgetPermissionPanel` never reads `.requirement` at all (`FloatingWidgetView.swift:532-630`), and Command Center shows a fixed "Approval needed" header (`CommandCenterView.swift:751`). SONNY-10's audit reached the same conclusion from the other direction on 2026-08-03. That is the entire reason row C is two branches; the friction branch 1 really delivers is in-scope tier-2 auto-run, and SONNY-97's contract forbids its closing comment from claiming otherwise.

**"`effectiveTier` stays honest" is not a sufficient invariant, and row B's four-item consumer list was incomplete.** `effectiveTier` is read or referenced 151 times across 24 files at `0fdac1c`, and there is a fifth structural decision gate nobody listed — SONNY-54's manual-routine-trust check at `AgentViewModel.swift:822-823`, whose own comment says it "mirrors `AgentRunner.execute`'s own gate." Separately, the `escalations` array's *contents* render independently on three surfaces (`FloatingWidgetView.swift:546-550`, `CommandCenterView.swift:777`, `AgentViewModel.swift:2404-2422`), so a relaxation could keep the tier arithmetically honest and still change what the user reads. Hence **I2**, the ratified upgrade to the inherited constraint: relaxation never writes `escalations` **and never changes `approvalCopy`** — it changes the *weight* of the ask, never the sentence. This is the class `CLAUDE.md`'s *enumerate before you subtract* rule exists for; SONNY-100 corrects the count everywhere it is stated as a closed list.

**The adversarial pass found four blocking defects in the drafted design, and each is now a contract clause rather than a lesson.** (1) The eligibility roll-up had *no data path into the mapping function* — `requirement(for:context:)` receives only the assessment and the context, so its own fail-closed containment was uncomputable as drafted; the fix is a `relaxationEligibility` field on `CapabilityRiskAssessment`, folded inside `AgentActionExecutor.assessRisk` exactly where `scopeVerdict` is folded today, with plan roll-up as **intersection across every step** and adapters able to narrow but never widen. (2) A **third** public tier→requirement path existed, not two: `RiskApprovalPolicy.requirement(for tier:)` (`RiskApproval.swift:77`) is public, tier-only and context-free — row B's "never two chained rules" violation in miniature — and it survives untouched if you only remove the two thin wrappers that call it. (3) Row C *breaks* re-arm-on-drift rather than merely sitting alongside it: the gate at `AgentRunner.swift:166-181` authorizes solely on `approvedTier >= effectiveTier`, which was sound only while requirement was a pure function of tier, so a lightweight consent can now be spent as an explicit approval at equal tier — and separately, the only branch turning a drifted `approvalRequired` into a graceful pending approval is gated `where routineTrustApproval != .notRequested`, a condition a row-C auto-run never satisfies, so today's code would hard-fail instead of re-prompting. (4) The origin grant would have made a workspace-*widening* edit silent — see below. All four were folded in before ratification; none is a hypothetical.

**The row-B forward flag on widening is answered, and the answer is one sentence with a real hazard behind it.** `edit_workspace` is the single operation on the origin grant's allowlist, its floor is tier 2, and its `assessRisk` escalates only on removal — so a pure *addition* would have produced `escalations: []`, stayed tier 2, and auto-run silently, turning the one edit the flag warned about into the one edit nobody sees. The ratified rule: **the origin grant covers edits that move entries *within* a boundary; it never covers an edit that changes *whether a dimension is a boundary at all*.** On top of it, adding a `PathWhitelist` root as a file location escalates to tier 3 — `.fileLocation` only, with the `.app`/`.webDomain` asymmetry recorded as a stated decision rather than left to read as a bug. SONNY-100 closes the flag's four recordings as pointers to the answer rather than deletions.

**A prerequisite that is a sequencing constraint, not a merge conflict.** `WorkspaceScope.verdict(for: .resolvedApp(...))` falls back to a normalized display-name key, so an app that merely *calls itself* "Figma" can earn `.inScope`; before row C the consequence was "no escalation", after row C it becomes silent execution. Stated honestly rather than inflated: the two compose safely today only **by coincidence of tier placement** — the sole producer of a `.resolvedApp` scoped resource is tier 1, which no grant column touches. **SONNY-84 (row F) is therefore a merge prerequisite for SONNY-97 and for that ticket only**, recorded on both tickets, and row C never touches `WorkspaceScope.swift`.

**The escalation-site count in the durable decision record was stale, and correcting it is what a quantified claim costs.** `docs/sonny-founder-design-decisions.md` said "all six escalation sites say some form of 'X already exists and would be replaced.'" There are **8** `CapabilityRiskEscalation` construction sites in `Sources/` and **all 8 raise to tier 3** — row B itself added the other two (`AgentActionExecutor.swift:458`'s out-of-scope escalation and `EditWorkspaceCapabilityAdapter.swift:228`'s removal escalation), and they are exactly the two that break the phrasing generalisation. Scope, method and SHA, per this repo's rule: syntactic construction sites in `Sources/` only, from `grep -rn "CapabilityRiskEscalation(" Sources/`, cross-checked against a `toTier:` sweep (10 lines; the two extras are the type's own property and `init` in `RiskApproval.swift`, and no site anywhere sets a `toTier` other than `.tier3`), against `.init`/factory/`escalations`-array indirection, and against a dead-code and compile-flag pass — first taken at `0fdac1c` and **re-measured at `ae1ca3c`** after the rebase, since row F touched three of the eight files. The count is 8 at both SHAs; three line numbers moved, which is the whole reason this repo stamps a measurement instead of stating it bare. Two things the number alone hides: what was ever load-bearing for row B's gate 3 was the *tier*, not the sentence, and that half held at both counts; and a site is not a runtime escalation, since `EditWorkspaceCapabilityAdapter.swift:228` sits in a `compactMap` over the edit's lists and can emit one per scoped-resource kind. The frozen `docs/sonny-branch-b-plan.md` and row B's own changelog entry still say six and are **left as written** — both are history stamped with the tree they were measured against, and this repo records corrections rather than quietly editing them.

**How the plan was produced, recorded because the method is the reason its claims can be spot-checked rather than trusted.** A six-lens read-only grounding sweep over the requirement-mapping surface, `effectiveTier`/`escalations` readers, all escalation sites, the scope model, the approval UI and the test surface — each with an independent refutation pass over its *negative* claims — followed by a five-lens adversarial attack on the drafted design (security regression, the composition trap, unattended/drift, product coherence, type plumbing), each finding adjudicated against the real tree. Every measurement in the plan carries `0fdac1c`. **One counting trap worth carrying forward:** the suite is 828 `@Test` functions across 53 files, all swift-testing, zero XCTest; `grep -c "func test"` returns 4 and all four are private helpers, so counting that way undercounts the regression surface by roughly 200×.

Known limitations / deferred scope:

- **Nothing implemented.** All five contracts are unstarted. SONNY-62 is In Progress on its own branch, ahead of everything.
- **Branch 1 was gated on two merges**, both recorded on the tickets: SONNY-84 (row F, the imposter-gap discharge) and SONNY-62 (it edits the same guard in `AgentRunner.execute` that SONNY-97 extends, so the two are serial over one function, not parallel). **SONNY-84 landed while this branch was open** — PR #44 merged at `ae1ca3c`, and this branch rebased onto it — so SONNY-62 is the only prerequisite still outstanding at this entry's date.
- **Branch 2 carries a founder-wireframe dependency** — the approval panel is the widget's, System B — and until it ships, branch 1's tier-3 clause has no user-visible effect at all. Stated rather than hidden, and written into SONNY-97's non-goals.
- **The `.runRoutine` exclusion's cost is accepted, not overlooked:** a user may ask why their routine still confirms inside a workspace where typing the same command does not. SONNY-54's per-routine trust toggle stays the single door, and SONNY-97's contract tells its implementer not to "fix" the asymmetry.
- **I6 is a pinned property, not a structural guarantee.** The unattended path reaches neither grant only because the scheduled dispatch makes two independent call-site choices — `source: .instantResolver` and `scope: .unscoped` (`AgentViewModel.swift:2283-2315`) — which is precisely why it gets a test named for the hazard.
- **A stale count survives in `Sources/` and is deliberately not fixed here.** `CreateWorkspaceCapabilityAdapter.swift:122` (`:113` before the row F rebase) says "all six existing escalation sites raise a tier". The count is stale for the same reason the decision record's was; the claim it is actually making — that every escalation site *raises* a tier — holds at 8. Fixing it would put a `Sources/` path in a docs-only diff and forfeit this branch's step-7 exemption, so it is recorded here for whoever next edits that file.
- **SONNY-100's contract asks for two edits its own scope lines forbid**, found while re-measuring for the count correction above and recorded rather than resolved here. Its requirement 4 — with an acceptance criterion reading "*every* place stating 'four things depend on `effectiveTier`' as a closed list is corrected" — names the doc comment at `AgentActionExecutor.swift:293-298`, which its never-touch list forbids outright ("all of `Sources/` and `Tests/`"), and `docs/sonny-branch-b-plan.md` §4's table, which its expected-touched-areas line bounds to "forward-flag closure pointer only — it is a frozen artifact, so touch nothing else in it". A contract whose requirements cannot all hold is a stop-and-report for SONNY-100's own session under WORKFLOW.md step 4, not a judgment call it gets to make — and the frozen-artifact half likely resolves the same way row B's history did above: record the correction where the work lands, leave the stamped history as written.

Run parallel with row F (`feature/app-catalog-dissolution`), disjointness recorded on both sides before either started: row F's three tickets put `AgentRunner.swift` and `RiskApproval.swift` on their never-touch lists explicitly, and this branch touches nothing in row F's expected areas. Reviewed by: pending — this entry is written pre-PR by the implementing session, per WORKFLOW.md step 7's ordering. Before opening the PR the session ran its own adversarial pass over the committed diff (four independent lenses — gate correctness, reachability across every `RiskApprovalDecision` construction and match site, test integrity, and every factual claim in the new comments — each finding then handed to a separate reviewer instructed to refute it). One finding survived refutation, and it is recorded above and fixed in `400b8c7`: the empty-acknowledged-set reachability claim. **That pass is a self-check, not the step-7 review** — it read the same session's own work.

Files changed:
- `Sources/MacAgentCore/RiskApproval.swift` (the `RiskApprovalConsent` model and the rule)
- `Sources/MacAgentCore/AgentRunner.swift` (the stale-approval gate, plus one trace line)

Tests: `env CLANG_MODULE_CACHE_PATH="$PWD/.build/clang-module-cache" swift test --disable-sandbox -Xswiftc -F -Xswiftc /Library/Developer/CommandLineTools/Library/Developer/Frameworks -Xlinker -rpath -Xlinker /Library/Developer/CommandLineTools/Library/Developer/Frameworks -Xlinker -rpath -Xlinker /Library/Developer/CommandLineTools/Library/Developer/usr/lib` -> exit 0, **874 tests in 62 suites** at `04ce7e4`, against a base of **858 in 62** re-measured on `main` at `8629881` in a detached worktree. Both ends were re-measured after the rebase rather than arithmetically adjusted, and the delta corroborates independently: **+16 runtime tests, and exactly 16 `@Test` declarations added with 0 removed** across `git diff origin/main...HEAD -- Tests/`. **Every figure in this entry from before the rebase describes a tree that no longer exists** — see the rebase note at the end of this entry for the SHA map. Pre-rebase, the branch measured **844 in 60** at `ffa007f` over a **828 in 60** base (`main` at `0fdac1c`), and **843 in 60** at `400b8c7`/`042f74e` before PR #46's fix round added its test. `swift build` -> exit 0. Eight mutations (M1–M8) run against the committed tree at `990ea44`, every one killed by the test named for it; the full battery, including the one result that corrected a claim rather than confirming it, is on SONNY-62's closing comment. **A ninth mutation (M9), written by PR #46's reviewer, survived that battery** — emit `risk.rearmed` on the `.notRequested` path too, so every ordinary first-time approval traces a re-arm that never happened. `aFirstTimeApprovalPromptIsNotTracedAsAReArm` closes it, proved against the committed tree at `ffa007f` (pre-rebase; the mutation battery was not re-run afterwards, and the SHA is named so that stays legible): M9 exits 1 with that test as the **only** failure, and a cruder variant deleting the `if case .approved` gate outright exits 1 with that test plus `anApprovalForOneTierThreeReasonDoesNotAuthorizeADifferentTierThreeReason`.

Behavior changed:
- **An approval now records what the user consented to, not merely how risky the run was when they were asked.** `RiskApprovalDecision.approved` carries a `RiskApprovalConsent` — a tier ceiling plus a `Coverage` — instead of a bare `CapabilityRiskTier`. `Coverage` has exactly two cases, and they say different things: `.acknowledgedReasons(Set<String>)` is one prompt answered by a human who was shown exactly those escalation reasons, and `.standingGrant` is a class of runs pre-authorized ahead of time with no prompt and therefore no reasons to compare against.
- **The stale-approval gate in `AgentRunner.execute` asks `RiskApprovalConsent.authorizes(_:)`**, which is a conjunction: the fresh assessment's effective tier must be at or below the consent's ceiling (unchanged, and still evaluated first), *and* every escalation reason in the fresh assessment must be one the consent acknowledged. Two different tier-3 causes no longer compare equal.
- **`AgentViewModel.performApproval` writes back `.approved(answering: request)`** — tier and reasons read from the one request that was on screen, by a single initializer, so no call site can pair a tier from one assessment with reasons from another.
- **A new `risk.rearmed` trace event** names the reasons a re-arm was not covered by, emitted only when a consent existed to be exceeded — `.notRequested` reaching the gate is the ordinary "this needs approval" path, not a re-arm.
- **`performStart`'s routine-trust check asks the gate instead of restating it.** It was a hand-written copy of the tier comparison; it is now `trustDecision.authorizes(request)`, which is the same predicate the engine enforces. Behaviour is identical for every input it can receive (a standing grant's reason half is vacuous by construction), and the copy that could have drifted is gone.

Behavior preserved (required, no blanket claims):
- **The unattended and routine-trust paths are untouched.** Both are `.approved(.tier2)` standing grants; the static-func overload keeps that spelling meaning exactly what it meant, and a standing grant is still a pure tier ceiling. A scheduled tier-3 plan still hits the structural backstop and pauses its schedule with SONNY-31's notice; a trusted routine run by hand still prompts at tier 3.
- **`.notRequested` still authorizes nothing, and the `.autoRun`/`.previewOnly`/`.refuse` requirement branches are unchanged** — the reason comparison lives only inside the `.lightweightConfirmation`/`.explicitApproval` case, where a consent exists to compare.
- **A tier that rose still re-arms regardless of reasons**, pinned by the pre-existing `staleTierTwoApprovalDoesNotAuthorizeLaterTierThreeEscalation` and by a unit test that can vary tier and reason set independently.

Architectural decisions / pitfalls discovered (required, write "none" if true):

**A consent record is not a risk measurement, and conflating the two is what produced the bug.** `effectiveTier` answers "how much may this cost"; it was never an answer to "what did the user agree to". The whole fix is separating those into different values that happen to travel together, and the shape generalises: row C (SONNY-97) adds the *answered requirement* to the same struct and the same function, because "a lightweight confirmation answered at tier 3, then re-read as an explicit approval at equal tier" is this same masking class arriving through a second door. `authorizes(_:)` takes the whole `RiskApprovalRequest` rather than its assessment for exactly that reason — the requirement is already in hand at the one call site that matters.

**Subset, not equality; exact strings, not normalisation.** A reason that *disappeared* means the world got less risky along that axis, so re-asking would be a prompt with nothing new in it — the rule is "no reason the user never saw", not "the world is byte-identical". But a reason that merely *reads* differently re-arms, because nothing in the engine can tell a cosmetic rewrite from `already exists at /a.md` becoming `already exists at /b.md`: same shape, and only one of them is harmless. The asymmetry is deliberate and is the cheap side of the trade — being wrong toward re-arming costs one extra prompt, and a re-arm provably cannot loop, since the re-arm carries the *fresh* request to the user and the consent recorded from it acknowledges the new reasons.

**Two notions of "no reasons", kept apart — the same lesson branch B recorded about two notions of empty.** `.standingGrant` and `.acknowledgedReasons([])` are both "this consent names no reasons", and collapsing them would have been the natural simplification. They are opposites in what they say: the empty acknowledged set is a *human answering a prompt that named nothing* — which is what every tier-2 confirmation is — and any reason appearing afterwards must re-arm; a standing grant is a tier ceiling with the reason axis simply not applicable, and a reason appearing afterwards must not. **The distinction cannot change an authorize/deny outcome on any assessment the app can currently produce, and the first version of this entry claimed otherwise.** The adversarial review pass caught it: for the two to disagree, a consent would need a tier-3 ceiling with an empty acknowledged set, and no adapter can produce that assessment — all 25 literal `defaultRiskTier` values in `Sources/` at `03e3814` are tier 2 or below (7/8/10 across tiers 0/1/2; the six other `defaultRiskTier:` assignments forward a descriptor's own literal, and the one adapter that varies its default at assessment time, `InvokeShortcut`, only lowers it), and all eight `CapabilityRiskEscalation` construction sites (seven adapters plus the scope evaluator in `AgentActionExecutor.scopeEscalations`) target `.tier3`, so a tier-3 assessment always carries at least one reason and a non-empty reason set always means tier 3. What the two *do* differ in today is the trace — only an answered prompt can emit `risk.rearmed` — and they will differ in outcome the day an adapter defaults to tier 3 or an escalation targets tier 2. Keeping them apart is still right, and stating a reachability it does not have was still wrong.

**Correction, appended 2026-08-13 (PR #46 review, F1) — the paragraph above justifies the right conclusion with a wrong population, and this is the second time this same argument has been corrected.** Retracted: the clause "the one adapter that varies its default at assessment time, `InvokeShortcut`, only lowers it". **Three** sites compute a `defaultTier` at assessment time, not one, and two of them move it in the opposite direction — swept at `04ce7e4` (first at `042f74e`, then re-run in full after the rebase onto row F, which edited several of the files counted here) over every `defaultRiskTier` occurrence and every `CapabilityRiskAssessment` construction site in `Sources/`, rather than over the adapters that looked relevant. `InvokeShortcutCapabilityAdapter.swift:71` picks `.tier1` on a clean run history and its own literal otherwise, so it does only lower; but `RunRoutineCapabilityAdapter.swift:61` and `SaveRoutineCapabilityAdapter.swift:58` each take the *maximum* of their own literal and the nested plan's `defaultTier` (`highestTier(_:_:)`, `first.rawValue >= second.rawValue ? first : second`), and `AgentActionExecutor.swift:326` maximizes across a plan's adapters — a maximum is structurally free to move a default *upward*. The 25-literals count, the 7/8/10 split, the six descriptor forwards (each a `static let` in `AppWebsiteActionDescriptors`, so one of those same literals) and the eight tier-3 escalation sites all re-measured true at `042f74e`, and again unchanged at `04ce7e4` after the rebase — row F renamed `OpenAllowlistedAppCapabilityAdapter` to `OpenAppCapabilityAdapter` and moved line numbers, but moved no adapter's tier. **The conclusion holds, on the argument the retracted clause never made:** a maximum over values that are all tier 2 or below is itself tier 2 or below, and a nested `defaultTier` is another such maximum one level down, so the bound holds at every nesting depth — no `defaultTier` reaches tier 3, and since each assessment forwards the escalations of any plan nested inside it, a tier-3 assessment still always carries at least one reason. The reusable lesson is the one this branch keeps re-learning: an exhaustive claim ("the one adapter that…") is a population claim, and a population claim is only worth what the sweep behind it covered. `Sources/MacAgentCore/RiskApproval.swift`'s own doc comment carries the corrected argument in full.

**The compiler was made the enforcement for the migration, deliberately.** `.approved(CapabilityRiskTier)` became `.approved(RiskApprovalConsent)` with a `static func approved(_ tier:)` overload, so the fifteen pre-existing tier-taking call sites keep compiling untouched — 2 in `Sources/` (`AgentViewModel.swift:1421`, `:2315`, both standing grants) and 13 in `Tests/`, counted at `282a796` over non-comment lines — *and* every `case .approved(let tier)` pattern match breaks loudly, which is the half that needed reviewing. A design where both halves kept compiling would have left a silently-changed pattern match somewhere; a design where neither did would have churned fifteen call sites into a diff nobody could read.

**A mutation battery corrected a claim this session had already written down.** `aTierTwoPromptAnsweredByTheUserDoesNotAuthorizeALaterTierThreeEscalation` was documented as pinning the tier half of the gate. With the tier ceiling deleted from `authorizes(_:)`, it still passed — because a fixed plan's default tier cannot move, so at the runner level a tier only ever rises *by* an escalation, and that escalation's reason arrives in the same assessment, where the reason check alone already catches it. The tier half is unisolatable at that level and is pinned one level down instead. The general form: a test's own doc comment is a claim like any other, and the battery is what checks it — reading the test back does not.

**A battery of eight mutations still missed the one property stated as a negative.** Every mutation this session wrote asked "is this behaviour present" — delete the gate, delete the write-back, delete the trace line — and each died. The property nothing asked about was `risk.rearmed` being *absent* where the code says it must be: M8 deleted the trace line and proved it exists, which is not the same claim. PR #46's reviewer wrote the missing one (M9: emit it on the `.notRequested` path too) and it survived all 843 tests. **A negative property needs a mutation that adds behaviour, not one that removes it** — and the test that kills it has to sit where the mutation is actually observable, which for this one is a tier-3 denial rather than the tier-2 denial the finding first suggested: a tier-2 `.notRequested` denial raises no escalations, so M9 emits nothing there and a test built on it would pass against both trees. Same shape as the enumerate-before-you-subtract rule in `CLAUDE.md`, one level up: a claim that something never happens is not tested by watching it happen correctly.

Known limitations / deferred scope:
- **Requirement-level drift at equal tier is not covered here** — it is SONNY-97's, and it is unreachable before row C ships (requirement is a pure function of `effectiveTier` today, so "same tier" implies "same requirement" and there is nothing to compare). Scope was held to reason-set drift on the row-C amendment's instruction.
- **Standing grants are not reason-checked**, by decision recorded above and in the `Coverage.standingGrant` doc comment.
- The `risk.rearmed` event goes to `AgentLogStore`, which no view renders — it is a trace for tests and diagnosis, not a user-facing surface.

Open questions (required, write "none" if true): none.

**SHA note, because this branch was rebased after PR #44 and PR #45 merged.** It started from `main` at `0fdac1c` and was rebased onto `8629881` once row F (`feature/app-catalog-dissolution`, PR #44) and row C planning (`docs/sonny-13-row-c-planning`, PR #45) had both landed. **Every SHA this entry, SONNY-62's ticket comments and PR #46's own body cite from before that rebase is a non-ancestor of the current head** — verified with `git merge-base --is-ancestor`, which reports all nine as not ancestors. The map, original -> replay, in order: `990ea44` -> `9112c43`, `282a796` -> `5b4d0b9`, `d8b7659` -> `78b3f12`, `03e3814` -> `17f2e6b`, `400b8c7` -> `5b448a7`, `042f74e` -> `dbb79a9`, and PR #46's fix round `8705192` -> `76c13fd`, `ffa007f` -> `0e018c0`, `f00e34b` -> `04ce7e4`. The conflict was this file's shared append point and nothing else, resolved keep-both in merge order — row F's entry, then row C planning's, then this one. **The code carried rather than being restated on faith:** `git diff` between the old tip and the new, restricted to each of this branch's five exclusively-owned `Sources/`/`Tests/` files, is zero lines in all five, and `git diff origin/main...HEAD --name-only -- Sources/ Tests/` lists only this branch's own six files. The sixth, `RiskApprovalTests.swift`, is the one file both branches touched: its only delta is row F's `local.apps.open-allowlisted-app` -> `local.apps.open-app` rename in the capability-tier table, absorbed with no SONNY-62 test lost. **Two measurements did not survive and were re-taken rather than carried** — the suite counts at both ends (above), and F1's population sweep, because row F edited several of the very files it counts. Re-swept at `04ce7e4`: the counts are unchanged (25 literals at 7/8/10, six descriptor forwards, eight tier-3 escalation sites, no `defaultRiskTier` above tier 2), but one cited line number had moved (`AgentActionExecutor.swift:323` -> `:326`) and is corrected above. That is the rule working as intended: a count re-measured is a count, a count carried across a moved tree is a guess.

Next branch: row C's implementation branch (`feature/approval-relaxation-structural`, SONNY-97 onward), which lists this ticket as a merge prerequisite; row F (`feature/app-catalog-dissolution`) remains in flight in parallel and is disjoint from both.

Status: complete
Date: 2026-08-13
Tickets: **SONNY-85** (provider-agnostic planner router — the client-side stand-in for spec §16.5's backend proxy, per §9.4's day-one mandate), **SONNY-86** (the Cerebras-served open-weights planner, `gpt-oss-120b`, as a config-selectable live A/B behind it). Reviewed by: pending — fresh session per WORKFLOW.md step 7.

Files changed (across `dcaf2d0`, `b3ad165`, `005d683`, plus this entry's commit):
