- `Tests/MacAgentCoreTests/PlannerProviderRegistryTests.swift` (new, 12 tests), `Tests/MacAgentCoreTests/CerebrasPlannerTests.swift` (new, 13), `Tests/MacAgentTests/PlannerSelectionTests.swift` (new, 4)

**Correction, appended 2026-08-13 (PR #47 review, F1 — HIGH, records-only, coordinator-adjudicated the same day).** Retracted from the `CerebrasPlanner.swift` bullet above: "fresh implementation with the experiment branch's spike as reference only per E13" — together with the same claim's stronger forms in SONNY-86's closing comment ("nothing was ported") and in `005d683`'s own commit message, which is immutable pushed history and is corrected by this paragraph rather than rewritten. The reviewer measured **123 of the spike `CerebrasPlanner.swift`'s 144 code lines (trimmed, non-blank, non-comment-only) trimmed-identical in the shipped file — 85.4%, at `5ba91e6`** — and this fix round reproduced that number independently (same method, same result: 144-line population, 123 matches) before writing it into any record. The accurate characterization is **rework of the spike's code, which §E13 explicitly authorizes** ("using the spike's code as a starting reference, reworked … with test coverage"); the defect is that SONNY-86's description set a stricter bar — "read it, port nothing" — that was neither met nor disclosed as a deviation, and the records then asserted the stricter bar as fact. What genuinely is new in the shipped file, verified against the 21 non-identical spike lines and the shipped remainder, so this correction does not overcorrect: the doc comments (including the dated live vendor-fact rationale for the output-mode default); the injectable `useNativeStructuredOutput` init parameter where the spike read the env var inline per request; the registry descriptor extension (`providerID`/`provider` — the spike predates the router entirely); `normalizedPlanText`'s lone-fence edge handing the text to the decoder unmodified where the spike's `strippedJSONText` returned an empty string; `schemaJSONText` extracted as its own function with different prompt-suffix copy; the `missingOutputText` → `missingMessageContent` rename; and the 13 fixture-backed tests — the spike shipped none (swept all 49 test files on `experiment/cerebras-gpt-oss-vision` for "Cerebras": zero hits). **Coordinator ruling, recorded so a future reader knows this was adjudicated rather than missed: the code stands as E13-sanctioned rework; only the records are corrected; the disclosure, not the reuse, was the defect.** The correction is append-only in every venue — this paragraph beside the bullet it retracts, a correction comment on SONNY-86, and a dated note appended to PR #47's body.

Tests: **903 in 65 suites, exit 0** at `005d683`, via the flagged command in `CLAUDE.md`. Baseline **874 in 62 suites, exit 0, measured at `48dbff9`** on this worktree before any change — +29 tests, none weakened, zero existing assertions modified. **Golden proof is a diff, not a claim:** `git diff main..005d683 -- Tests/MacAgentCoreTests/PlannerBoundaryTests.swift Sources/MacAgentCore/AgentPlan.swift` is empty, and both prompt goldens pass unmodified. Five-mutation battery at `005d683` (committed tree), 5/5 killed: unknown-selection notice silenced → 7 registry tests fail; unavailable-provider fallback replaced with rethrow → `unavailableSelectedProviderFallsBackToTheDefaultWithTheReasonInTheNotice`; fence normalization disabled → `fencedContentIsNormalizedBeforeDecoding`; native opt-in stops sending `response_format` → `nativeStructuredOptInSendsResponseFormatAndTheUnmodifiedSharedPrompt`; usage recorded only after successful decode → `inventedOperationRejectsThroughDecodeStrictAndStillRecordsUsage` (both assertions).

What changed for the user: with no configuration, nothing — the default path constructs the byte-identical `OpenAIPlanner(usageRecorder:)` call that used to be inline, behind the same prompt and schema. With `SONNY_PLANNER=cerebras` (and `CEREBRAS_API_KEY` in env), planner calls go to the open-weights planner instead, through the same `prepare → assessRisk → execute` gates — a plan is a plan regardless of provider. A selection Sonny cannot honor (unknown id, or a selected provider whose construction fails, e.g. key missing) falls back to OpenAI **with a dismissible notice strip in the widget saying who actually planned and why** — never a silent planner swap.

Behavior preserved: the risk/approval engine is untouched (`RiskApproval.swift`, `AgentRunner.swift`, `AgentActionExecutor.swift` absent from the diff — row C's surfaces, shared file edits confined to the planner-selection region of `AgentViewModel.swift`). Voice stays direct (`OpenAITranscriber` untouched). Prompt content, plan schema, `AgentPlanDecoder`, and all local stores untouched. The default provider's construction failure propagates through the same catch with the same `OPENAI_API_KEY` wording as before.

Architectural decisions / pitfalls discovered (required, write "none" if true):

**The registry owns selection and construction; the construction site owns neither.** `performStart` asks `PlannerProviderRegistry.default.makePlanner(selection:usageRecorder:)` and consumes a `SelectedPlanner` — adding a provider is a descriptor plus one `register` line in MacAgentCore, pinned by a test that makes a stub provider constructible through the same call with no edit outside the registry. The §A1 obligations (opt-in usage recording via the recorder handed to construct, cancellation as `CancellationError`/`URLError(.cancelled)`, per-provider `LocalizedError`) are documented on `PlannerProvider` itself because `Planning` is the entire seam — there is no translation layer above it to add them afterwards; the recorder pass-through is pinned by identity.

**"Unavailable" and "unknown" both fall back visibly; only the default's own failure throws.** A non-default provider that fails to construct falls back to the default with the reason in the notice — an A/B whose alternate is misconfigured must degrade loudly, not fail the task and not silently swap. The default's own failure propagates unchanged, which is what keeps the no-configuration path byte-identical including its failure mode.

**A "visible notice" needed an enumeration first.** `AgentLogStore` renders in zero UI files (swept all 16 `Sources/MacAgent/*.swift`), so the tempting log-only notice would have been a silent swap in practice. The notice got its own `@Published` channel — the subject-split rule `scheduledRunNotice`'s doc comment establishes (not a failed task, not Sonny's data) — cleared at the next dispatch, and rendered by the widget as its own strip. Command Center deliberately does not render it: the widget is the sole command surface and permanent overlay, and CC's notice region sits adjacent to approval-flow code the parallel row C session owns.

**Schema-in-prompt is the Cerebras default because of a measured, dated vendor fact — and the pin ties the decision to its justification.** Re-verified live 2026-08-13 (inference-docs.cerebras.ai/capabilities/structured-outputs): native structured-output schemas cap at 5,000 characters, limits strictly enforced since 2026-07-21, `null`/`anyOf` listed as supported types (nullable-unions-in-strict-mode still not explicitly clarified). The shared plan schema serializes to **9,514 characters at `005d683`** (sorted-keys JSON), so native mode is a live validation error today. A test asserts the schema still exceeds the cap: if it ever shrinks below 5,000, the pin fails and the mode default gets re-decided on purpose instead of inherited. The `SONNY_CEREBRAS_STRUCTURED=1` opt-in exists to measure that rejection against the live API.

**Fence normalization is input normalization, not reliability UX.** Schema-in-prompt has no server-side output guarantee, and a model told "no markdown fences" can still fence a valid plan. A wrapping fence is stripped before `decodeStrict`; everything else malformed still rejects, propagates as a normal planner error with usage already recorded (mutation-pinned ordering — the tokens were spent either way), and no retry machinery exists anywhere (C10).

**The shared usage parser already spoke Chat Completions.** `AIUsagePayloadParser.tokenCounts` reads `input_tokens`/`prompt_tokens` and `output_tokens`/`completion_tokens` as alias lists, so the Cerebras planner records through the identical parser and record builder — no second parser, no new record shape.

**The kickoff prompt claimed a `SONNY_PLANNER=cerebras` branch already existed in `performStart`; the tree said otherwise.** A sweep found `SONNY_PLANNER` nowhere in `Sources/` — it exists only on the experiment branch (plan doc §1). Resolved per WORKFLOW.md step 4: the description's "constructed at exactly one site" is what was implemented against, and the variable *name* was still adopted for founder continuity. The prompt is context; the description is the contract.

**Mutation batteries: committed trees, surgical mutations.** This branch's battery ran against the committed tree by rule — and the rule paid for itself when the first mutation attempt (a `perl -0pi` one-liner) corrupted UTF-8 punctuation across the file's doc comments and left a syntax error; `git checkout` restored the exact committed bytes and the battery was redone with single-hunk Edit mutations. A mutation tool that can damage more than it mutates is itself a reason the tree must be committed first.

Known limitations / deferred scope:
- The fallback notice renders in the floating widget only (plus a run-log line); Command Center shows no strip, by the reasoning above. It is also not persisted into task history — `CompletedTaskRecord` has no notes field, and a store-schema change was out of both tickets' scope.
- `SONNY_PLANNER` is read once at app start (a process's environment cannot change under it — `OPENAI_API_KEY` has the same property). Changing the selection means relaunching, which is how the founder already launches with keys.
- Native structured-output mode is *expected* to be rejected by the live API today (HTTP validation error surfacing as `badResponse`) — that is the measurement the flag exists for, not a defect.
- `CEREBRAS_MODEL` overrides the model id, mirroring `OPENAI_MODEL`; no other Cerebras configuration surface exists.

Open questions (required, write "none" if true): none.

Manual checklist for the founder (SONNY-86's ratified item, plus two this branch owes for surfaces no agent can drive):
1. Launch the packaged app with `CEREBRAS_API_KEY` set and `SONNY_PLANNER=cerebras`. Run three representative commands and confirm the plans execute through the normal gates (previews, confirmations, approvals exactly as usual). Run the same three under OpenAI (unset `SONNY_PLANNER`) for an informal side-by-side feel — no benchmark, no gate (C5/C6).
2. Launch with `SONNY_PLANNER=nonsense` and run any free-text command: the widget should show the planner notice strip ("Sonny doesn't have a planner called “nonsense”…") while the task still completes under OpenAI.
3. Launch with `SONNY_PLANNER=cerebras` but **no** `CEREBRAS_API_KEY`: the strip should name Cerebras as unavailable with the missing-key reason, and the task should still plan under OpenAI.

Behavior changed:
- **In-scope tier 2 auto-runs.** A task whose plan-level scope roll-up is `.inScope` executes its tier-2 work with no prompt; tier 3 in scope maps to `.lightweightConfirmation`. Both resolve in one exhaustive `(effectiveTier, grant)` switch in `RiskApprovalPolicy.requirement(for:context:)` — now the **only** public path to a `RiskApprovalRequirement` (there were three; the two wrappers are deleted and the tier-only baseline is `private`, compiler-enforced).
- **The workspace sheet's edits auto-run under the origin grant** (`PreparedPlanSource.directUserAction` + per-operation eligibility): an add to a configured dimension applies with no prompt; a non-emptying removal pauses at a lightweight ask with the identical tier-3 sentence. **Boundary-changing edits do not**: first entry into an empty dimension, emptying removals (by the evaluator's canonical answer), and subsuming folder adds drop the origin grant alone — `byWorkspaceScope` stays set, which is exactly why eligibility is evaluated per grant (PR #45, F2). Adding a whitelist root itself escalates to tier 3 with a consequence-naming reason.
- **An approval decision now records the requirement the user answered** (`RiskApprovalConsent.answeredRequirement`), and the gate re-arms when the freshly derived requirement is stricter — including at equal tier, the case tier and reason comparison cannot see. A drifted relaxed auto-run re-arms to a pending approval instead of hard-failing (`performStart`'s catch dropped its trust-only `where` clause).
- **A granted silent run says so**: one muted line on the widget's result panel ("Ran without asking — …"), naming the workspace or the on-screen origin, owned by a pure tested function. Tier-0/1 runs never trace — they auto-ran in every grant column — and a prompted run never traces.

Behavior preserved (required, no blanket claims):
- **The assessment is untouched by relaxation** (I1/I2): `effectiveTier`, `escalations` and `approvalCopy` are byte-identical with and without each grant, pinned through the real runner in row B's whole-assessment shape. `assessRisk` takes no context and stays origin-blind.
- **The unattended path reaches neither grant** (I6) — still by the two independent call-site choices (`source: .instantResolver`, `scope: .unscoped`), now with a test named for the hazard replicating the scheduled dispatch's exact shape around a covering workspace. Scheduled tier-3 behavior (pause-and-notify) and the routine-trust door (SONNY-54) are unchanged: standing grants remain pure tier ceilings on the new requirement axis exactly as on the reason axis, pinned by every pre-existing scheduled/trust test passing unmodified.
- **Escalate-never-block** (I4): the only `.refuse` cells are tier 4's, in every column — pinned by the written-table cross-product.
- **`edit_workspace`'s removal escalations, both reason variants, and removal-unit grouping are byte-identical** — the widening escalations append after them, and every pre-existing `EditWorkspaceTests` assertion passes unmodified.
- **Safe mode's seam**: `safeModeRequirement = stricter(of: baseline, safeModeFloor)` on the stated permissiveness rank; P1 (Safe mode never more permissive) and P2 (a grant never less permissive than `.none`) hold over the full 600-cell cross-product on every policy dial position. Row H's SONNY-90 replaces one named `safeMode: false` literal.

Architectural decisions / pitfalls discovered (required, write "none" if true):

**The tier-3 half of the charter has no user-visible effect from this branch, and saying so is a requirement, not modesty.** `.lightweightConfirmation` and `.explicitApproval` render identically on every surface until `feature/approval-relaxation-surface` (SONNY-101) ships. The friction this branch delivers is in-scope tier-2 auto-run and the sheet-edit origin grant — and SONNY-99's trace is what keeps that delivery from being silent.

**The trace keys on the grant having changed the outcome, not on the grant having fired.** An in-scope tier-1 run *reports* a grant on its request while auto-running for its own reasons — tiers 0/1 auto-run in every grant column — so a trace keyed on "grant != none" would fire on steps that were always silent and train the user to ignore the one that matters. The pure function gates on tier ≥ 2, which is exact under every policy (no tier-2-or-above cell is `.autoRun` without a grant). The pin at the real surface is a tier-1 in-scope run leaving ordinary silence.

**The reported grant is a field on the request, populated at the one deriving site.** SONNY-99 cannot touch the engine, so SONNY-97 shipped the carrier: `RiskApprovalRequest.relaxationGrant`, filled where the requirement is derived, `.none` under Safe mode (I5 has a reporting half: a Safe-mode run relaxed nothing and must not claim it did — mutation-pinned). It is reporting-only; nothing reads it to gate anything (I8's second clause, restated on the field).

**A requirement-drift re-arm needs the answered requirement, and a sibling Optional field was the right carrier.** `Coverage.acknowledgedReasons` could have grown a payload, but a sibling `answeredRequirement: RiskApprovalRequirement?` keeps SONNY-62's fresh API intact, keeps standing grants axis-free by construction (`nil`), and inherits the SONNY-62 rule that `init(answering:)` is the only site allowed to record the non-nil value — tier, reasons and requirement all read from one request, so no call site can record a consent nobody gave.

**The per-grant eligibility formula earned its F2 correction in code.** Mutation M1 rebuilt the exact single-slot form PR #45's reviewer hand-traced, and it was killed by three tests including the chain-dispatch case that exists because of that trace — the written 60-row grant table is what made the kill a table lookup rather than a judgment call.

**Two windows no test can reach, said out loud.** The widened auto-run drift catch and the RunRoutine eligibility forward are both real code with no deterministic test reachability (intra-call drift; a statically-zeroed intersection). The repo's alternatives — delete them, or pretend they are covered — are both worse: each carries a comment stating exactly why it exists and when it becomes observable, the engine halves are runner-level pinned, and the closing comments carry the reachability arguments.

**The `.fileLocation`-only widening asymmetry is a stated decision, recorded so it is not read as a bug**: file locations are the only kind matched by containment, so one entry can convert a territory; `.app` has no hierarchy (`appKey` resolves to a bundle identifier — there is no "root app"); `.webDomain` has suffix matching but is bounded by `SafeURL` validation and the specific host the user typed, with no equivalent of the whitelist root.

**SONNY-99's wireframe exception, discharged at its bounded smallest**: one line of muted caption text on `WidgetResultPanel`, tokens already on that surface, no new component, no new token, System B otherwise untouched. Command Center renders no result surface at all today, so the line is widget-only — building a CC result surface would have exceeded the exception. The completed-task record deliberately gained no persisted field (requirement 4's under-build option, chosen and reasoned on the ticket).

**SONNY-100's contract conflict was discharged by coordinator amendment, not judgment**: comment-only edits in exactly two `Sources/` files (`AgentActionExecutor.swift`'s "four things" doc comment, `CreateWorkspaceCapabilityAdapter.swift`'s "six sites" line — the recorded landing spot from PR #45) were pre-authorized on the ticket; `docs/sonny-branch-b-plan.md` stays frozen except the one forward-flag closure pointer, its stale counts discharged by the current-truth docs. Zero executable lines changed in either file, and the full suite was re-run rather than claiming the docs-only exemption.

Known limitations / deferred scope:
- **Branch 2 (SONNY-101 + SONNY-50) is the entire user-visible half of the tier-3 clause**, plus the approval-panel chip work. It carries the founder-wireframe dependency and starts after this branch merges.
- **SONNY-90 (row H)** replaces the one `safeMode: false` literal with the real Settings value and owns revisiting `safeModeFloor` (currently `.explicitApproval`) — the floor, never the formula.
- **Row I** adds per-app consent as an `ApprovalContext` field and its own `PreparedPlanSource` case; the compiler forces classification of its operation in the eligibility switch, and classifying it as granting neither is the recorded answer (SONNY-92).
- **The ran-without-asking trace does not survive the run into task history** — requirement 4's decided under-build; rows D/E's task-detail work is the natural owner if provenance should persist.

Open questions (required, write "none" if true): none raised here. Row B's scheduled-pre-check question stays open with its cost amended in place (above, in row B's entry); the widening flag is closed.

**SUPERSESSION BLOCK, appended 2026-08-13 — the consequence-rule pivot. Everything above in this entry is the record of a design that shipped, passed review, and was then replaced on this same branch before merge; this block is the current truth.**

Two things happened after the clean cycle-1 review at `e1a7aa9` and the rebase to `972c62a`. First, the founder's manual pass found the relaxation grant **never fired in the live packaged app** — an in-workspace tier-2 draft still prompted. The 950 green tests pinned the mapping function; nothing pinned the product's dispatch path into it, and the grant path was never debugged because of what came second: **the founder issued a superseding product directive.** Sonny asks permission only when an action is **destructive** (destroys or replaces existing user data or user-built artifacts: file overwrites, replace-on-save of routines/workspaces/snippets, deletions) or **affects someone other than the user** (send/post/share/publish/purchase — no such capability exists in v1, so the class is armed but empty until vision actions arrive). Everything else runs without asking, made legible by the trace this branch already built. Where the old preconditions — row B's scope gating, the frozen row-C plan's charter, the in-scope/origin grant conditions — conflict with the rule, the rule wins. Durable decision record: `docs/sonny-founder-design-decisions.md` → "The consequence rule"; spec §11.2/§11.3 re-amended; a supersession notice sits atop `docs/sonny-row-c-plan.md`.

**What shipped (commit `4caf005`, `feat(core): SONNY-97 SONNY-98 SONNY-99 the consequence rule`):**

- **`CapabilityRiskEscalation` gains a `Consequence` class** — `destructive` / `affectsOthers` / `advisory` — non-defaulted in the initializer, so every construction site must classify or the build fails, and `asksFirst` switches over it exhaustively with no `default:`. All nine sites classified (the plan said eight; SONNY-98's widening escalation made it nine — enumerated, not carried): SaveRoutine, SnippetSave, CreateWorkspace replace-on-save and LargestFilesZip, WebResearchMarkdown, CreateLocalDraft output collisions are **destructive**; the executor's out-of-scope escalation, EditWorkspace's removal escalation (both wordings, the dimension-emptying one included — consciously superseding the week-old Q4 ratification, a recorded coordinator call, founder-vetoable), and EditWorkspace's whitelist-root widening are **advisory**. Advisory escalations still raise `effectiveTier` — the tier stays an honest severity signal — and their sentences land on the ran-without-asking trace instead of a prompt, from any origin: the classification is origin-blind.
- **The mapping became the rule.** `RiskApprovalPolicy.requirement(for:context:)` (still the sole public path, I8 intact): Safe mode first, unchanged formula; any asks-first escalation → `.explicitApproval` at every tier that can run (the defense-in-depth term on tiers 0–2 is unreachable through any adapter today and pinned by a hand-built test, so the day an escalation targets tier 2 it asks without anyone remembering to make it); otherwise tiers 0–2 → `.autoRun` unconditionally; advisory-only tier 3 → `.autoRun`; escalation-free tier 3 → fail-closed `.explicitApproval`; tier 4 → `.refuse`. The mapping no longer produces `.lightweightConfirmation` at all.
- **The grant machinery is deleted, not dormant**: `OperationRelaxation` (the per-operation eligibility OptionSet and its exhaustive classification), `RelaxationGrant` (both grants and the grant/applied computation), `CapabilityRiskAssessment.relaxationEligibility` and the executor's intersection fold, `RunRoutineCapabilityAdapter`'s nested eligibility forwarding, `EditWorkspaceCapabilityAdapter`'s boundary-changing narrowing with its `changesWhetherTheDimensionIsABoundary` and subsumption predicates, `RiskApprovalRequest.relaxationGrant`, and `ApprovalContext.origin` (the rule reads no origin; `PreparedPlanSource` itself survives for dispatch, logging, and the pending-arm rule). The `scopeVerdict` roll-up survives as **data** — task binding, chips, the future vision cage — and gates nothing.
- **The trace reshaped (SONNY-99's chassis, kept):** `AgentViewModel.ranWithoutAskingTrace` (née `relaxationTrace`), from the pure `AgentActivityPresentation.ranWithoutAskingLine(requirement:effectiveTier:advisoryReasons:)`. Only `.autoRun` at tier ≥ 2 traces; advisory reasons are named verbatim ("Ran without asking — worth knowing: …"); with nothing advisory the line states the rule ("Ran without asking — nothing here is destructive, and it affects no one else."). Tier 0/1 and answered prompts never trace, as before. In-run only, as before — no persisted field.
- **SONNY-62's re-arm applies to whatever still asks**, unchanged: consents still carry tier + acknowledged reasons + answered requirement, and an auto-run that turns destructive mid-flight (the output file appearing between assessment and execution) re-arms to a pending approval with no false `risk.rearmed` event — pinned at runner and product level with the mid-flight-write drift lever, since the old context-flip lever died with the grants.
- **Two product seams added for the coverage class the manual failure demanded:** `AgentViewModel.safeModeEnabled` (the named site SONNY-90 backs with Settings — tests drive Safe mode through the real path with it) and an injectable `PathWhitelist` on `AgentViewModel` (read by `makeExecutor()` and `resolveTaskScope`, so the assessment and the scope agree on what a path is, hermetically). `ConsequenceRuleDispatchTests` drives the REAL dispatch path — typed command through a registry-injected fake planner into `performStart` — and pins: an in-workspace tier-2 draft auto-runs, a plain unscoped tier-2 draft auto-runs, an overwrite still prompts and approving performs it, and Safe mode makes all three ask.

**Consequences named rather than discovered later:** SONNY-54's manual-trust half is subsumed — nothing tier-2 asks, trusted or not, so `manualRoutineTrustDecision` can no longer authorize anything (the machinery stays; the toggle's remaining door is the unattended one, whose tier-2 ceiling still pauses destructive routine steps — `ScheduledRoutineRunTests` rewritten accordingly, superseding the manual half of PR #34's F4 ruling). The policy dials are inert on the ordinary path — `requireApprovalForTier1` now has **no observable effect anywhere**, `tier2Mode == .previewOnly` shows only inside Safe mode — kept pending a founder decision on deleting them (flagged, not decided). I10/the imposter gap is moot: scope grants nothing, so a name-fallback `.inScope` buys nothing. Row I's vision operations arrive into `Consequence`, a classification that already asks, rather than into a relaxation allowlist.

**Tests (all at `4caf005`; suite 944 tests in 68 suites, all passing; the flagged command):** `@Test` accounting from 950 at `972c62a`: −30 `ApprovalRelaxationTests.swift` and −5 `RelaxationTraceTests.swift`, deleted whole with the machinery they pinned; −2 in `EditWorkspaceTests.swift` (six SONNY-98 narrowing tests became four consequence-rule tests, keeping the two-notions-of-empty fixture and every reason string); +22 `ConsequenceRuleTests.swift` (written tables over tier × class-combo × Safe mode, never sampled; the kept properties — Safe mode never more permissive, advisory never asks, destructive always asks; every construction site's class by name; the unattended ceiling; the drift re-arms; the removed-wrapper sweep); +5 `RanWithoutAskingTraceTests.swift`; +4 `ConsequenceRuleDispatchTests.swift`. Behavioral migrations were per-test decisions, not mechanical flips: lifecycle tests whose *purpose* was a paused state moved to destructive fixtures (draft collisions, snippet replaces); tests whose purpose was the superseded prompting were rewritten to pin the new truth by name. **Mutation battery on the classification, against the committed tree `4caf005`, 16/16 killed, each by named tests** — every destructive site flipped to advisory dies (e.g. `everyReplaceOnSaveEscalationIsDestructiveAndStillAsks`, `everyOutputCollisionEscalationIsDestructiveAndStillAsks`), every advisory site flipped to destructive dies (e.g. `workspaceEntryRemovalEscalationsAreAdvisoryAndAutoRunInBothWordings`, `anOutOfScopePlanAutoRunsWithTheAdvisoryFactRecordedAndSafeModeStillAsks`), and the mapping/trace mutations (asksFirst flips both ways, the fail-closed empty-tier-3 term, the tier-0–2 defense term, the skipped Safe-mode return, both trace gates) each die to the test written for them.

**Known residuals:** `WorkspaceScope.swift` and `PlanScopedResources.swift` carry three stale comment mentions of "relaxation eligibility" — vacuously true now, left because both files are never-touch (row F's file; row B's evaluator) and a comment breach was not worth it; flagged for their owners. SONNY-101's lightweight-vs-explicit contract likely collapses under the rule — flagged on the ticket for the founder, not decided here. PR #48's clean cycle-1 PASS is void for the pivot commits; a fresh review follows on the final state.

Architectural decisions / pitfalls discovered (required, write "none" if true):

**Window selection is two inputs, not one, and the discipline is the service's whole point.** ScreenCaptureKit documents no order for `SCShareableContent.windows`, so candidates come from SC (bundle-id, layer-0, on-screen, 80pt floor) while the *choice* follows `CGWindowListCopyWindowInfo`'s front-to-back order — a dialog over the app's main window is what gets captured, pinned by the test the spike's live bug demanded. `SCWindow` is not Sendable, so the backend hands out value-type snapshots and re-resolves the live window by id at capture time — a window that closed in between fails cleanly, which is the target-gone path anyway.

**The TCC asymmetry is the onboarding's state machine.** An Accessibility grant lands in-process; `CGPreflightScreenCaptureAccess` cannot flip for Screen Recording until a fresh launch — so the model shows relaunch guidance on request rather than polling for a flip that can never come, and the packaged-app relaunch seam is `open -n` + terminate. Neither permission consults an Info.plist usage key (the spike's packaged-app finding, upheld: this branch's bundle builds and signs clean without one; the founder's manual pass is the live confirmation).

**Redaction confidences are structural, not tuned, and the fill is the fail-closed blur.** A match whose *validity* the detector can verify (vendor prefix, passing Luhn, in-range SSN, key-block marker) sits above the threshold; a shape-only match (entropy blob, Luhn-failing card, bullet run, bare spaced pair) sits below it and is redacted-and-flagged anyway — so "threshold in both directions" is pinned on one class with one digit's difference. Regions are painted opaque black rather than Gaussian-blurred: a blur is a convolution with partially invertible residue on text-sized regions, a fill is provably zero-information. Vision runs with `usesLanguageCorrection = false` — correction would "fix" token shapes into dictionary words before the detector sees them. Toolchain fact: this Swift Regex engine rejects lookbehind; two patterns use a lookahead plus a manual preceding-character check.

**`RedactedPayload`'s non-bypass is fileprivate, and Codable would have broken it.** Construction is possible only in the service's own file — module-internal code included, so row I's egress function cannot fabricate one — and the type is deliberately not Codable because a `Decodable` conformance is a public initializer in disguise (`JSONDecoder` would mint payloads from arbitrary bytes). Pinned by a source-scan test plus a module-wide construction-site enumeration.

**An egress with no ledger entry is un-constructible, not merely asserted.** `makePlanner` takes the recorder non-defaulted and wraps every planner on all three construction paths; the entry is written *before* delegation (a prompt that leaves and then fails still left); the fallback path records the provider that actually received the prompt; and a source-enumeration test pins that the two network planners are constructed nowhere outside their own provider descriptors — the registry is structurally the only door. The ticket's "assert in debug" is discharged by this construction-forcing shape; a runtime IO assert would crash dev builds on a full disk and prove less.

**A mutation-battery survivor is a finding, not a footnote — the ledger join is an id because of one.** The first join key was `startedAt` shared between the ledger record and the task record; battery mutation E10 (recorder minted a fresh `Date()` instead of the threaded instant) *survived*, because both stores persist ISO8601 and whole-second truncation makes date equality unable to see sub-second drift — the same truncation that would silently unlink a real run's records whenever a re-minted instant straddled a second boundary. Root fix, not a stronger test: `CompletedTaskRecord.egressRunID` (optional, decodeIfPresent like `trigger`) joined to `AIEgressStore.record(forRunID:)`, threaded through a per-task property with the same lifecycle as `pendingTaskHistoryStartedAt`, after which the re-run mutation dies deterministically.

**The egress classification is now a live forcing function, bidirectionally.** SONNY-10's one-directional rule became "in the set (or the switch) *if and only if* it can leave the device", restated above `dataEgressOperations`, and `EgressClassificationTests` holds all 31 `AgentOperation` cases against it in an exhaustive no-`default` switch — a new operation fails compilation until classified. The solitary-`writeMarkdown` false-"no" (SONNY-32) was fixed in the direction its own analysis recommended: `isHackerNewsPreset` no longer counts a file write as membership, and the solitary shape is rejected as an incomplete plan naming the real problem, spies proving no network collaborator is touched.

**Safe mode's trust boundary splits attended from unattended, and the split is the contract's own.** The standing tier-2 grant answers Safe mode's requirement like any requirement at or below its ceiling, so scheduled runs are unaffected (no new unattended prompt class; a schedule Safe mode silently suspended would be one) — while the SONNY-54 *manual-run* shortcut is gated on `!safeModeEnabled`, because a per-routine convenience grant must not quietly override the global caution dial while the user is present to answer. Both directions pinned. The label relocation runs through two pure presentation selectors on the `firstRunApprovalExplainerLines` precedent, so both directions are pinned without a view-inspection harness.

**A sweep's own tooling can be the narrow grep.** The construction-site enumeration test initially failed against `AgentViewModel.swift` — a doc comment mentioning `OpenAIPlanner(usageRecorder:)` — while the session's earlier hand grep had used `grep -v "//"`, which drops any *line containing* `//` and would have missed a real constructor carrying a trailing comment. The test now filters comment-prefixed lines and the claim is enumerated, not sampled; the incident is this repo's claims-and-evidence rule caught in the act by its own machinery.

Consequence, stated plainly: the founder cannot toggle Safe mode or read a ledger record in the live app until those land; both tickets carry exact build-notes for the surface session (the id join read; the honestly-empty state; the toggle binds to a property whose `didSet` already persists). The load-failure banner's live wiring rides with the ledger surface — no production code loads the store for display yet, so there is nothing to banner; the store-level corrupt-load throw is pinned now.

- **The parked policy dials** (`requireApprovalForTier1`, `tier2Mode`): proposal on SONNY-90 is delete both (one provably dead everywhere, the other constructible by no site), awaiting the founder's call — not decided here.

- Pre-send screenshot preview is row-I content by the coordinator's kickoff note; its structural halves (the ask, the capture currency, the redacted-only payload) shipped here.
- **(Appended 2026-08-14, PR #49 F10)** `PlanScopedResources.swift`'s `.writeMarkdown` justification cites a premise SONNY-88 deleted ("`isHackerNewsPreset` fires on the mere presence of a `write_markdown` step"); the conclusion still holds — the step remains reachable only inside a Hacker News unit and still reports `webDomain("news.ycombinator.com")` — but the recorded reason is stale. Flagged for the file's owner (row B's evaluator; never-touch for this branch), the same class as row C's two flagged mentions.

Open questions (required, write "none" if true): the two founder wireframes, the dial proposal, and the egress-performer triage above — all recorded on SONNY-88/SONNY-90; nothing else.

Manual checklist for the founder (packaged app only — TCC ignores bare `swift run`):
1. Settings › Security & Access › "Set up screen access": walk the Screen Recording grant end-to-end — Request access → toggle Sonny on in System Settings → the modal's relaunch guidance → Relaunch Sonny → reopen: Granted, and the readiness row reads Ready.
2. Accessibility: Request access → grant → "Check again" shows Granted *without* a relaunch.
3. Revoke either grant in System Settings → rows accurate on next look; deny/ignore → instructive needs-action copy, no crash, no silence, and no unexpected TCC prompt during ordinary non-vision commands.
4. Run any normal planner command, then delete local data from Settings › Data → the deletion summary names Data-Sent-to-AI records, and (once the surface lands) the ledger is empty.
5. SONNY-88/90's remaining items (task detail shows the prompt egress with provider/model; Safe-mode toggle → benign command asks with the label visible; Normal → no label anywhere) ride with the gated surfaces.

Next branch: the founder's wireframes unblock SONNY-88/90's surfaces on this branch (or a successor session on it); after this branch merges, row I (`feature/vision-actions`, SONNY-91–96) consumes the capture service, the redaction pipeline, the ledger's vision entry kinds, and the Safe-mode pre-send seams — with `feature/approval-relaxation-surface` (SONNY-101, SONNY-50) still pending its own founder design pass.
